“Nay, my lord, to my shame. But I know this man, and I would call him good. Also he is beloved of the men-at-arms.”

“That will suit my purpose well,” Simon nodded, but he did not disclose what was his purpose. “I think to make Harold the Smooth-Tongued steward in Hubert’s room.”

“Then ye will do wisely, sir, for he is an honest man, and sober. What comes to Hubert?”

“Naught,” Simon answered. “He goes.”

“Thus ye will be rid of a very pretty mischief-brewer, sir. He is full of indignation at your coming, and although he durst not go openly against you, he might do much harm by his talk.”

“Ay.” Simon rose. He pointed to the sheets of parchment that lay scattered over the table. “Have the goodness to make me fair copies of these, Master Talmayne. I go now to send for Maurice of Gountray.”

===Chinese===
“没有，老爷，惭愧。不过这个人我认得，我可以称他一声好人。甲士们也都拥戴他。”

“那正合我的用意。”西蒙点点头，却不肯说破用意是什么。“我打算让「巧舌」哈罗德（Harold the Smooth-Tongued）接替休伯特做总管。”

“那您就办得明智，先生，此人为人诚实，又有节制。那休伯特怎么办？”

“不怎么办，”西蒙答道。“他走人。”

“这样一来，您就除掉了一个顶会酿事生非的角色，先生。他满肚子都是对您到来的怨愤，虽然不敢明着跟您作对，那张嘴却足以坏事。”

“嗯。”西蒙站起身，指了指摊满桌面的几张羊皮纸。“劳驾替我把这些誊清出来，塔尔曼老爷。我这就去传冈特雷的莫里斯。”

===Original===
Bernard stood up.

“My lord, if he comes not be not too enraged, for he—”

Simon glanced over his shoulder, smiling rather grimly.

“Dost thou think I shall bungle my affairs, Master Talmayne?”

Bernard looked him in the eyes.

“Nay, my lord. Your pardon.”

===Chinese===
伯纳德起身。

“老爷，他若是不来，您可别太动气，因为他——”

西蒙回头一瞥，笑得颇有几分冷峻。

“你觉得我会把自己的事办砸么，塔尔曼老爷？”

伯纳德直视着他的眼睛。

“不会，老爷。恕罪。”

===Original===
Simon gave his short laugh and went out.

He sent his squire to summon Maurice, but Roger returned alone.

“My lord, he will not come!” he said, wide-eyed. “He—he bade me tell you he—he comes not at any—any—any—”

“Well?”

“C-coxcomb’s call, my lord!”

“So?” Simon smiled unpleasantly. “Then I will e’en go to him.”

===Chinese===
西蒙短促地一笑，走了出去。

他派扈从去传莫里斯，罗杰却独自回来了。

“老爷，他不肯来！”他瞪圆了眼睛说。“他——他叫我回禀您，他才不应任——任何——任何——”

“嗯？”

“纨——纨绔子的传唤，老爷！”

“是么？”西蒙笑得叫人发毛。“那我索性自己去见他。”

===Original===
Roger put himself in front of him.

“Sir, take me with you!”

Simon looked down at him.

“Wherefore?”

“I—indeed, I mislike his looks, sir!”

Simon laughed, and taking his squire by the shoulders put him aside.

===Chinese===
罗杰拦到他身前。

“先生，带我一起去！”

西蒙低头看他。

“为什么？”

“我——说实话，我瞧他神色不对，先生！”

西蒙笑了，抓着扈从的肩膀把他拨到一旁。

===Original===
“I need not thy protection, lad. Go thou to Malcolm, and bid him be ready to accompany me forth in an hour.”

“Oh!” Roger ran after him. “Sir, let me ride with you! I am not weary, and Malcolm—”

“Thou didst hear me, Roger?” Simon said softly.

Roger sighed and fell back.

“Ay, my lord.”

Simon strode out into the sunlight. He crossed the courtyard to the men-at-arms’ quarters, and went in quietly. He walked through the hall, past staring, whispering soldiers, and made his way to the room which he knew to be Gountray’s.

===Chinese===
“我用不着你保护，小子。去告诉马尔科姆，叫他准备一下，一个钟头后随我出门。”

“哦！”罗杰追着他跑。“先生，让我跟您骑马去吧！我不累，马尔科姆——”

“我的话你没听见么，罗杰？”西蒙轻声说。

罗杰叹了口气，停住脚步。

“是，老爷。”

西蒙大步走进阳光里。他穿过庭院，朝甲士的营房走去，悄没声地走了进去。他穿过大厅，从一个个瞪眼窃语的兵士身边走过，径直朝他知道是冈特雷住所的那间屋子走去。

===Original===
He entered with his noiseless step, and found Maurice up with an oath and stood as if at bay.

Simon walked forward unhurriedly. He favoured Maurice with a long look before he spoke.

“This time I have come to you,” he said abruptly. “Another time I shall not do that.”

“I care not for your threats!” Gountray cried.

“I never threaten,” Simon answered composedly. He went to the table and lifted two wine bottles from it. These he flung out of the window with unerring aim.

===Chinese===
他悄无声息地跨进门，只见莫里斯咒骂一声，霍地立起，像陷入绝境的困兽般钉在原地。

西蒙从容不迫地走上前，把莫里斯打量了好一阵，才开口。

“这一回，是我来找你，”他劈头说。“下一回，我不会再这样。”

“我不吃你这一套威吓！”冈特雷喊道。

“我从不动辄威吓，”西蒙心平气和地答道。他走到桌边，拎起桌上的两只酒瓶，随手一掷，分毫不差地抛出了窗外。

===Original===
“Now, by God—” Gountray roared, and sprang forward.

Simon’s cold voice checked him.

“Do ye think it no shame, Maurice of Gountray, for a strong man to become a drunken sot?” he said.

Maurice flushed to the ears.

“I’ll not be answerable to you for my actions!” he snapped.

===Chinese===
“好哇，老天在上——”冈特雷咆哮着扑上前去。

西蒙冰冷的声音止住了他。

“冈特雷的莫里斯，一个好端端的壮汉沦为醉醺醺的酒囊，你不觉得羞耻么？”他说。

莫里斯红到了耳根。

“我的行为犯不着向你交代！”他厉声说。

===Original===
“Ay, that will you,” Simon said, “or leave this my land. I care not which ye choose, but an end will I have to your carousing and your rebellious insolence.”

“Rebellious insolence, forsooth!” Maurice cried. “Ye have yet to prove yourself strong enough to be my master! Think ye I will bend the knee to a pert boy not out of his teens?”

“Ay,” Simon answered.

“Then know that it is not so! I will fight ye for as long as ye remain here, and my men will refuse to do your bidding! One and all will stand by me! Ye have chosen to slight me, but I will show you of what stuff Maurice of Gountray is made!”

“Ye have shown me,” Simon said deliberately. “Within a week of my coming hither I knew you for a drunken knave who proves himself trustless in the absence of a master. I see you now, a common, brawling malcontent whose muscles are weak for want of training, whose temper is soured by the lawless, pleasure-seeking life ye have led during these past months. I have little use for such, Maurice of Gountray. I want true men about me, not worthless braggarts who bluster and shout, yet who have not honour enough or strength to keep their men in order when the master is away.”

===Chinese===
“不，偏要向我交代，”西蒙说，“不然就滚出我这片土地。两条路由你挑，我无所谓，但你的狂饮和你的犯上无礼，必须到此为止。”

“犯上无礼，亏你说得出口！”莫里斯嚷道。“你还得先证明你有本事当我的主子！你以为我会向一个不满二十的傲慢小子屈膝？”

“不错，”西蒙答道。

“那你听清楚了，偏不是这样！只要你还待在这儿，我就同你斗到底，我的人也绝不听你的号令！他们一个不落都会站在我这边！你既存心轻慢我，我就叫你看看，冈特雷的莫里斯是何等材料做的！”

“你已经叫我看过了，”西蒙一字一顿地说。“我来了不出一个礼拜，便看穿你是个酒鬼无赖，主子一不在，原形便露。我如今看到的你，是个庸俗吵闹的怨愤之徒，肌肉因疏于操练而松软，脾气也叫这几个月你过的那种无法无天、寻欢作乐的日子泡坏了。这样的人，我毫无用处，冈特雷的莫里斯。我要身边尽是真汉子，不是那些只会咋咋呼呼、主子一走便既没荣誉也没气力约束部下的废物吹牛客。”

===Original===
Livid with rage, Maurice sprang forward again. His passion enveloped him, so that all semblance of sanity was gone. Simon had supplied the spark that was needed to set his rancour in a blaze. In a flash he had whipped his dagger from its sheath and had rushed upon Simon, blindly.

There was a moment’s wild struggle, and then Simon’s hands were about his wrists like iron clamps, bearing them downwards. Panting, Maurice glared into the green-blue eyes, and saw them passionless.

“Twice in my life hath a man sought to slay me foully,” Simon said. “This is the second time. The first was when a base cur, a traitor little above the swine, could not worst me in a fight. Then, being base, he drew steel and would have stabbed me.” He paused, staring grimly into Maurice’s eyes, until they sank, and the dark head with them. Then, with a quick, scornful movement he released Gountray’s wrists, and turned away, presenting his back, fair mark for an assassin’s dagger.

The tinkle of steel falling on the stone floor sounded behind him, and a man’s laboured breathing. He went quickly to a chair, and sat down, not even looking at Gountray.

Maurice spoke unsteadily.

===Chinese===
莫里斯气得脸色铁青，再次扑上前去。怒火把他整个吞没，神智的影子一点不剩。他积郁的怨毒，正缺西蒙递来的这点火星，此刻轰然燃起。眨眼间他已从鞘里拔出匕首，什么也看不见似的朝西蒙直扑过去。

一阵狂野的扭打过后，西蒙的双手已如铁箍般钳住他的手腕，向下压去。莫里斯喘着粗气，瞪着那双蓝绿色的眼睛，看到的只有一片沉静，毫无怒意。

“我这一生，有人想要卑鄙地杀我，至今两回，”西蒙说。“这是第二回。头一回，是个下贱的狗东西，一个比猪高不了多少的叛徒，拳脚上赢不了我，便使出下作手段，拔出利刃要捅我。”他顿住，森然盯进莫里斯的眼底，直到那双眼睛垂了下去，深色的头颅也随之低下。而后，他带着一个迅疾而轻蔑的动作松开冈特雷的手腕，转过身去，把后背亮给对方——那是刺客匕首最好的靶子。

身后响起钢器坠地的叮当声，还有一个男人粗重的喘息。他快步走到椅子前坐下，连看都没看冈特雷一眼。

莫里斯的声音发颤。

===Original===
“I have—never—done that—before.”

Simon said not a word. Maurice turned, flung out his hands.

“You goaded me to it! I would never have drawn steel had you not taunted me so!”

Simon turned his head and looked at him. Maurice went to the window, leaden-footed, and stood with his face averted. After a moment he came back into the room, his mouth set as though in pain.

“Well … Kill me!” he said. “My honour’s dead.”

===Chinese===
“我从前——从没——干过——这种事。”

西蒙一言不发。莫里斯转过身来，两手一摊。

“是你逼的我！你若不那么挖苦我，我绝不会拔刀！”

西蒙转过头来看他。莫里斯拖着铅块似的双脚走到窗前，背对着屋里站了片刻，随即又回到屋里，嘴角紧抿，像忍着痛。

“好啦……杀了我吧！”他说。“我的名誉已经死了。”

===Original===
Still Simon said nothing. Maurice stood before him, twisting his hands, his head bowed. Suddenly he looked up, and his voice quivered.

“Ah, can you not speak?” he cried. “Are you made of ice? I have sought to stab you foully, like a—cur! What will you do with me? Death would be welcome!”

“I seek not your death,” Simon answered sternly. “But by this one foul act have you placed your life and your fortune in my hands.”

Maurice straightened himself a little, but his head was bowed still, his fingers twitching.

“Well,” Simon said slowly, “I will make you my Marshal.”

For one whirling second Maurice was dazed. He took a hesitating step forward, staring in blank amazement. Then he recoiled.

===Chinese===
西蒙还是不作声。莫里斯站在他面前，绞着双手，垂着头。忽然他抬起头来，声音发颤。

“哎呀，你就不能说句话么？”他喊道。“你是冰做的么？我刚想卑鄙地捅你，像条——狗一样！你要拿我怎样？杀了我，我求之不得！”

“我不要你的命，”西蒙厉声答道。“可你这一桩卑鄙行径，已经把你的性命和身家，一并交到了我手里。”

莫里斯稍稍挺了挺身子，头却仍旧垂着，手指抽搐着。

“这样吧，”西蒙缓缓地说。“我立你做我的纠仪长。”

一瞬间莫里斯只觉天旋地转，整个人懵住了。他迟疑着上前一步，怔怔地瞪着，随即又向后缩。

===Original===
“Ah, you mock at me!” he cried.

“I do not mock.”

Maurice opened his mouth to speak, but only passed his tongue between his dry lips. He was trembling, and sweat stood on his brow.

“Will—will you not—explain—?” he said hoarsely.

“Sit down,” Simon ordered him, and waited to see him sink limply into a chair. “What I have said, I have said. I will make you my Marshal, but I will have obedience from you.”

“But—but—” Gountray’s hand flew to his head as one in wild bewilderment “—I sought to kill you! In that moment I could have done it, ay, and would have done it!”

===Chinese===
“啊，你这是耍弄我！”他喊道。

“我不是耍弄你。”

莫里斯张口想说话，却只伸出舌头舔了舔干裂的嘴唇。他在发抖，额上沁出了汗。

“你——你能不能——解释一下——？”他哑着嗓子说。

“坐下。”西蒙命令道，等着看他瘫软地陷进椅子里。“我说出的话，绝无更改。我会立你做我的纠仪长，但我要你服从。”

“可是——可是——”冈特雷的手猛地抱住脑袋，慌乱得没了方寸“——我是存心要杀你的！那一刻我下得了手，是的，也真会下手！”

===Original===
“I know.”

“Then—My lord, you torture me! What punishment will you inflict?”

“None.”

“None!” Gountray came to his feet. “You—you—forgive?”

“I forget,” Simon said.

“But why, why? What have I done to deserve your mercy?”

===Chinese===
“我知道。”

“那——老爷，你这是在折磨我！你要给我什么惩罚？”

“不给。”

“不给？”冈特雷腾地站起身。“你——你竟肯——饶恕？”

“我忘了。”西蒙说。

“可为什么，为什么？我做过什么，配得上你的开恩？”

===Original===
“Naught. It is my pleasure. Sit ye down again, and listen. When I came hither I did find your men disorderly and drunken, yourself no better. Yet I do know a man when I see one, and I do know that ye are one, if ye will it so. And I do also know a ruler of men and a fighter. Therefore I say that I will make ye Marshal in Edmund’s room, where ye shall prove yourself worthy of my trust. But I will have obedience and no black looks. So if ye hate me and wish me dead, get thee gone from Beauvallet, for thou art of no use to me.”

There fell a long silence. Then as Simon’s words sank well into his soul, Maurice came to his knees before him, sobbing drily in overwrought gasps.

“Ye cannot mean what ye say! What trust could ye place in me?—a cur who is like to stab you in the back when ye are unarmed!”

Simon smiled a little at that, but he said nothing.

“Hanging is my desert! Ye have said that ye found all in disorder here, and myself a drunken sot! True it is—God pity me! What use have you for me now?”

“I have told you.”

===Chinese===
“什么也没做。我乐意如此。重新坐下，听我说。我来的时候，你手下的人散漫酗酒，你自己也好不了多少。可我一向看得出何等样人是块材料，我也看得出你是一块，只要你愿意。我同样看得出，你既能驭人，也能打仗。所以我说，我要立你接埃德蒙的缺做纠仪长，你要证明自己配得上我的信任。但我既要你服从，也不要你摆脸色。你若恨我、盼我死，就趁早离开博瓦莱，你那样对我毫无用处。”

长久的沉默。西蒙的话一字一字沉进他的心底，莫里斯扑通跪倒在他面前，一阵阵急促的干噎哽在喉头。

“您说的不可能是真心话！您能信我什么？——信一条趁您手无寸铁就朝您后背捅刀子的狗么！”

听了这话西蒙微微一笑，却没有作声。

“绞索才是我应得的下场！你说过你发现这里乱作一团，说我是个醉鬼酒囊！全是实情——上帝可怜我吧！你如今还能拿我有什么用？”

“我说过了。”

===Original===
Then Maurice caught his hand and kissed it.

“My lord, I swear that since ye are pleased to forget my treachery and to elevate me thus undeservedly, I will never—give you just cause to—regret it—so help me, God!”

“That I know,” Simon said calmly, and laid his hand on Gountray’s shoulder, gripping it.

Maurice raised his head and looked full into the compelling eyes.

“My lord—forgive!” he whispered.

“It is as nothing,” Simon answered, and rose. “Come thou to me this even, for there is much I would ask of you, and I think ye can fitly advise me.” He held out his hand, and after a moment’s shamed hesitation Maurice laid his own in it. In that long grip was his allegiance to Simon sealed.

===Chinese===
莫里斯一把抓住他的手，吻了上去。

“老爷，我起誓，您既甘愿忘掉我的悖逆，又这样把不配的抬举加在我身上，我绝不让您有朝一日——有正当的缘由——为今日后悔——上帝助我！”

“这个我知道，”西蒙平静地说，一手按上冈特雷的肩头，紧紧一握。

莫里斯抬起头，直直望进那双慑人心魄的眼睛。

“老爷——饶恕！”他低声说。

“这不算什么，”西蒙答道，站起身来。“今晚到我这里来，我有许多事要问你，我想你正好能替我出些主意。”他伸出手去，莫里斯羞惭地迟疑了一瞬，把自己的手放了上去。那长长的一握，就此封定了他对西蒙的效忠。

## X / 第十章

===Original===

How He Brought Order Into His Lands

===Chinese===

他如何整肃领地

===Original===

The next thing Simon did was to dismiss Nicholas of the Guards. At the same time he made it known that Basil of Mordaunt was to succeed him. Thus he did away with almost all opposition, for Basil was an easygoing, generous fellow, liked by his peers, and respected. Nicholas did not take this dismissal quietly. As soon as he was out of Simon’s hearing he fell to shouting his grievance over the estate, vowing that he would pay no heed to the new, upstart lord, but would hold his place and his men in Simon’s very teeth. In this he had little support, for the guards were weary of his hectoring and blustering. They listened to him in silence, but when he had gone they conferred amongst themselves, and for the most part agreed that they would be well rid of him. Yet for very fear of him and because they did not know their lord’s temper, they remained obedient to Nicholas until they should see which way the wind would blow. Some few declared openly that they would stand by Nicholas, but these were his friends and their number was small.

Nicholas went roaring to the men-at-arms with intent to stir up rebellion. Gountray was no friend of his, but among the men he counted some six or seven allies. He found them murmurous and ill-at-ease, for they had a new captain in Walter of Santoy who was busily employed in disciplining them. Nicholas knew better than to approach him.

===Chinese===

西蒙接下来办的一件事，便是撤了卫队的尼古拉斯（Nicholas），同时放出话去，由莫丹特（Mordaunt）的巴兹尔（Basil）接任。这一着几乎消弭了全部反对，因为巴兹尔为人随和宽厚，同侪都喜欢他，也敬重他。尼古拉斯却不肯悄悄咽下这口气。刚一走出西蒙听不见的范围，他就在领地上大嚷自己的冤屈，赌咒说不会把那个新来的暴发户老爷放在眼里，偏要当着西蒙的面占住自己的位子、攥着自己的人马。附和他的却寥寥无几，因为卫队早已受够了他的作威作福、吆五喝六。众人听他嚷时默不作声，等他一走便互相商议，大多觉得甩掉他正是求之不得。只是慑于他的威风，又不摸自家老爷的脾性，他们仍听从尼古拉斯号令，且待看清风向再说。有几个公开放话要站在尼古拉斯一边，但那都是他的私交，人数甚少。

尼古拉斯又咆哮着跑到甲士中间，存心煽动叛乱。冈特雷（Gountray）与他并不交好，可这群人里他自认还有六七个同党。只见众人窃窃私议、心神不宁——他们新来了一位统领，桑托伊（Santoy）的沃尔特（Walter），正忙着整肃军纪。尼古拉斯颇有自知之明，不敢去招惹他。

===Original===

“Maurice of Gountray will stand my friend,” said he loudly. “If Maurice is dismissed he will be at one with me. He and I will smash this fellow!”

“It is rumoured that Maurice of Gountray is Marshal in Edmund’s room,” one of his friends said uneasily.

Nicholas laughed gustily.

“A likely tale! Why, he hath sworn how he will meet this lord, and hath cursed his name! I warrant ye I shall find a friend in him.” He swaggered across the courtyard, and came most opportunely upon Gountray who emerged from a door leading into the castle.

===Chinese===

“冈特雷的莫里斯（Maurice of Gountray）会站在我这边，”他高声说。“莫里斯要是也丢了差事，必定与我同心。我俩合伙，打垮这小子！”

“有传闻说，冈特雷的莫里斯接了埃德蒙（Edmund）的缺，当上了纠仪长。”他的一个同党不安地说。

尼古拉斯纵声大笑。

“说得倒像真事儿似的！哼，他还发过誓要会一会这位老爷，把他的名字都咒遍了！我打包票，我准能在他那儿找到个朋友。”他大摇大摆穿过庭院，恰巧迎面撞上冈特雷——冈特雷正从一道通往城堡的门里走出来，时机凑巧得不能再好。

===Original===

“Ha, good Maurice!” Nicholas cried, past enmity forgotten. “Come hither, man! There is somewhat I would say to thee.”

Maurice paused a moment and waited till Nicholas came up to him.

“I have orders to see ye leave this place within the space of seven hours,” he said coldly. “Look to it that ye are gone.”

Nicholas lost a little of his colour, but he strove to laugh as at a joke.

===Chinese===

“哈，好莫里斯！”尼古拉斯喊道，往日的仇隙忘了个一干二净。“过来，伙计！我有话跟你说。”

莫里斯停了片刻，等尼古拉斯走近。

“我奉命监督你七个钟头之内离开此地。”他冷冷地说。“你自己留神，务必走人。”

尼古拉斯脸上失了几分血色，却强作笑颜，仿佛听了个笑话。

===Original===

“Why, this is pretty hearing, beshrew me! From whom do ye take your orders, Maurice of Gountray?”

Maurice looked him steadily between the eyes.

“From my lord of Beauvallet, sirrah.”

“Ho-ho! Do you tell me that, Master Gountray? But yesterday ye did speak brave words against him!”

“Much hath happened since yesterday, Nicholas Conrad, and for what I have said against my lord am I heartily ashamed. Ye will leave this land today.” He strode on, and as he passed him Nicholas noticed the chain about his neck that bespoke his marshal’s office.

===Chinese===

“哟，这可真是动听的信儿，活见鬼！冈特雷的莫里斯，你这命令是领的谁的？”

莫里斯目光笔直，紧盯着他两眉之间。

“博瓦莱大人的，你这奴才。”

“嗬嗬！冈特雷老爷，你这话是对我说的？就在昨天，你还满口豪言，把他数落了个够！”

“昨天以来发生的事可不少，尼古拉斯·康拉德（Nicholas Conrad）。我先前说过冒犯大人之言，如今羞愧无地。你今天就得离开这块领地。”他大步前行，擦身而过时，尼古拉斯瞥见他颈间那条链子——纠仪长职分的标志。

===Original===

Back he went to the guardroom to find Basil of Mordaunt in his place. Then his rage knew no bounds, but he had little support now that the men saw that my lord’s word was not idly spoken. The end of it was that Nicholas departed from Beauvallet in an hour, calling down curses on Simon’s head.

In the week that followed strange and strenuous changes were wrought in Beauvallet. Malefactors were brought to judgment and Simon’s hand was heavy upon them. When they sought to rebel, the men found that his yoke was securely round their necks, and his new officers implicitly obedient to him. The week passed in grumbling and petty mutinies, but at the end of the week men knew Simon for master. Regulations were formed, irksome at first, but sound, as the wiser fellows realised; Simon was found to be ruthlessly just, and if his rule was stern, at least he was not above knowing his men individually. He had ever a nod and a curt word of greeting for all who crossed his path, and he mingled freely amongst them, saying little, but making himself familiar to them. The peasants were set to work again, and laboured with a will, because work meant fair wages. Walter of Santoy had orders to drill his men, and although they groaned under it, they submitted, and very soon put some life into their labours, for no one knew when Simon would appear upon the scene, watching closely from under his jutting brows, chary of praise, but giving it where it was due.

Disgust was felt when he ordained that archery was to be practised, and some of the peasants who were compelled to enter into this sport grumbled loudly, and declared that Simon worked them to a shred. But when he came himself with his great bow, and shot with them they ceased their lamentations to admire his skill. And when he declared that to the man who could shoot an arrow farther than his own he would award a prize of a grant of land, competition became keen, and day after day saw the serfs fitting arrows to bow till they could almost rival the archers themselves.

Within the castle all was quiet. Master Hubert had departed, wailing, and the new steward slipped into his place. There was plenty of work and plenty of good food, a fair dole of ale or sack, and sports to occupy spare hours. In a surprisingly short time the men of Beauvallet settled down under the new regime, and were content.

===Chinese===

他回到卫队室，却发现巴兹尔已经坐在了他的位子上。这一下他的怒火再无遮拦，可众人眼见大人的话绝非空口白话，附和他的已没有几个。结局便是：不出一个钟头，尼古拉斯便离开了博瓦莱，一路把毒咒泼向西蒙的头顶。

接下来的一星期，博瓦莱里立起了种种新奇而剧烈的变革。作奸犯科之徒被一一送上审判，西蒙的手腕沉重似铁。众人想要造反时，才发现他的轭已稳稳套在自己颈上，而他的新任属官对他唯命是从。这一个星期牢骚不绝、小规模哗变此起彼伏，可到了周末，人人都已认下西蒙这位主子。章程一条条立了起来，起初惹人恼火，却都切实可行，明白些的人渐渐看出了其中道理；人们发现西蒙执法无情却公正，治下虽严，他却并不觉得挨个认得手下人有失身份。凡与他打个照面的，他总有一记点头、一句简慢的招呼；他毫不拘礼地混在众人中间，话不多，却让自己为大家所熟识。农夫们重新被派上活计，干得卖力，因为干活意味着公道的工钱。桑托伊的沃尔特奉命操练人马，众人虽然怨声载道，却也不敢不从，很快便在操练中来了精神，因为谁也不知道西蒙几时会突然现身，从高高隆起的眉骨下密切注视——他吝于夸奖，可该夸的绝不含糊。

他下令操练箭术，引来一片反感，一些被迫加入这桩营生的农夫大声嘀咕，说西蒙要把他们使唤得脱层皮。可等他亲自背着那张大弓来同他们一起射，众人便停了哀叹，转而叹服他的箭艺。待他宣布，谁的箭射得比他远，便赏谁一块封地，竞争顿时白热化；一天又一天，农奴们搭箭在弦，练得几乎能同正经弓箭手一较高下了。

城堡之内一片安宁。休伯特老爷（Hubert）哀叹着离去，新总管悄然补位。活计充足，饭食丰盛，麦酒或葡萄酒各有一份像样的定量，闲暇时还有游艺消遣。不过短短时日，博瓦莱的人便在新规矩底下安顿下来，心满意足。

===Original===

It was not until the end of the month that Montlice rode over to see Simon. He came without warning one day, and appeared before the castle just before ten, accompanied by his son and his cousin. Simon was shooting with his men, so Gountray, who received the guests, dispatched Arnold, Simon’s page, to fetch him.

Arnold sped out across the country, clad in the new green-and-russet livery. He came upon Simon among the archers, in the act of loosing an arrow from his bow.

Simon watched the arrow’s flight, and without turning his head, spoke to his page.

“Well, Arnold?”

This was an uncanny trick he had, and which greatly bewildered and discomposed his men. No matter how softly one might creep up to him, he always knew of the approach, and needed not to see who it was who drew near. Arnold was accustomed to the trick, so he showed no surprise.

“My lord, there are guests at the castle! My Lord of Montlice, Sir Alan, and my Lord of Granmere. Master Gountray sent me to fetch you.”

===Chinese===

直到月底，富尔克才骑马过来看西蒙。一天，他毫无预告地来了，十点不到便出现在城堡前，随行的是他的儿子和表兄。西蒙正与部下射箭，接待宾客的冈特雷便派西蒙的侍童阿诺德去唤他。

阿诺德身穿崭新的绿褐相间号衣，飞奔着穿过原野。他在弓箭手中间找到了西蒙，后者正张弓放箭。

西蒙目送飞箭，头也不回，对侍童开了口。

“怎么样，阿诺德？”

他有这么一手怪异的本事，把部下们弄得又困惑又狼狈。无论来人多么轻手轻脚，他总能察觉靠近的动静，甚至不必看来者是谁。阿诺德惯熟这一手，故而不显惊讶。

“大人，城堡里来客人了！蒙利斯大人、艾伦爵士，还有格雷曼大人。冈特雷老爷打发我来请您。”

===Original===

Simon rose from his knee.

“I will come,” he said. He stayed but to speak with Santoy a moment and followed Arnold to the castle. Arnold would have taken his bow, but Simon shook his head, smiling.

“How far wouldst thou bear it, child?”

Arnold drew himself up till he stood half as high as the bow.

“I could carry it, my lord, indeed!”

“I doubt not thy good will,” Simon said, but he would not relinquish the bow.

===Chinese===

西蒙单膝起身。

“这就来。”他说。他只停下来同桑托伊说了片刻话，便随阿诺德往城堡去。阿诺德伸手要接他的弓，西蒙笑着摇头。

“你能扛多远，小家伙？”

阿诺德挺直身子，站起来刚好有半张弓高。

“我拿得动，大人，真的！”

“你的好意我不疑心。”西蒙说，可弓仍不肯撒手。

===Original===

Arnold walked demurely behind him then. It was a curious turn of character in Simon that he liked children. His pages fell over one another to serve him and were perfectly happy if he but nodded to them, while the littlest one of all’s pride when Simon lifted him over a broad ditch one day knew no bounds. He was Gountray’s son, a dark, curly-headed boy of eight named Cedric, who owed his office to his own impertinence. When he found that his father would not speak for him to Simon, he determined to speak for himself. So up he went to the castle, a chubby little fellow with merry eyes, and waylaid Simon on his way out.

Simon, remembering his own coming to Fulk of Montlice, was amused. He made Cedric page with Gountray’s consent, and the child seemed to walk straight into his rather dormant heart. He was the one person in all Beauvallet who would openly defy Simon, and once when he burst into tears of rage at being thwarted, his father and the Secretary were struck dumb by the sight of him seated on Simon’s knee in the great hall.

He it was who now entertained Simon’s visitors with engaging and solemn conversation.

===Chinese===

于是阿诺德规规矩矩地跟在他身后。西蒙的性子里有一处古怪的地方：他喜欢孩子。他的侍童们争先恐后地伺候他，只要他点一点头，便觉得幸福无比；而顶小的那个更是自豪得无以复加——有一天西蒙把他抱过一道宽水沟，打那以后他便觉得自己了不得。这孩子是冈特雷的儿子，黑黑的脸、卷卷的头发，八岁年纪，名叫塞德里克（Cedric）；他这份差事，全凭自己那份没大没小挣来。他见父亲不肯替他向西蒙开口，便决定自己为自己开口。于是他一路奔上城堡，一个圆滚滚、眼睛欢亮的小家伙，在西蒙出门的路上把他截住了。

西蒙想起自己当年投奔蒙利斯的富尔克的情形，觉得有趣。他征得冈特雷同意，让塞德里克做了侍童；这孩子仿佛径直走进了他那颗久已沉眠的心。全博瓦莱敢公然违逆西蒙的，独他一人；有一回他心愿受挫、气得放声大哭，他父亲和书记官（Secretary）看见他坐在西蒙膝头——就在大殿之上——都惊得说不出话来。

此刻正以娓娓动听又煞有介事的谈吐招待西蒙客人的，正是这位小爷。

===Original===

“And who art thou, young hop o’ my thumb?” Fulk asked him.

Cedric answered importantly.

“I am my lord’s page. I made him take me.”

Fulk burst into a roar of laughter.

“Oh, tit for Simon’s tat!” he cried. “How didst thou make him, prithee?”

“I said that I *would* be his page. And I am. He calls me the little one.”

===Chinese===

“那么你又是哪一位呀，小拇指般的小不点儿？”富尔克问他。

塞德里克神气活现地答道。

“我是大人的侍童。是我叫他收我的。”

富尔克轰然大笑。

“哦，好一桩一报还一报，这回报到西蒙头上啦！敢问小爷，你是怎么叫他收下的？”

“我说了我偏要当他的侍童。现在我当上了。他管我叫小家伙。”

===Original===

Alan smiled, drawing the small person to him.

“That sounds not like Simon,” he remarked. “Dost thou like thy lord?”

“Ay, I love him dearly. As much as my father.” Cedric paused to give weight to his next statement. “I have sat upon his knee,” he announced with due solemnity.

“Holy Virgin!” Fulk said. “What comes to our Simon?”

Simon entered at this moment, and Cedric, wriggling free of Alan’s hold, skipped towards him.

===Chinese===

艾伦微笑着，把这小人儿揽到身边。

“这话可不像西蒙的做派。”他评了一句。“你喜欢你的大人吗？”

“喜欢，我爱他爱得不得了。跟我爱爹一样多。”塞德里克停顿了一下，好给接下来那句话添足分量。“我坐过他的膝盖。”他以应有的庄重宣布。

“圣母在上！”富尔克道。“我们的西蒙这是怎么了？”

恰在此时西蒙走了进来，塞德里克从艾伦怀里扭脱出来，蹦跳着迎上去。

===Original===

“My lord, I received these guests with my father, and I gave them chairs, but I have not done your bidding!” He chuckled mischievously and danced before Simon.

Simon gave him his arrows.

“Put these away then, little miscreant—and see thou dost not play with them!” he added as Cedric trotted off. He came forward and grasped Fulk’s hand.

“My lord, ye are more than welcome, and you, Lord of Granmere. Well, Alan?”

===Chinese===

“大人，我跟我爹一起接待了客人，还给他们让了座，可您吩咐的事我还没办呢！”他顽皮地咯咯一笑，在西蒙面前手舞足蹈。

西蒙把箭递给他。

“那就把这些收好，小捣蛋鬼——可不许拿它们闹着玩！”他补上一句，塞德里克小跑着去了。西蒙上前来，握住富尔克的手。

“大人，您肯大驾光临，真是再欢迎不过，格雷曼大人也是。怎么说，艾伦？”

===Original===

“Never saw I so great a change in any land!” Fulk assured him. “We came to pry upon thee and to see how thou wert progressing, and behold! the place is as orderly as a monastery! As we passed we saw on all sides good work on hand, while as for thy household, it is as quiet as the grave! What hast done, lion-cub?”

“It was very easy,” Simon answered. “I struck at the heads of the disorder. How fares Montlice?”

“We miss thy strict hand,” Fulk grimaced. “But Alan doth what he can. God’s my life, when I think that scarce a month ago this land was peopled by drunken rogues, and the crops going to ruin for want of care, and look at it now, I can scarce believe mine eyes!”

“I am not surprised,” Granmere remarked. “From what I had seen of thee, I had thought to see thee conquer within the month. Who was yon chubby page?”

===Chinese===

“天底下哪块领地见过这么大的变化！”富尔克向他担保。“我们本是来窥探你的，想瞧瞧你干得怎么样，谁知一看！这地方齐整得像座修道院！一路过来，只见四下里活计井井有条；至于你的家务，更是静得像座坟墓！你都干了什么，小狮子？”

“容易得很。”西蒙答道。“我专打乱源的头子。蒙利斯近来如何？”

“我们还真想你那把铁腕。”富尔克做了个鬼脸。“好在艾伦勉力撑持。老天爷在上，想想一个月前，这地方住着的还尽是醉醺醺的无赖，庄稼没人经管，眼看烂在地里；再瞧瞧如今，我简直不敢信自己的眼睛！”

“我毫不意外。”格雷曼品评道。“凭我先前对你的见识，我早料到你不出一个月便能大功告成。方才那个圆脸蛋的侍童是谁？”

===Original===

Simon smiled a little.

“That is my Marshal’s son.”

“Who sits upon thy knee,” Alan teased.

Simon looked up.

“Did he say that? ’Twas but once, when he cried because that I chid him for some fault.”

===Chinese===

西蒙微微一笑。

“那是我的纠仪长的儿子。”

“还会坐你膝头呢。”艾伦打趣道。

西蒙抬起头来。

“他这么说的？统共就那么一回：他犯了错，我责备了他，他就哭了。”

===Original===

“Simon,” Fulk interrupted, “I demand that ye loose thy tongue and tell me all that thou hast performed here!”

“Well, sir, if ye must have the full tale, will ye come out whiles my varlets lay dinner?”

“Ay, that will we,” Fulk nodded, and rose. “Alan would stay with thee, if thou’lt permit him.”

Alan locked his arm in Simon’s affectionately.

“I shall stay whether thou likst it or no.”

“Why, of course thou canst stay!” Simon said, and led them forth into the sunlight.

===Chinese===

“西蒙，”富尔克打断他，“我要你打开话匣子，把你在这儿成就的每一件事都讲给我听！”

“好吧，老爷，您既要听全须全尾，可愿到外头走走，趁我的仆役们摆饭？”

“好啊，走。”富尔克点头起身。“艾伦想留下来陪你，只要你允许他。”

艾伦亲热地挽住西蒙的胳膊。

“管你乐意不乐意，我横竖是要留下的。”

“嗳，你自然留得！”西蒙说着，领他们走进阳光里。

===Original===

They returned presently to dinner, when Simon presented his marshal, his captain, and all his other officers. It was nearly three hours later when they came away from the table, and Fulk took Simon aside.

“Simon lad, thou art now come to manhood,” he began, by way of preamble. “There is a proposition I would set before thee.”

“My lord?”

Fulk tapped him on the shoulder.

“Look ye, boy, thy land should have a mistress, ay, and an heir! Now it is in my mind to give thee my daughter Elaine, though I had intended her for John of Balfry’s son. What dost thou say to that?”

===Chinese===

不久众人回来用饭，西蒙把他的纠仪长、统领和其余各位属官一一引见。离席已是将近三个钟头之后，富尔克把西蒙拉到一旁。

“西蒙，我的孩子，你如今已然成人。”他开场引话道。“我有桩事要向你提出来。”

“大人？”

富尔克拍了拍他的肩。

“你听着，小子，你的领地该有位女主人，是啊，还该有个继承人！如今我心里的盘算，是把我的女儿伊莱恩许给你，虽然我原本是把她留给巴尔弗里的约翰（John of Balfry）的儿子的。你对此怎么说？”

===Original===

Simon compressed his lips.

“Why, sir, I say that albeit I do thank thee for the honour ye would do me, yet were it best that ye should give the lady to Robert of Balfry.”

“Thou’lt none of her?” Fulk was incredulous. “Bethink you, silly boy, she is comely and gentle, and fair-dowered!”

“Ay, sir, but she loves not me, and I love not her.”

===Chinese===

西蒙抿紧嘴唇。

“老爷，我的回答是：您欲加于我的殊荣，我感激不尽，可您最好还是把这位小姐许给巴尔弗里的罗伯特（Robert of Balfry）。”

“她你都不要？”富尔克难以置信。“想想清楚，傻小子，她容貌标致，性情温柔，嫁妆又丰厚！”

“是啊，老爷，可她不爱我，我也不爱她。”

===Original===

Fulk was inclined to be offended.

“Mayhap thou dost look higher for thy bride?”

“Nay. I look nowhere for a bride. I have no love for women, and I think to remain a bachelor.”

“But that is folly, lad!” Fulk cried, a little appeased. “A docile wife is a great thing to have!”

“Is it, sir?” Simon said drily. “Methinks I admire not gentleness, nor docility.”

===Chinese===

富尔克颇有动气之意。

“莫非你想为新娘子攀个更高的门第？”

“不。我哪里都不去找新娘。我对女人没有爱意，打算终身做个光棍。”

“可那是傻话，孩子！”富尔克嚷道，气消了些。“有个温顺的妻子可是天大的好事！”

“是么，老爷？”西蒙干巴巴地说。“依我看，温柔我欣赏不来，温顺更不必说。”

===Original===

“But, thou dost love children, Simon!”

“Do I?” Simon considered the point. “Nay, I think not.”

“Thou dost, lad! What of thy little page?”

“Cedric? Yes, I do care for him, yet I want him not for mine own.”

“Simon, Simon, thou quibblest! Since I have been in Beauvallet I have seen more pages than thou canst possibly have need of! What made thee take them—children that they are?”

===Chinese===

“可你的确喜欢孩子，西蒙！”

“我喜欢么？”西蒙把这一点思量了一番。“不，我想并不。”

“你就是喜欢，孩子！那你那个小侍童怎么说？”

“塞德里克？是啊，我确是疼他，可我并不想要属于我自己的孩子。”

“西蒙，西蒙，你这是强词夺理！我来博瓦莱这些日子，见到的侍童之多，你根本用不着！你收他们做什么——他们还是些孩子呢！”

===Original===

“They—they are useful to me,” Simon answered, rather lamely. “They run mine errands.”

“How many hast thou?” Fulk demanded sternly.

“Six,” Simon said gruffly.

“And what does one man want with six pages?” Fulk persisted.

“I—I find employment for them.”

===Chinese===

“他们——他们对我有用处，”西蒙答道，颇有几分底气不足。“他们替我跑腿。”

“你有几个？”富尔克厉声盘问。

“六个。”西蒙粗声粗气地说。

“一个大男人要六个侍童做什么？”富尔克紧追不放。

“我——我给他们找差事做。”

===Original===

“Tush!” said Fulk. “Thou dost like to have them follow thee about.”

“Nay! I send them from me—when they plague me.”

“Simon, thou canst not deceive me,” Fulk told him. “Thou hast a love for children, and shouldst breed thine own.”

Simon flushed a little.

“Nay.”

===Chinese===

“呸！”富尔克说。“你就是喜欢他们跟前随后地跟着你。”

“才不是！他们缠磨我的时候，我都把他们撵走。”

“西蒙，你骗不了我，”富尔克对他说。“你就是疼爱孩子，该自己生养几个。”

西蒙微微涨红了脸。

“不。”

===Original===

“And I say, ay!”

“My lord, it is to no avail that ye seek to persuade me. I will take no woman to wife.”

Fulk grunted, but he knew Simon too well to argue any further.

“Well, please thyself. But one day ye will know that I was right, and a man must take a wife unto himself.”

“I will tell you when that day comes,” Simon promised.

===Chinese===

“我却说，是！”

“大人，你想劝服我，那是枉然。我不会娶任何女子为妻。”

富尔克哼了一声，但他太了解西蒙，没有再争下去。

“好吧，随你的意。可总有一天你会明白我是对的，男人总得娶一房妻子。”

“等到那一天，我会告诉你。”西蒙许诺。

---

===Original===

Alan remained at Beauvallet a week, and Simon was rather glad of his companionship. He organised a chase for Alan’s amusement, and hired mummers from a neighbouring town. But Alan was quite content to dispense with these forms of entertainment, and to please Simon he went with him to practise archery. When he came away from this tedious sport, he shot Simon a sidelong glance. Simon was aware of it, without seeing it.

“Well?”

“How hast thou contrived to endear these men to thee, Simon?”

“Have I? Some of them like me not.”

===Chinese===

艾伦在博瓦莱盘桓了一个星期，西蒙对有他作伴颇为欢喜。他安排了一场围猎给艾伦解闷，又从邻镇雇来一班演剧的优伶。可艾伦对这些娱乐全不放在心上，倒为了讨西蒙欢喜，随他一同去练射艺。这项乏味的消遣散场时，他斜睨了西蒙一眼。西蒙没有看见，却察觉到了。

“怎么了？”

“你是怎么设法让这些人对你满怀爱戴的，西蒙？”

“有么？有些人并不喜欢我。”

===Original===

“But most do like thee. What is it they do find to love in thee? What do any of us find? Thou art stern, and cold, and hast no love for any man.”

“Alan, if thou dost wish to prate of love, go do so to thy ladylove. I know nothing of it.”

“Why do thy men love thee?” Alan insisted.

“I know not. Perchance because I bend them to my will.”

===Chinese===

“可大多数人确实喜欢你。他们究竟在你身上找到了什么可爱之处？我们这些人又找到了什么？你严厉、冷酷，对任何人都没有爱。”

“艾伦，你若想奢谈情爱，找你的情人说去。此事我一无所知。”

“你的人为什么爱你？”艾伦不肯罢休。

“不知道。或许因为我迫使他们屈从我的意志。”

===Original===

“That may be so,” Alan mused. “But why do the children so dote on thee?”

“Because I pay but little heed to them.”

“Nay, that cannot be so. In truth, Simon, long as I have known thee, I still know thee not. Something there is ’neath thy coldness of which I wot not.”

“There is hunger,” Simon said, thereby closing the conversation.

===Chinese===

“或许是这样。”艾伦沉吟道。“可孩子们为何也这般疼恋你？”

“因为我极少留意他们。”

“不，不会是这样。说实话，西蒙，我认识你虽久，却仍看不透你。你的冷淡之下藏着什么东西，是我所不知道的。”

“有饥饿。”西蒙说，就此结束了这场谈话。

---

===Original===

When Alan had returned to Montlice, Simon set about reforming his men-at-arms, and archers, with so much success that within the space of six months he had a very fair army at his beck and call, composed of peasants’ sons, and some wandering soldiers. Walter of Santoy proved himself an admirable captain, so that Simon relaxed some of his vigilance, and turned his attention to the cultivation of his land. In Gountray he had full confidence, and Maurice would have worked himself to death to please his lord.

And so the year rolled placidly by and the New Year came. Then, when Simon had begun to look about him in search of fresh emprises, came Geoffrey of Malvallet, his father, one damp morning, to visit him.

When word was brought of his coming, Simon went swiftly out to meet him, and knelt to receive his guest.

===Chinese===

艾伦回到蒙利斯之后，西蒙着手整训他的甲士和弓箭手，成效卓著：不出六个月，一支颇为可观的军队已能随传随到，成员尽是农家子弟，外加一些漂泊四方的散兵。

就这样，一年平静地流转过去，新年来临。此时西蒙已开始环顾四方，寻觅新的功业；一个阴湿的清晨，他的父亲马尔瓦莱的杰弗里（Geoffrey of Malvallet）前来拜访。

得报贵客临门，西蒙疾步出迎，跪地以迎宾客。

===Original===

“My lord, ye do me great honour,” he said gravely.

Geoffrey raised him.

“I hardly dared come to thee, Simon, but now I have an excuse for this visit which perhaps thou dost think importunate.”

Simon led him to his private room.

“Nay, sir, I am honoured.”

===Chinese===

“大人，您这实在是我的莫大荣幸。”他肃然道。

杰弗里扶他起身。

“西蒙，我几乎不敢来见你。不过这回登门，我总算有个由头——这趟拜访，你或许正嫌其冒昧。”

西蒙把他引进私室。

“不，老爷，是我的荣幸。”

===Original===

Geoffrey glanced around.

“Well, thou hast estates, after all. Of thine own endeavour.”

“As I did say I would have them,” Simon answered, and sent a page to bring ale. “What is your will of me, sir?”

“I am the bearer of a letter to thee from thy half-brother,” Malvallet answered. “Will ye read it?”

“From Geoffrey? Ay, that will I, and gladly! Will ye not be seated, sir?”

===Chinese===

杰弗里环顾四下。

“好啊，你毕竟有了田产。全凭你自己出力挣来。”

“如我当年所说，我自会有田产。”西蒙答道，随即派侍童去取麦酒。“老爷，您要我为您做什么？”

“我给你捎来了你异母兄弟的一封信。”马尔瓦莱答道。“你要看么？”

“杰弗里写来的？要看，自然要看，求之不得！老爷请坐可好？”

===Original===

Malvallet chose a chair by the window, and watched Simon break the seals of Geoffrey’s letter.

“To Simon, Lord of Beauvallet.

“Dear and entirely well-beloved, I greet thee well, and send messages of joy and congratulation on thy new good fortune. I do know thy land and like it well. May thou prosper exceedingly as thou deservest!

===Chinese===

马尔瓦莱在窗边拣了把椅子坐下，看着西蒙拆开杰弗里信上的封印。

“致博瓦莱领主西蒙。

“亲爱的、至为挚爱的兄弟，我问候你安好，并为你的新好运致以欣喜与祝贺。你的领地我是识得的，且甚是喜欢。愿你昌盛兴隆，不负你所配得的福分！

===Original===

“My brother, I do write to urge thee that thou shouldst come hither with what force thou mayst muster to join again with the Prince in quelling that most naughty rebel, Owen de Glyndourdy, whose followers are rife in this ill-fated land. Despite the fair promises of His Majesty’s Council, made in August, saying that he should have men and provisions enough to march boldly out against the rebels, naught hath been forthcoming, and at this date at which I write our force numbers little over five score men-at-arms and twelve score archers. Now thou art thine own master wilt thou come not again to fight at my side as thou didst promise? Matters grow serious here in Wales, for thou must know that in December of last year fell Cardiff, and Harlech, and Llampadarn, our most cherished fortresses. The rebel Owen hath not been so great before, and indeed, if we are to conquer him we must set out against him, and that as soon as spring shall have come. And with the spring, come thou, my brother, and I will promise thee as goodly a battle as that of Shrewsbury which thou didst so much enjoy.

===Chinese===

“兄弟，我写信是要力劝你，把你调集得起的兵力统统带来，与亲王再度会合，剿平那大逆不道的叛贼欧文·格伦道尔；他的党羽在这片多灾多难的土地上甚为猖獗。陛下的御前会议八月间虽有美言许诺，说他必有充足的兵员粮草，足以放开手脚出讨叛军，到头来却一无下文；至我落笔之日，我军人马不过甲士百余名、弓箭手二百四十名。如今你自己便是自家的主人，可肯不负前约，再来与我并肩作战？威尔士这边局势愈发严重，你须知去年十二月，我们最珍爱的几座要塞——卡迪夫（Cardiff）、哈莱克（Harlech）、兰帕达恩（Llampadarn）——相继陷落。叛贼欧文声势从未如此浩大；说实在的，我们若要剿灭他，便须出师征讨，而且春回即须动身。待到春回，就来吧，我的兄弟。我保你有一场大好厮杀，不逊于你当年乐在其中的什鲁斯伯里之战。

===Original===

“I send thee my love and greetings.

“Geoffrey of Malvallet.

“Written at Shrewsbury.”

Simon folded the parchment slowly.

===Chinese===

“谨致爱意与问候。

“马尔瓦莱的杰弗里。

“写于什鲁斯伯里。”

西蒙慢慢折起羊皮纸。

===Original===

“Wilt thou go?” Malvallet asked abruptly.

Simon seemed to consider. His eyes wandered to the window and stared out across the quiet fields. He brought them back to his father, and smiled.

“It seems likely, my lord,” he said.

===Chinese===

“你去么？”马尔瓦莱突兀地问。

西蒙似在思量。他的目光游移到窗前，越过静谧的田野凝望出去，而后收回来望向他的父亲，微微一笑。

“多半是要去的，大人。”他说。

---

===Original===

He rode next day to Montlice to take counsel of Fulk. To my lord’s surprise Alan sprang up, vehement.

“If thou dost go, Simon, then so will I!” he exclaimed. “Too long have I rested at home! I will lead our men to Wales, and I too, will taste the joys of battle!”

When he had recovered from his amazement, Fulk scoffed.

===Chinese===

次日他骑马前往蒙利斯，向富尔克问计。令老爷吃惊的是，艾伦霍地站起，言辞激昂。

“西蒙，你若去，我也去！”他嚷道。“我在家里闲得太久了！我要领咱们的人马去威尔士，我也要尝尝作战的滋味！”

富尔克惊魂稍定，嗤笑一声。

===Original===

“Little joy wilt thou find in battle.”

Alan turned sharply.

“If thou dost say me nay, my lord, then will I go in Simon’s train. Alone!”

“No need for such heat,” Fulk grunted. “Thou shalt go if thou dost wish it. When dost thou think to depart, Simon?”

===Chinese===

“作战的滋味，你可尝不出多少欢乐。”

艾伦猛然转身。

“您若说个不字，大人，我便随西蒙的队伍走。一个人走！”

“犯不上发这么大的火，”富尔克哼道。“你愿意去，便去。西蒙，你打算几时动身？”

===Original===

“Next month, my lord, towards the end, so that I shall come to Wales in March.”

“And leave thy land masterless?”

“Nay. Maurice of Gountray shall rule in my stead.”

“As he ruled when Barminster died?” Fulk inquired with heavy sarcasm.

“I am not Barminster,” Simon said.

===Chinese===

“下个月，大人，月末动身，如此三月初我便能到威尔士。”

“就这样把你的领地撂成无主之地？”

“不会。冈特雷的莫里斯将代我治理。”

“就像巴明斯特死时他代管的那样？”富尔克语含重重讥刺地问道。

“我不是巴明斯特。”西蒙说。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
How He Won His Gilded Armour

March saw him in Wales at his brother’s side, engaged in hard fighting and hard generalship. April brought him back to Shrewsbury unscathed, but May saw him marching south to Usk, one of the Prince’s trusted officers, and the Prince’s friend. And at Usk, where they fought the rebels fifteen hundred strong, he engaged with Glyndourdy’s son Griffith, and fought him in single combat till he had him worsted from sheer fatigue. Then took he Griffith prisoner and surrendered him to the Prince.

Henry was enthusiastic over his prize, and smote Simon on the back.

“Ah, Beauvallet! Would that I had thee ever by my side! What wilt thou of thy prisoner?”

“His armour, sir,” Simon answered. “His ransom, if ransomed he be, is yours. But, if it pleases your Highness, I would have his gilded armour.”

===Chinese===
他如何赢得鎏金铠甲

三月，他在威尔士随兄长出征，一面苦战，一面苦心统兵。四月，他安然返回什鲁斯伯里；到了五月，他又挥师南下乌斯克（Usk）——此时他已是亲王信任的将领之一，也是亲王的朋友。在乌斯克，他们与一千五百之众的叛军交锋，西蒙与格伦道尔之子格里菲斯（Griffith）交上了手，两人单打独斗，直斗到格里菲斯纯粹力竭不支。随即他生擒格里菲斯，将他献与亲王。

亨利见了自己这份战利，喜不自胜，一掌拍在西蒙背上。

“啊，博瓦莱！但愿你永在我身边！你想得俘虏些什么？”

“他的铠甲，殿下。”西蒙答道。“他的赎金，若他当真被赎，便归您。但若殿下恩准，我想要他这副鎏金铠甲。”

===Original===
“That is a strange wish!” Henry said. “Wherefore? Dost like the golden tint so much?”

“Ay, and the workmanship, sir.”

“Thou shalt have it, then,” Henry promised. “Simon of the Gilded Armour!” He laughed, linking his arm in Simon’s. “Verily, I do believe it is a new title thou seekest! Already have I heard tell of Simon the Lynx-Eyes, Simon the Coldheart, Simon the Lion, Simon the Soft-Footed, and I know not what beside! Whence come these names, lad?”

“From foolish men’s tongues, my lord,” Simon answered.

“Then shall I be foolish,” Henry said, “for I shall call thee Simon the Silent.”

===Chinese===
“这可是个怪念头！”亨利说。“缘因何在？你就这么喜欢这层金色？”

“是，还喜欢它的做工，殿下。”

“那便赐你，”亨利许诺道。“‘金甲’西蒙（Simon of the Gilded Armour）！”他大笑着挽住西蒙的手臂。“说真的，我看你是在给自己谋一个新封号呢！什么‘锐目’西蒙、‘冷心’西蒙（Simon the Coldheart）、‘狮子’西蒙、‘轻足’西蒙，还有什么别的名号，我都听人说起过了！这些名头都是打哪儿传出来的，小子？”

“出自愚人的舌头，大人。”西蒙答道。

“那我便当一回愚人，”亨利说，“我就唤你‘沉默’西蒙。”

===Original===
The middle of July saw Simon home again, with Geoffrey and Alan riding one on either side of him. Between these two enmity was dead, for when Geoffrey had clasped Simon’s hands on his coming to Wales, Alan had stood aloof and ill-at-ease, seeing which Geoffrey had gone to him with his charming smile.

“Our sires dispute, Sir Alan, but what shall we do?”

“For my part I would we might agree!” Alan had answered instantly, and grasped Malvallet’s hand.

===Chinese===
七月中旬，西蒙回到家中，杰弗里与艾伦一左一右，策马伴在他身侧。这两人之间的仇怨已然消解：当初西蒙初到威尔士，杰弗里上前紧握他的双手时，艾伦却立在一旁，疏离而局促；杰弗里瞧在眼里，便带着他那迷人的笑容走向艾伦。

“我们的父辈争执不休，艾伦爵士，可我们又当如何？”

“就我而言，我但愿我们能修好！”艾伦当即答道，一把握住了马尔瓦莱的手。

===Original===
When Simon rode into Beauvallet he found all quiet and in good order, and a glint of satisfaction came to his eyes. At the castle door his household stood to welcome him. But one there was who forgot decorum and ran forward, arms outstretched.

“My lord! my lord! Lift me? Oh, lift me!” Cedric cried, almost sobbing with excitement and heedless of his father’s shocked protest.

Then Simon the Coldheart bent in his saddle and hoisted his page up with one strong hand, and held him against his shoulder. One little arm encircled his neck, the other plump hand gripped Simon’s doublet tightly; Cedric gave a wriggle of content, and buried his face on Simon’s shoulder.

===Chinese===
西蒙策马进了博瓦莱，但见四处安静齐整，眼中掠过一丝满意。城堡门前，满府上下肃立相迎。偏有一人忘了规矩，张开双臂奔上前来。

“大人！大人！抱我？啊，抱抱我！”塞德里克（Cedric）喊道，激动得几乎哽咽，全不理会父亲惊惶的呵斥。

于是，‘冷心’西蒙俯身鞍上，一只有力的手将他的小侍童拎起，搂在自己肩头。一条小胳膊环住他的脖颈，另一只胖乎乎的小手紧紧攥住西蒙的紧身上衣；塞德里克满足地扭了扭身子，把脸埋进西蒙的肩膀。

===Original===
Simon looked down at the curly head with a curious smile on his lips.

“Thou hast missed me, Cedric?”

The arm tightened about his neck; Cedric nodded.

“Methought thou’dst have forgot thy lord.”

Up came the dark head, indignant.

“I am not a babe﻿—to forget thee so soon!”

===Chinese===
西蒙低头望着那颗鬈发的脑袋，唇边浮起一抹奇特的笑意。

“你想我了，塞德里克？”

那条胳膊把他搂得更紧；塞德里克点了点头。

“我还当你会把自家主君忘掉呢。”

那颗黑发小脑袋愤然扬起。

“我又不是奶娃娃——哪会这么快就把你忘了！”

===Original===
“Cedric!” exclaimed Gountray, coming forward. “Thou must not speak so to my lord! To say ‘thee’﻿—thus pertly!”

“I will!” Cedric announced stoutly. “My lord cares not!”

“My lord, forgive his rudeness!” Gountray said in concern. “Indeed, I can do naught with him since ye are gone. He minds me not. I doubt I am too soft with him, but I have no other son, and﻿—and perchance I spoil him with indulgence.”

===Chinese===
“塞德里克！”冈特雷（Gountray）抢上前来，失声道。“不许这样同大人讲话！直呼‘你’——这般放肆！”

“我偏要！”塞德里克斩钉截铁地说。“大人才不在乎呢！”

“大人，恕他无礼！”冈特雷忧心地说。“说真的，您不在的这些日子，我拿他毫无办法。他根本不听我的。只怕是我待他太软，可我就这一个儿子，而且——而且兴许是我把他娇纵坏了。”

===Original===
“Let be!” Simon said shortly. “Loose thy grip, little one; I would dismount.” He handed Cedric to Gountray, and swung lightly down from the saddle. He had a word of greeting for all who stood there, and many were the inquiries after his welfare. He answered each man in kind, and passed into the castle, Cedric dancing at his side, and his other pages following him like a troop of puppies, so that when he stopped to speak with his secretary he stood in the midst of a small band of green-and-russet clad boys, towering above them, while they swarmed about him, relieving him of first this, and then that, and squabbling amongst themselves for the supreme honour of bearing his sword away. One flew to unbuckle it, three others laid hold of the scabbard, glaring at one another belligerently, and two more knelt to unfasten Simon’s spurs. He seemed quite unaware of these somewhat noisy ministrations, but talked calmly over the pages’ heads to his amused secretary. Being smaller by far than the rest, Cedric found himself with naught to carry away. Not to be outdone, he climbed upon a chair and removed Simon’s cap from his head. He also tried to remove the surcoat from Simon’s shoulders, and his fat little fingers tugged busily at the clasps until Simon became aware of his efforts. Then he put them all from him.

“Have done, have done! Would ye have me quite unrobed? Go put my cap away, Cedric! Roger, take my sword from that babe; he will fall over it. Edmund, fight not over my spurs! Thou’lt scratch thyself. Take heed! And be ye all gone till I send for you, turbulent brats!” He nodded to Gountray. “I will speak with thee after supper, Maurice, and thee also, Bernard.” He strode away to the staircase, and went swiftly up to his chamber, followed only by Malcolm, his squire.

Walter of Santoy cast a laughing glance at Gountray.

===Chinese===
“由他去！”西蒙截断道。“松手，小家伙；我要下马。”他把塞德里克交给冈特雷，轻捷地翻身下鞍。他向在场诸人一一寒暄，众人也争相问他一路安好。他一一应对，随后步入城堡；塞德里克在他身侧雀跃相随，其余侍童则像一窝小狗崽似的跟在后面。待他停下与文书说话时，人已站在一小群穿绿褐两色号衣的男孩正中，高出他们一头；孩子们蜂拥围拢，先卸下他这一件，又卸下他那一样，还为谁有殊荣替他捧走佩剑而争执不下。一个扑上去解扣带，另三个抓住剑鞘，气势汹汹地互相瞪视，又有两个跪下去解西蒙的马刺。这一片闹哄哄的伺候，他浑似未觉，只神色平静地越过侍童们的头顶，与看得饶有兴味的文书交谈。塞德里克比谁都矮小，落得两手空空、无物可拿。他不肯服输，爬上一张椅子，把西蒙头上的帽子摘了下来。又想去褪西蒙肩上的罩袍，胖胖的小手指忙不迭地拽那些搭扣，直到西蒙察觉了他的举动，才把那双小手轻轻拂开。

“住手，都住手！你们是要把我扒个精光么？去把我的帽子收好，塞德里克！罗杰，把剑从那奶娃娃手里拿开，他该叫它绊倒了。埃德蒙（Edmund），不许抢马刺！会抓伤自己的。当心！统统退下，等我传唤再来，你们这群闹腾的小崽子！”他向冈特雷点了点头。“晚饭后我与你有话谈，莫里斯，还有你，伯纳德（Bernard）。”说罢大步走向楼梯，迅速上楼回了自己的居室，身后只跟着扈从马尔科姆一人。

桑托伊的沃尔特（Walter of Santoy）含笑瞥了冈特雷一眼。

===Original===
“This place will soon be overrun with pages,” he remarked. “Surely I did see three more than when we left Beauvallet?”

“Ay,” Gountray replied. “My lord had given orders they were to be enrolled. One falls over them at every step, but it is my lord’s pleasure. And since my lord did strike Patrick of Kildare senseless for beating little Edmund, two days before he set out on his travels, never have children been more indulged in this land! As for mine own son, he is grown so defiant and mischievous that only my lord can check him.”

“Things have come to a pretty pass,” the steward sighed, for he was weak with children and they plagued him unmercifully.

“Pretty indeed,” Bernard said softly. “Methinks it is a sweet thing to see the iron lord with these babes about him like flies around a honey-jar.”

“They are very importunate,” Roger complained. “They cluster about my lord so that there is naught for us poor squires to do. And he will not say them nay. And﻿—and when I did push Donald so that he fell﻿—I meant not that he should, but I was angered﻿—he would not have me near him for three whole days! So that *Malcolm* waited upon him!” At the thought of this past injury his eyes flashed, and he withdrew to dwell upon it darkly.

===Chinese===
“这府里眼看要侍童成群了。”他说道。“方才我分明看见，比咱们离开博瓦莱时又多了三个？”

“是啊，”冈特雷答道。“大人早就吩咐要把他们一一录用。如今走一步绊着一个，可这是大人的意趣。自打大人动身出游前两日，为着小埃德蒙挨打，一拳打得基尔代尔的帕特里克（Patrick of Kildare）不省人事，这地方的孩子就再没有不受娇纵的了！至于我那儿子，他变得桀骜顽劣，唯有大人还镇得住他。”

“事情落到这般美妙的田地喽。”总管叹了口气。他天生拿孩子没有办法，孩子们便毫不留情地作弄他。

“妙，实在是妙。”伯纳德柔声道。“依我看，瞧那位铁打的主君身边围着这群奶娃娃，恰似苍蝇围着蜜罐，当真是桩甜美的乐事。”

“他们可真缠人。”罗杰抱怨道。“他们把我大人团团围住，我们这些可怜的扈从便无事可做了。而大人又不肯说他们半个不字。而且——而且我推了唐纳德（Donald）一把，叫他跌了一跤——我不是存心要他摔，只是火气上来了——他就整整三天不许我靠近他！倒叫马尔科姆伺候了他一场！”一想到这桩旧怨，他眼中火光一闪，退到一边独自生闷气去了。

===Original===
After supper, Maurice of Gountray came to Simon’s room to render an account of his stewardship. Simon listened intently to all that he said, and read over the accounts. Maurice spoke hesitantly, anxious lest he should have failed to satisfy his lord. Just at the end of his recital he looked at Simon almost shyly.

“There﻿—there is one other matter, my lord, in which ye may perhaps think I have exceeded my duty. In your absence I﻿—I did what seemed best to me.” He paused, unaccountably nervous before this man who was full fifteen years his junior. Simon said nothing so Maurice continued, squaring his shoulders: “I did discover three lewd fellows, sir, among your guard, who were friends of Nicholas. They were set upon stirring the men to rebellion in your absence, the which Basil reported to me. So I did summon them to﻿—to judgment, sir, and Edwin of Palmer, whom I saw to be the leader, I banished in your name. The other two I did punish﻿—and they are quiet now.” He looked up again, diffident, and in his eyes was a look of fidelity such as is seen in the eyes of a dog.

“Thou hast done well,” Simon said. “In all things thou hast acted as I should have acted had I been here.”

===Chinese===
晚饭后，冈特雷的莫里斯来到西蒙房中，禀报他代管家业的始末。西蒙凝神听完他所说的每一句，又把账册细读一遍。莫里斯言辞迟疑，唯恐有一处不能令主上满意。禀报将毕，他望向西蒙的目光几乎有几分羞怯。

“还有——还有一桩事，大人，您或许会认为我逾越了本分。您不在的时候，我——我照自己以为最妥当的法子处置了。”他顿住了，在这整整比自己小十五岁的人面前，没来由地紧张。西蒙不置一词，莫里斯只得挺起双肩，接着说：“我查出您卫队里有三个泼皮，大人，他们是尼古拉斯（Nicholas）的朋党，趁您不在，正要煽动人马作乱，是巴兹尔（Basil）报与我的。于是我传他们来——来受审，大人；那个我看是领头的，帕尔默的埃德温（Edwin of Palmer），我以您的名义将他放逐了。另外两个我也惩治过了——如今都已安分。”他又抬起头来，怯生生的，眼中是狗一般忠贞的神色。

“你办得好。”西蒙说。“桩桩件件，你都做得同我本人在此所会做的一般无二。”

===Original===
At the sound of that cool voice, Gountray sat straighter in his chair, and one or two worried lines upon his brow were smoothed away.

“If﻿—if I have pleased you, sir, I﻿—can be easier in mine own mind.”

“I am pleased, but it is no less than I expected.”

“My lord﻿—I have but one ambition in life, and that is to merit your trust, so that I may﻿—in time﻿—wipe out the black memory of what I﻿—sought to do to you.”

===Chinese===
听见这冷静的嗓音，冈特雷在椅中坐直了些，眉间一两道愁纹也随之舒展。

“若——若我能令您满意，大人，我——心里便能安生些了。”

“我是满意，但这并未超出我的意料。”

“大人——我此生只有一个抱负，便是不辜负您的信任，好让我终有一日——洗去我曾图谋加诸您身之事的那段黑色记忆。”

===Original===
Simon brought his fist down upon the table between them.

“A year ago I said three words to thee, Maurice of Gountray: ‘I have forgotten.’ ”

“Ye have not yet said: ‘I have﻿—forgiven,’ my lord,” Gountray answered low.

“Then I say it now. I have forgiven. Though why thou shouldst want forgiveness from any man, I know not. The past is dead.”

===Chinese===
西蒙一拳砸在两人之间的桌上。

“一年之前，我对你说过三个字，冈特雷的莫里斯：‘我忘了。’”

“可您还不曾说过：‘我——宽恕。’大人。”冈特雷低声答道。

“那我现在便说。我宽恕了。虽然你何须讨任何人的宽恕，我实在不解。往事已死。”

===Original===
“My lord, I﻿—I thank you! And for all that you have done for me, upholding mine authority, and permitting my son to tease you, I thank you.”

“Thank me not for pleasing myself,” Simon answered. He rose, and Maurice with him, and as Gountray would have left the room, he spoke again, more lightly. “Thou wilt think me careless, Maurice. Before I went to supper I walked out to cast a look on my lands, and Cedric followed me. He ran a sharp thorn into his hands, and it bled grievously before he showed me what had happened.” Then as Maurice looked rather anxious. “I pulled the thorn out and bound his hand. I think it will be well tomorrow.”

“Sir, it is kind indeed of you to take such pains with Cedric! I will go look to him.” His hand was on the latch of the door when Simon spoke again.

“I could not but hurt him, but he shed not one tear.”

===Chinese===
“大人，我——我谢您！您为我所做的一切——维护我的权柄，还容我儿子同您胡闹——我谢您。”

“不必谢我，我不过是遂自己的心意。”西蒙答道。他起身，莫里斯随之起身；冈特雷正要告退出屋，他又开了口，语气轻快了些。“你要说我粗心了，莫里斯。晚饭前我出去看了看我的田庄，塞德里克跟了去。一根尖刺扎进了他手里，等他让我瞧见时，血已经流了不少。”见莫里斯神色颇为焦急，他又道：“刺我已拔出，手也包扎了。想来明天便无碍了。”

“大人，您肯为塞德里克这样费心，实在仁厚！我这就去照看他。”他的手已搭上门闩，西蒙却又开了口。

“我免不得弄疼了他，可他一滴眼泪也没掉。”

---

===Original===
He rode to Malvallet a week later, and was royally entertained by his father. When he had gone again, Malvallet turned to his son Geoffrey who still remained at home.

“Geoffrey, I do love that boy,” he said abruptly.

“And I, sir.”

Malvallet spoke bitterly.

“I shall never be more to him than a friend.”

===Chinese===
一周后，他策马前往马尔瓦莱，父亲待他极尽隆重。待他离去之后，马尔瓦莱转向仍留在家中的儿子杰弗里。

“杰弗里，我真疼那孩子。”他突兀地说道。

“我也是，父亲大人。”

马尔瓦莱语含苦涩。

“我于他，终究不过是个朋友，再不能更多了。”

===Original===
Geoffrey said nothing to that, and there fell a silence. Then he looked across at his father, smiling.

“Thou wouldst have liked to see him when he took Owen’s son prisoner, sir. On my word, he was there, there and everywhere, vying with the Prince himself in spurring our men onward. Then he came upon Griffith in one part of the field, and engaged him to single combat. Methought they never would have done, for Griffith is no weakling, sir, and he tilted and hacked at Simon until my heart was in my mouth. But Simon is untiring, and at last Griffith’s arm sank, and he yielded himself prisoner. Simon haled him to the Prince, and demanded naught from him but his armour, a curious set, gilded over, and so delicately fashioned that when on it hath scarcely any weight at all. And when next we fought, he wore that armour so that he was a mark for all eyes. Seeing him so much to the fore, his men did press onward to join him, inspiring the others. That victory the Prince vows is due to Simon’s valour alone. Henry hath a great liking for him, sir, and would have kept him at his side had Simon willed it so.”

Malvallet nodded slowly.

===Chinese===
杰弗里没有接话，一晌无语。随后他望着对面的父亲，微微一笑。

“父亲大人，他生擒欧文之子的那一幕，您见了必定称心。说句实话，他东突西杀，无处不在，在催促我军奋勇向前一事上，连亲王本人也与他并驾齐驱。后来他在战场一处撞上格里菲斯，挑他单独较量。我当他们永远打不完了，因为格里菲斯绝非弱者，父亲大人；他又是突刺又是猛砍，直杀得我心都提到了嗓子眼。可西蒙不知疲倦，格里菲斯的手臂终于垂了下去，束手就擒。西蒙把他拖到亲王跟前，分毫不取，只要了他那副铠甲——一副奇特的铠甲，通体鎏金，打造得极为精巧，穿在身上几乎毫无分量。下一次接战，他便披挂着这副铠甲，成了万众瞩目的靶心。众人见他如此抢前，他的部下便奋力向前追随他，又带动了其余兵士。亲王赌咒说，那一仗的胜利全凭西蒙一人之勇。亨利极喜欢他，父亲大人；西蒙若肯，原可留在亲王身边。”

马尔瓦莱缓缓点头。

===Original===
“Ay. He will be great one day﻿—if he wills it so.”

“And if no woman comes into his life to divert his thoughts,” Geoffrey said.

“There is no woman as yet?”

Geoffrey laughed.

“Holy Virgin, sir, if thou couldst but see Simon with a maid! He pays no heed to them, nor seems to notice their presence! I tell him he will fall one day, and Alan tells him, too, but in truth, sir, I think he never will!”

===Chinese===
“是啊。终有一日他会成大器——只要他愿意。”

“还要没有女人闯进他的生命，分去他的心神。”杰弗里说。

“眼下还没有女人么？”

杰弗里朗声大笑。

“圣母在上，父亲大人，您若是见过西蒙同姑娘相对的模样就好了！他对她们毫不上心，仿佛根本没瞧见人在眼前！我对他说，他总有一天会栽跟头，艾伦也这样说他，可说真的，父亲大人，我看他永远不会！”

===Original===
“I wonder,” Malvallet said.

“Or if he doth, ’twill be before some timid, pale-faced wench who will make of herself a carpet for his disdainful feet!”

“I﻿—wonder,” Malvallet said again.

===Chinese===
“难说啊。”马尔瓦莱道。

“即便有朝一日他动了心，那也必是个怯生生、白面皮的丫头，甘愿铺平了自己，做他倨傲脚下的地毯！”

“难——说啊。”马尔瓦莱又说了一遍。

## Part
				II

## Part II / 第二部

## I / 第一章

===Original===
How He Came to Normandy

===Chinese===
他如何来到诺曼底

===Original===
He stood upon a hill by Alençon, looking out over France, and the wind blew his fair hair all about his face, and whipped his surcoat round his mailed form. He was past thirty now, and ten years had gone by since he became Lord of Beauvallet.

Behind him, sprawling on the soft grass, was his squire, a handsome youth with black curls and merry eyes. They were thoughtful now and admiring, for they rested on Simon, pondering him.

Simon stood motionless, half-turned away; he had not moved or spoken for some minutes, but was frowning over the fair land stretched at his feet. His squire watched the grim profile respectfully, glancing from the massive, projecting brow with the deep-set eyes shining from beneath it, to the strong jawbone, outlined clearly in the bronzed, lean cheek. One of Simon’s hands hung listless at his side, and presently clenched a little; the spurred feet were well apart and firmly planted. The squire reflected idly that the pose stood for all the strength and purpose that were Simon’s. He rolled over on to his side, supporting his head on one slim hand, still watching Simon.

===Chinese===
他站在阿朗松（Alençon）城外的一座山冈上，眺望着法兰西的原野。风把他金色的头发吹得拂了满脸，又把罩袍紧紧裹在他披甲的身躯上。他如今已年过三十；自他成为博瓦莱领主以来，十个年头已经过去。

他身后，柔软的草地上斜躺着他的扈从（squire）——一个黑鬈发、眼睛欢快的俊美少年。此刻那双眼睛里满是思量与钦慕，因为它们正落在西蒙（Simon）身上，细细打量着他。

西蒙一动不动地立着，身子半侧向一旁；他已有好几分钟没挪动，也没说话，只是蹙着眉，望那片铺展在脚下的锦绣河山。他的扈从满含敬意地端详着那严峻的侧影：目光从那隆阔前突的额角，到额檐下深陷而熠熠发亮的眼睛，再到青铜色瘦削面颊上轮廓分明的结实颚骨。西蒙一只手无意识地在身侧垂着，这会儿微微攥紧了；带马刺的双脚分立，稳稳扎在地上。扈从闲闲地想，这副身姿，正是西蒙全部的膂力与心志的写照。他侧过身翻了个向，用一只纤细的手撑住脑袋，依旧望着西蒙。

===Original===
This was not the first time that Simon had set foot on French soil. Twice before had he marched into this land; once under the King’s brother, Thomas, Duke of Clarence, and again under Henry himself, when they had fought at Agincourt. He was famous now for his generalship; his name was linked with that of Clarence, or of Umfraville; he was spoken of as the Fifth Henry’s friend, and the Iron Lord. Of some he was beloved, of others hated, but no man ignored him or thought him of little count. He had become great, and this by his own wit and strength. He had no equal save the King himself in generalship; no commander was so instantly obeyed, and no commander was so greatly respected by his men. He had power and wealth, a splendid body, fit for any hardship or endurance, a not unpleasing countenance, and a quick, cool brain. Yet something he seemed to lack, for with all his assets and attainments, he was cold as stone, almost as though some humanising part of him had been left out in his fashioning. There were those who said that a softer side of love and passion was not in him, but Henry the King, wiser than these, would point to some frolicking page in the Beauvallet green and russet when he heard this criticism.

“What! Do ye think Beauvallet hath no tenderness within him? Fool, what of the children?”

The critics were silenced then, for Simon’s love of children was well-known.

===Chinese===
西蒙踏上法兰西的土地，这已不是头一遭。他曾两度进军这个国度：一次在国王的兄弟克拉伦斯公爵托马斯（Thomas, Duke of Clarence）麾下，一次在亨利（Henry，今已加冕为王，即亨利五世）御驾亲率之下，阿金库尔（Agincourt）一役便是同他打的。如今他运筹帷幄的将才已名满天下；人们把他的名字与克拉伦斯、与乌姆弗拉维尔（Umfraville）相提并论；说起他，都道是亨利五世的朋友，‘铁大人’。有人爱戴他，有人憎恨他，却没有人敢漠视他，也没有人把他看作无足轻重。他已臻显赫，而这一切，全凭他自己的才智与膂力挣来。用兵之道，除国王本人无人与他比肩；没有哪位将令像他这样令出即行，也没有哪位将令像他这样深受部下敬重。他有权有财，体魄雄健，什么样的艰苦磨难都经得起，相貌也并不讨人厌，头脑更是敏捷冷静。然而他仿佛缺了点什么：纵有这许多天资与造诣，他冷得像石头，几乎就像造物主捏造他的时候，把他身上那点叫人成其为人的东西给漏掉了。有人说，柔情与热恋这一路物事，压根不在他天性之内；但国王亨利比这些人高明，每听得这种议论，便只朝博瓦莱家那身穿绿褐两色衣装、正在嬉闹的某个小侍童（page）一指。

“怎么！你们以为博瓦莱心里就没有温柔？蠢材，那些孩子呢？”

非议者顿时语塞，因为西蒙疼爱孩子，是人尽皆知的事。

===Original===
“It sleeps,” Henry said once to Simon’s half-brother. “It will awaken one day.”

Geoffrey turned his head.

“Of what speak you, sir?”

Henry’s eyes were upon the distant Simon.

“Of the passion that lies in Simon.”

===Chinese===
“它在沉睡，”亨利有一次对西蒙的异母兄弟说。“总有一天，它会醒过来。”

杰弗里（Geoffrey）回过头来。

“陛下在说什么，先生？”

亨利的目光落在远处西蒙的身上。

“说西蒙心里潜藏的那份激情。”

===Original===
“There is none, sire. Once I thought as you think, but I have known him for fifteen years, and never once have I seen him melt, or lose one jot of his coldness. Save with the children.”

“Ay, save with the children. By that sign, Geoffrey, I do know that there is that in him that will spring to life one day.”

“There is icy rage, sir,” Geoffrey answered, smiling. “What manner of woman will it be before whom Simon will fall? How many fair maids hath he passed by? And now he is past thirty. He is not like to love. It is too late.”

Henry smiled, laying his hand on Malvallet’s arm.

“Geoffrey, Geoffrey, sometimes thou art a fool! Alan is wiser.”

===Chinese===
“那可没有，陛下。从前我也同您一样想，可我认识他十五年了，从来不曾见他有片刻消融，也不曾见他那份冷淡减却分毫——除了对着孩子们的时候。”

“是啊，除了对着孩子们。凭这一点兆头，杰弗里，我就知道，他身上有一样东西，终有一天会活转过来。”

“他身上有的是冰封的怒火，先生。”杰弗里笑着答道。“能让西蒙拜倒的，会是怎样一个女人？多少佳丽从他眼前走过？如今他年过三十，多半是不会动情了。太迟啦。”

亨利微微一笑，把手搭上马尔瓦莱（Malvallet）的手臂。

“杰弗里，杰弗里，你有时候真是个蠢人！艾伦比你明白。”

===Original===
“Alan is very wise in all matters of the heart, sir,” Geoffrey retorted. He cast a laughing glance to where sat the young Montlice, chin in hand and soft eyes dreaming.

Henry followed his look, echoed his laugh.

“What a trio have I about me!” he said. “My Soldier, my Knight, and my Poet.”

===Chinese===
“但凡沾着儿女情长的事，艾伦都贤明得很呢，先生。”杰弗里回敬道。他笑着瞟了一眼那边的年轻蒙利斯（Montlice）——手托着下巴，一双柔眼正做着梦。

亨利顺着他的目光望去，跟着笑出声来。

“瞧我身边这一班人马！”他说。“我的武人，我的骑士，我的诗人。”

---

===Original===
And as such they were known, close friends all three, and each one unlike the other. Clarence once named them Iron, Flame, and Silver, and marvelled at their friendship, but the King’s name for them was more apt. Simon was all a soldier, dauntless and cool, born to rule and to lead; Geoffrey, the Knight, had a hot courage, a courtier’s tongue, and an impetuous spirit; Alan, the Poet, was a dreamer, unfit for wars, yet partaking in them much as some troubador of a hundred years before, born to love, perhaps not greatly, but often and sweetly. He followed where Simon led, but Geoffrey would sometimes leap ahead with characteristic blindness, only to be dragged back by Simon’s inflexible will. They had been together now, this ill-assorted trio, for many years. Geoffrey and Alan had watched Simon’s gradual conquest of his lands with amused yet admiring eyes; they saw him rise to fame without feeling a spark of jealousy stir within them; they looked on Simon as master, but they thought him a child in everything that had to do with the heart. Time and again had they watched him with some fair lady, breathlessly waiting to observe a change in him. Each time disappointment came, for although he had met the greatest and most lovely ladies of the time not one of them had ever stirred the sleeping passion within him. He was not, as some strong men, timid of the gentler sex; in a maiden’s presence his tongue did not stumble, nor did his tanned cheeks flush. It was simply that he had no room for women in his life, and no liking for them in his heart.

===Chinese===
他们就以这样的名号为人所知：三人交情亲密，性情却各不相同。克拉伦斯曾给他们起名叫‘铁’、‘焰’、‘银’，惊叹于他们的友谊；不过国王给他们起的名号更贴切。西蒙是纯粹的武人，无畏而冷静，天生就该统御、就该领兵；杰弗里——那位骑士——勇气炽烈，一条舌头是朝臣的舌头，一副性子是奔涌的性子；艾伦——那位诗人——是个梦想家，本不适合征战，却照样深深参战其中，活脱脱是一百年前的行吟诗人再世：天生为爱而生，或许爱得算不得深，却爱得殷勤，也爱得甜蜜。西蒙指向哪里，他便跟到哪里；杰弗里却常常带着他那一贯的莽撞一头抢到前头，末了还得被西蒙不可动摇的意志拖拽回来。这个不甚般配的三人组合，至今已相伴多年。杰弗里和艾伦饶有兴味而又满怀钦佩，眼看着西蒙一步步征服他自己的领地；眼看着他声名鹊起，心里却不起半星妒意；他们奉西蒙为主上，却也认定：但凡沾着心的事，他就是个孩子。多少回，他们屏息静气地盯着他陪伴某位贵妇人，等着瞧他在她面前生出一丝变化。每一回都以失望告终——虽则他见过当世最尊贵、最美丽的夫人小姐，却没有一个搅动过他心中那份沉睡的激情。他不像某些刚强的男儿那样畏惧女流；在少女面前，他的舌头不会打绊，晒黑的面颊也不会泛红。只可惜他的人生里没有留给女子的位置，他的心里也没有存下对她们的喜爱。

---

===Original===
Cedric, the squire, plucked a blade of grass and began to suck it meditatively. His eyes were upon Simon’s broad shoulders, and he was wondering if his would ever match them in breadth or straightness. He sighed a little, for he was a slim youth, not square-set as was his lord, and without the iron muscle that had been Simon’s long before he had attained Cedric’s age. His eyes travelled down Simon’s tapering flanks, to the arched, spurred feet, and then up again to that stern, rugged face. He had not been told why they had tramped out of Alençon this afternoon, and he knew better than to ask, privileged though he was. He had followed Simon to this hill, silent all the way, for Simon was deep in thought. Cedric guessed that he was puzzling over some weighty problem, by the frown on his brow, and the grimness about his mouth. They had been stationary upon this hill for a long time now, and Cedric rather wished that his lord would say or do something to relieve the tedium, instead of gazing far away at the distant horizon.

Then Simon spoke in his deep, grave voice, without turning his head.

“Canst find naught better to do than stare at thy lord, child?”

Accustomed as he was to Simon’s unexpected ways, Cedric was startled. He had thought that Simon had forgotten his presence, nor been aware of the fixed scrutiny behind him.

===Chinese===
扈从塞德里克（Cedric）摘下一根草茎，若有所思地衔在嘴里吮着。他的眼睛盯住西蒙宽阔的肩膀，暗自纳闷，自己那副肩膀将来能不能赶得上这份宽厚挺拔。他轻轻叹了口气：他是个清瘦的少年，不像主上那样敦实方正，也没有那一身铁打的筋肉——西蒙在他这个年纪之前很久，就已练就了这副筋骨。他的目光顺着西蒙渐渐收窄的腰身往下游走，掠过绷成弓形、带着马刺的双脚，又抬起来，回到那张严峻粗粝的脸上。今天下午他们为什么徒步走出阿朗松，没有人告诉过他；他也有自知之明，不去开口动问，尽管他在主上跟前素来是有体面的。他一路默不作声，跟着西蒙上了这座山冈，因为西蒙沉在思虑里。看他眉间的蹙痕、嘴角的冷峻，塞德里克猜他是在为什么沉重的大事苦思。他们在这山冈上伫立已经很久了，塞德里克挺盼着主上能说句什么、做点什么，替这份沉闷解解乏，别只是远远凝望着天边。

西蒙终于开口了，声音低沉而庄重，头也没有回。

“除了盯着你家主上瞧，就寻不出更好的事做了么，孩子？”

塞德里克纵然习惯了西蒙种种出其不意的做派，还是吃了一惊。他原以为西蒙早忘了他在场，压根不曾察觉背后这道盯得死紧的目光。

===Original===
“Nay, my lord, I think not.”

Simon smiled a little.

“I am so pleasing to thine eye?”

“Yes, sir,” Cedric answered simply.

Simon moved at last, and looked down at his sprawling squire. There was a note of feeling in his voice now.

===Chinese===
“没有，大人，我想没有。”

西蒙微微一笑。

“我竟这般养你的眼？”

“是的，先生。”塞德里克老老实实地答道。

西蒙总算动了动，低头望着四肢摊开的扈从。这一回，他的话音里带上了一缕情意。

===Original===
“Thou lazy pup!” he said, still smiling. “Take that grass out of thy mouth.”

Cedric ejected it, laughing up at Simon. He made no effort to rise, for well he knew that he was privileged in his lord’s eyes. Other pages had come and gone, but for none had Simon cherished the same affection that he felt for Cedric of Gountray, who, long years ago, had forced himself upon his notice.

“Lord, when do we move from Alençon?” he asked presently. “Are we to remain here for aye?”

“When the time comes ye will know,” Simon answered curtly.

Cedric was in no way abashed. He sat up, hugging his knees.

===Chinese===
“你这懒崽子！”他说，嘴边仍挂着笑。“把嘴里那根草拿掉。”

塞德里克把草吐掉，仰着脸冲西蒙直笑。他压根没打算起身，因为他心里透亮：在主上眼里，他是享有特权的。侍童来了又走，换了一拨又一拨，却没有一个得享西蒙对冈特雷（Gountray）的塞德里克的那份疼爱——多年以前，正是这个孩子硬闯进了他的眼帘。

“大人，咱们什么时候离开阿朗松？”过了一会儿，他问道。“难道就一直在这儿待下去不成？”

“时候到了，你自然会知道。”西蒙简慢地答道。

塞德里克半点也不臊。他坐起身来，抱住膝盖。

===Original===
“It is soon, I think,” he said shrewdly, and cast a glance upward at Simon’s impassive countenance. “I wonder, do we march with the King, or with the Duke?” He paused a moment, “Or alone?” he added softly.

Simon vouchsafed no reply, but jerked his head, as a sign that they were going. He set off with striding steps towards the town, Cedric trotting along beside him.

Within the gates they came upon Sir Alan, whereupon Cedric fell discreetly to the rear.

Alan slipped his arm in Simon’s, looking up at him with the subservient affection that not all the years had tempered. He was very little changed from the youth Simon had met without the castle of Montlice. His face had retained its girlish curves, his figure its slender grace. He was attired in silks and velvets, for he scorned a soldier’s garb save when it was necessary.

===Chinese===
“我想，快了。”他精明地说道，一面抬眼瞟了瞟西蒙那张纹丝不动的脸。“我猜，咱们是跟国王走，还是跟公爵走？”他略略一顿，轻轻补上一句：“还是自家单走？”

西蒙不肯赏一个回答，只把头一摆，示意该动身了。他迈开大步朝城里走去，塞德里克在一旁小跑着跟上。

进城门不久，他们遇上了艾伦爵士，塞德里克便识趣地落到后头去了。

艾伦把手臂挽进西蒙的臂弯，仰头望着他，那份依顺的亲昵，经年累月也不曾磨淡。比起当年西蒙在蒙利斯城堡外遇见的那个少年，他几乎没有变：脸上还留着少女般的柔和轮廓，身段还存着纤秀的优雅。他一身绫罗绸缎、天鹅绒的衣裳——除非迫不得已，他才不屑穿行伍的行头。

===Original===
“Simon,” he said in an undertone, “whence comes this talk of sending thee to Belrémy?”

“From idle men’s tongues belike,” answered Simon shortly.

“It is not true?”

“True enough, but prate not of it, Alan.”

“Thou art indeed to pit thy strength against the Lady Margaret of Belrémy?”

“Ay.”

===Chinese===
“西蒙，”他压着嗓子说，“派你去贝雷米（Belrémy）的风声，是打哪儿刮起来的？”

“八成是闲人的舌头。”西蒙短促地答道。

“不是真的？”

“是真的，可别去嚼舌头，艾伦。”

“你当真要凭一身膂力，去同贝雷米的玛格丽特小姐（Lady Margaret）较量？”

“正是。”

===Original===
“Where Umfraville hath failed thou art to conquer. Shalt thou take the town, Simon?”

“God willing.”

Alan chuckled softly, whistling “*Deo Gratias*” below his breath.

“I too shall come, of course,” he remarked dreamily. “I have a mind to see this Lady Margaret.”

Then Simon smiled.

===Chinese===
“乌姆弗拉维尔折戟的地方，你要去把它攻克。你拿得下那座城么，西蒙？”

“上帝愿意的话。”

艾伦低低地哧笑出声，压着嗓子吹起口哨，吹的正是那支《Deo Gratias》（“感谢上帝”）的调子。

“我自然也要去的。”他悠悠地说道。“我倒有心见识见识这位玛格丽特小姐。”

这一回，西蒙笑了。

===Original===
“There will be no lovemaking while thou art with me, Alan.”

“Will there not? Thou shouldst bear the Lady Margaret off thyself, lad. That indeed would be a conquest.”

“Um!” Simon grunted. “A spitfire to wife? I thank thee.”

They were nearing the King’s quarters, and passed several knights who waved a greeting, or asked a question of Simon.

===Chinese===
“你跟着我，就别想谈情说爱，艾伦。”

“是没有么？那你该亲自把玛格丽特小姐劫了走才是，老弟。那才算得上真正的征服呢。”

“嗯！”西蒙从鼻腔里哼出一声。“娶个泼辣货做妻子？多谢你的好意。”

他们快到国王的驻跸处了，一路经过几名骑士，有的挥手向他们致意，有的拉住西蒙问上一句。

===Original===
“I came in search of thee,” Alan said. “The King would speak with thee. Where hast been?”

“Over yonder, upon the hill.”

“Wherefore?”

“To think, and to breathe. The town chokes me. Dost thou come with me to the King?”

“Ay. Geoffrey is there, and swears he will go with thee to Belrémy. So we fare forth together once more.”

===Chinese===
“我是来寻你的，”艾伦说。“国王要同你说话。你上哪儿去了？”

“在那边，山冈上。”

“去做什么？”

“去想一想，去喘口气。这座城闷得我透不过气。你同我一道去见国王么？”

“去。杰弗里也在那儿，赌咒发誓说要跟你同去贝雷米。咱们仨又要一起出征了。”

===Original===
They entered the house and made their way up the staircase to the King’s apartments. Henry was there, with Geoffrey of Malvallet, and Gilbert of Umfraville. He looked up as Simon entered, and smiled.

“Hither, my Soldier. I did send for Gilbert.”

Umfraville came forward to grip Simon’s hand.

“Unlike me, thou’lt be like unto Caesar, Simon,” he said. “To Belrémy wilt thou go, and where I saw, thou wilt also conquer.”

“Thou wouldst have conquered but for the short space of time accorded thee,” Simon answered slowly.

===Chinese===
两人走进那座宅子，顺着楼梯上去，来到国王的居所。亨利正在那里，同马尔瓦莱的杰弗里，还有乌姆弗拉维尔的吉尔伯特（Gilbert of Umfraville）在一起。见西蒙进来，他抬起头，笑了。

“过来，我的武人。我方才遣人去请了吉尔伯特来。”

乌姆弗拉维尔走上前来，紧紧握住西蒙的手。

“你同我不一样：你会像恺撒一般，西蒙，”他说。“你去贝雷米；我到过的地方，你还要把它征服下来。”

“若不是拨给你的时日太短，你早就攻克了。”西蒙缓缓答道。

===Original===
Henry laughed, signing a sheet of parchment that was spread out before him.

“Hark to my Soldier! He blames me for Umfraville’s defeat.”

“Nay, sire!” Geoffrey interposed swiftly. “He is not so ungallant.”

“He is not gallant at all,” responded Henry. He pushed the parchment from him, and turned to look at Simon. “He is honest. Tell me, Simon, was it my fault that we took not Belrémy?”

“Ay, sir,” Simon replied imperturbably. “Ye did underrate the enemy. The task had been too easy before.”

===Chinese===
亨利朗声大笑，在摊在他面前的一卷羊皮纸上签了字。

“听听我的武人！乌姆弗拉维尔吃了败仗，他倒怪起我来了。”

“那倒不是，陛下！”杰弗里连忙插进话来。“他还不至于这般失了骑士的风范。”

“他压根就没有骑士风范，”亨利应道。他把羊皮纸推开，转身望着西蒙。“他有的只是诚实。告诉我，西蒙，咱们没能拿下贝雷米，是我的过错么？”

“是的，先生。”西蒙泰然自若地答道。“您把敌人看轻了。先前的那些差事，都太容易了。”

===Original===
“That is so,” nodded the King. “And now a woman baulks me. So I send her Simon the Coldheart.”

Geoffrey laughed out.

“Nay, nay, your generals feel no love for her, sir! She is a very Amazon. Is it not so, Gilbert?”

“Ay, so I believe. I have not seen her, nor any of my men.”

===Chinese===
“说得是。”国王颔首。“如今一个女人挡了我的道。所以我把‘冷心’西蒙派给她。”

杰弗里放声大笑。

“不，不，您麾下的将官对她可没有半分爱慕之心，先生！她是位地道的亚马逊（Amazon）女杰。是不是这样，吉尔伯特？”

“是啊，我想是。我没见过她，我的人也没有一个见过。”

===Original===
“They say she is garbed in armour and fights at the head of her men.”

“Whether that be true or not, her men are wildcats,” Gilbert said ruefully. “I met them but once when a body marched out upon us by night. Thou wilt do well to have a care, Simon. The town is so strongly fortified that ’twould take thee months to batter down the walls. Provisions they seem to have in plenty.”

“By the gleam in Simon’s eyes, I know it to be a task after his heart’s desire,” Henry said quizzically.

Simon gave his short laugh.

===Chinese===
“听说她顶盔贯甲，一马当先，领着她的人马冲杀。”

“这话是真是假且不论，她手下那帮人可是一群野猫。”吉尔伯特懊丧地说。“我只同他们照过一次面——在夜里，一队人马杀出来突袭我们。你好生当心，西蒙。那座城设防极坚固，单是把城墙轰开，就得耗上你几个月。粮草么，他们似乎囤得足足的。”

“瞧西蒙眼里的那道光，我就知道，这差事正合他的心意。”亨利打趣道。

西蒙短促地笑了一声。

===Original===
“Ay, sir. I will hand you the keys of Belrémy.”

“And I will write a canzonet to music on it,” Alan said. “Save that our King be not with us, it will be another Agincourt.”

“What, dost thou go with Simon, my Poet?” Henry asked. “Who then will charm mine ears with song?”

Alan blushed, shaking back his curls.

“So please your Majesty, I must e’en stay by Simon lest he lose his heart to Margaret the Amazon,” he bowed.

===Chinese===
“是的，先生。我会把贝雷米的城钥匙，亲手奉到您手上。”

“我还要为它谱一支曲儿呢。”艾伦说。“只除了咱们国王不在场，这一仗，便是又一场阿金库尔。”

“怎么，你要跟西蒙去么，我的诗人？”亨利问道。“那谁来用歌声悦我的耳？”

艾伦红了脸，把鬈发向后一甩。

“陛下恕罪，我只得守在西蒙身边，免得他把心丢给亚马逊女杰玛格丽特。”他一躬身。

===Original===
“Nay, the woman Simon will wed must be some puling lass with a timid tongue,” Henry retorted. “It is always thus.”

“’Twould be to mate an eagle with a dove, sir,” Gilbert said. “Simon will return to you an enslaved creature, having prostrated himself at the Amazon’s proud feet.”

“Well, she is a fair maid, so I hear,” Henry said. “Dost thou covet her, Simon?”

“Nay, sire. Her lands rather. Alan shall charm her into submission.”

===Chinese===
“不对，西蒙将来娶的，准是个哭哭啼啼、舌头羞怯的小妮子。”亨利回敬道。“向来都是如此。”

“那无异于叫雄鹰去配鸽子，先生。”吉尔伯特说。“西蒙回到您跟前时，准是个俯首帖耳的奴仆，早已拜倒在亚马逊女杰倨傲的脚下了。”

“不过，听说她倒是位美貌的小姐。”亨利说。“你觊觎她么，西蒙？”

“不，陛下。我觊觎的是她的领地。艾伦自会用甜言蜜语，哄得她纳款归降。”

===Original===
Henry laughed.

“Is that thy reason for taking my Poet from me?”

“What else, sir?” Simon answered, smiling. “A soldier he is not, nor a leader.”

“And what shall Geoffrey do?”

“Oh, there is work enough for Geoffrey,” said Simon tranquilly. “Whither do ye go, sir, when ye quit this town?”

===Chinese===
亨利大笑。

“你从我身边夺走我的诗人，就是为了这个？”

“不为此，又为什么，先生？”西蒙笑着答道。“他既不是武人，也做不了统帅。”

“那杰弗里去做什么？”

“哦，够杰弗里干的活儿有的是。”西蒙不动声色地说。“先生，您离开这座城之后，往哪里去？”

===Original===
Henry looked up at him gravely.

“Back to Falaise, my Soldier.”

Simon nodded.

“Ay, take that town, sir. It is worthy of the endeavour.”

“So if the King take Falaise, Simon shall take Belrémy,” Gilbert remarked. “Who shall say which task be the harder?”

===Chinese===
亨利抬起头，神色郑重地望着他。

“回法莱斯（Falaise）去，我的武人。”

西蒙点了点头。

“是，把那座城拿下来，先生。它值得费这番气力。”

“这么说来，国王拿下法莱斯，西蒙拿下贝雷米。”吉尔伯特道。“谁敢说哪一桩差事更难？”

===Original===
“I shall say.” Alan had seated himself by the window, apart from them, but now he turned his head, smiling sweetly upon Sir Gilbert of Umfraville.

“Speak, Sage,” Henry invited.

Alan crossed his legs.

“Belrémy is the harder task, sire, saving your presence.”

Geoffrey frowned.

===Chinese===
“我就敢说。”艾伦早已在窗边坐下，离他们远远的，这时却转过头来，冲乌姆弗拉维尔的吉尔伯特爵士甜甜一笑。

“讲来听听，贤士。”亨利欣然道。

艾伦跷起了二郎腿。

“更难的是贝雷米，陛下，恕我斗胆。”

杰弗里皱起眉头。

===Original===
“Wherefore, Alan?”

“Because the Sire de Mauny rules Falaise, and the Lady Margaret rules Belrémy.”

Geoffrey shook his head.

“What dost thou mean?”

===Chinese===
“为什么，艾伦？”

“因为守法莱斯的是莫尼老爷（the Sire de Mauny），守贝雷米的是玛格丽特小姐。”

杰弗里摇了摇头。

“你这是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Ay, propound me this riddle,” Henry said.

“ ’Tis very simple, sir. A man holds Falaise, and a woman, Belrémy. I would sooner fight a man than a woman.”

“This woman,” Gilbert corrected. “Alan is right. When a woman guards her own she is more dangerous than a man. Yet this lady knows not Simon.”

“And Simon knows not her,” Alan answered gently.

===Chinese===
“对，把这个谜说给我听听。”亨利说。

“再简单不过了，先生。守法莱斯的是个男人，守贝雷米的是个女人。同男人交手，总比同女人交手来得痛快。”

“是‘这个女人’。”吉尔伯特纠正道。“艾伦说得不错。女人守护自家产业的时候，比男人更危险。只是这位小姐还不认识西蒙。”

“西蒙也不认识她。”艾伦温声答道。

## II / 第二章

===Original===
How He Encamped Before Belrémy

Midway through October in that year of grace, 1417, Simon appeared before the town of Belrémy, with an army of fifteen hundred strong, Geoffrey of Malvallet leading the van, John Holland, Earl of Huntingdon, the left wing, and himself the right, Alan of Montlice with him, acting Master of Simon’s Horse. Two squires came in Simon’s train, Cedric of Gountray and Edmund Marnet. In the rear, with the ordnance and provisions, were the surgeons, the priest, and one John Tarbury, with his officers, as Master of Works.

Belrémy stood upon a slight incline, with its castle frowning down upon this force, and its grey walls sullen and forbidding.

===Chinese===
他如何在贝雷米（Belrémy）城前安营

时维主历 1417 年十月过半，西蒙兵临贝雷米城下，麾下大军一千五百之众：马尔瓦莱的杰弗里（Geoffrey of Malvallet）统领前锋，亨廷登伯爵约翰·霍兰（John Holland, Earl of Huntingdon）领左翼，西蒙亲领右翼，蒙利斯的艾伦（Alan of Montlice）随侍其侧，暂代西蒙的骁骑长（Master of the Horse）之职。随西蒙出征的尚有两名扈从：冈特雷的塞德里克（Cedric of Gountray）与埃德蒙·马内特（Edmund Marnet）。后队随军械粮秣而行的，有军医、随军神父，还有一位塔伯里的约翰（John of Tarbury），正携其属下任职工事总监（Master of Works）。

贝雷米坐落在一道缓坡之上，城堡蹙眉俯临这支军队，灰色城墙阴沉肃杀，拒人千里。

===Original===
“God’s my life! I like not this place!” murmured Alan, at Simon’s side.

Simon looked out from under his heavy brows, surveying the town, and Alan saw him smile. It was his tiger-snarl, and Montlice shivered a little, pitying Belrémy.

Simon turned, glancing along his halted army. He spoke over his shoulder to his squires.

“Fetch me John of Tarbury. Alan, bid Huntingdon march on to cover the western side. He knows.”

===Chinese===
“上帝作证！这地方我可不喜欢！”艾伦在西蒙身旁低声咕哝。

西蒙自浓眉之下凝望这座城，艾伦看见他笑了。那是他虎豹扑食般的狞笑，蒙利斯不禁微微打了个寒噤，暗替贝雷米叫苦。

西蒙转过身，目光扫过停驻待命的大军，侧过肩头对扈从们吩咐。

“去把塔伯里的约翰找来。艾伦，传令亨廷登进兵，掩住西面。他心里有数。”

===Original===
Within an hour the army was at work, under Simon’s direction. His men were set to build wooden huts, for Simon anticipated a prolonged siege, and winter was drawing on. Trenches were dug, and palisades erected for the protection of the army, and until these were finished, some ten days later, the camp was hard at work, both officers and men.

Simon sent a herald to the town, bidding them surrender, but the Lady Margaret returned a hot answer, that he should enter Belrémy over her dead body. Simon had no taste for heroics, and he received this answer indifferently.

And so he began his blockade, hearing occasionally some tidings from the King. He had learned the art of war under Henry, and he followed his precepts strictly, with the result that he lost no men, save by sickness, during all that weary siege. Nor did he once lose patience, although Geoffrey of Malvallet was nigh to weeping from boredom and inactivity.

===Chinese===
不到一个钟头，全军已奉西蒙号令动起工来。兵士们被派去搭建木屋，因为西蒙料定围城必然旷日持久，而冬日正一天天逼近。壕堑掘成，栅栏立起，以护卫大军；直到约莫十日后诸事完备，这座营盘始终上下忙碌，官佐士卒无一闲人。

西蒙遣一名传令官入城，谕令守军归降；玛格丽特小姐（Lady Margaret）却回了他一封火气腾腾的答书：除非踏着她的尸身，否则休想进得了贝雷米。西蒙素来不爱这一套英雄做派，听了这答复只淡然置之。

封锁就此开始，其间偶尔有国王那边的消息传来。他的兵法得自亨利亲授，处处谨遵师训，于是在这场熬人的围城全程之中，除病殁者外，他未折损一兵。他的耐性也不曾失守一时，倒是马尔瓦莱的杰弗里几乎要因百无聊赖、无所事事而哭出来。

===Original===
“Simon, Simon, art thou grown timorous?” he cried one night, standing by Simon without his tent.

“Nay,” Simon answered placidly. “Nor am I of a sudden foolhardy, Geoffrey.”

Geoffrey jerked his shoulder in impatience.

“Shall we sit down before this town forever?” he demanded. “To what avail your bombardment? The walls of Belrémy seem made of granite! They laugh at thy guns! I tell thee, Simon, this is waste of time!”

===Chinese===
“西蒙，西蒙，你可是胆怯了？”一夜，他立在西蒙帐外高声嚷道。

“不，”西蒙心平气和地答道，“我也不会突然变得有勇无谋，杰弗里。”

杰弗里不耐烦地一耸肩。

“我们要在这座城前永远围坐下去么？”他质问道，“你那通炮轰顶什么用？贝雷米的城墙简直像是花岗岩铸的！人家笑话你的大炮！我告诉你，西蒙，这是白白浪费时间！”

===Original===
Simon deigned no answer, nor looked at his half-brother.

“To what avail?” Geoffrey asked peevishly.

“So that I may weaken their fortifications, and, by hunger, weaken the soldiers.”

“And thy mine? Dost thou hope to enter the town under ground?”

“Maybe,” Simon answered.

“Were I in thy place I would storm it now in full force!” Geoffrey exclaimed.

===Chinese===
西蒙不屑置答，也不看他的异母兄弟一眼。

“顶什么用？”杰弗里怏怏地问。

“好削弱他们的城防，再借饥饿削弱他们的兵卒。”

“那你那条地道呢？你指望打地底下进城么？”

“也许。”西蒙答道。

“我若处在你的位子上，此刻就全军齐上，一举强攻了！”杰弗里嚷道。

===Original===
A little smile flitted across Simon’s face.

“That I know. Yet I am wiser than thou.”

Geoffrey laughed at that, and slipped his arm in Simon’s.

“Ay, I know. How much longer, Coldheart?”

“Thou shalt feast at Christmas within those walls,” Simon said, pointing. “I pledge thee my word.”

“A month hence!”

===Chinese===
西蒙脸上一抹浅笑掠过。

“这我知道。可我比你高明。”

杰弗里听了大笑，伸手挽住西蒙的手臂。

“是啊，我知道。还要多久，‘冷心’？”

“圣诞之时，你便能在那道城墙里赴宴了。”西蒙抬手一指，“我以言语为誓。”

“一个月之后！”

===Original===
“Nay, three weeks only. Fret not, Geoffrey. I do indeed know my strength.”

“Oh, I doubt it not!” Geoffrey heaved a sharp sigh. “My men grow troublesome, and murmur.”

“Check their murmuring, then. ’Twere to more avail than this whining in mine ear.”

Geoffrey flushed.

===Chinese===
“不，只消三个星期。莫急，杰弗里。我确乎知道自己有几斤几两。”

“哦，这个我半点不疑！”杰弗里重重地叹了口气。“我的人越来越不听话，都在嘀咕抱怨了。”

“那就去弹压他们的嘀咕。这总比在我耳边诉苦来得有用。”

杰弗里涨红了脸。

===Original===
“I have not thy power over them. I can lead them into fight, but I cannot hold them in leash.”

“Ay, but thou canst; none better.” Simon spoke slowly, not looking at Malvallet. “Quell thine own complaining, Geoffrey, and thou mayst then rebuke thy men.”

“Even as thou dost now rebuke me?”

“Even as I do now rebuke thee.”

===Chinese===
“我可没有你那份弹压他们的威势。领他们冲锋陷阵，我行；把他们像猎犬似的拴紧缰绳，我不行。”

“不，你行，而且无人比你更行。”西蒙缓缓说道，并不看马尔瓦莱。“先平息你自己的抱怨，杰弗里，再来申饬你的人。”

“就像你这会儿申饬我一般？”

“就像我这会儿申饬你一般。”

===Original===
There fell a silence upon them, until Geoffrey spoke again.

“Thou art right, Simon. I will mend my ways. Thy pardon.”

Simon turned, hand outstretched. Some of the severity went out of his face.

“What is this fiery blood that runs in thy veins?” he asked, and gripped Geoffrey’s fingers till the bones cracked. “Is it Malvallet blood?”

===Chinese===
两人之间落下了一阵沉默，直到杰弗里重新开口。

“你说得对，西蒙。我会改过。望你宽宥。”

西蒙转过身来，伸出手去。他脸上的严酷消退了几分。

“你血脉里奔流的这一股烈性，到底是什么血？”他一边问，一边攥住杰弗里的手指，直攥得骨节咔咔作响。“是马尔瓦莱的血么？”

===Original===
