“Nay, for it is not in thee. Give ye good night, Simon, I’ll school myself. Even as Alan,” he added, as the young Montlice came towards them. “What dost thou, pretty poet, out of thy bed at this hour?”

Alan came to Simon’s side, and laid a hand on his shoulder, leaning on it. His head was bare, and he was wrapped about in a great velvet coat, unlike the other two, who wore their armour. His dark eyes shone in the light of the fire at their feet, and he spoke softly.

“The night was so still,” he said. “Your voices woke me. What is toward?”

===Chinese===
“不是，因为这血并不在你身上。晚安，西蒙，我会管束好自己。跟艾伦一样。”他见年轻的蒙利斯朝两人走来，又补了一句。“俊俏诗人，这个时辰不在床上待着，跑来做什么？”

艾伦来到西蒙身边，一只手搭在他的肩头，倚靠着。他头上不戴盔，身上裹着一件宽大的天鹅绒外袍，与那两位一身甲胄的不同。他一双黑眸在脚边篝火的光里发亮，说话轻声细语。

“夜里太静了，”他说，“你们说话的声音把我吵醒。有什么事？”

===Original===
“Naught,” Simon answered. “Geoffrey pants to scale yonder walls.”

“Geoffrey must always fight,” Alan nodded. “I think I would we might remain here forever. There is peace in the air, and an ode in my head.”

“There is frost in the air,” Geoffrey shivered. “If Simon will not march in, I could find it in my heart to wish they would march out upon us, so we might have action at last. Simon hath pledged me his word we shall feast in Belrémy on Christmas Day, Alan.”

“He must always be boasting,” Alan replied. “I pray God we may enter together and whole.”

“That will not be if thou dost forget thine armour,” Simon said. His deep voice cut through the stillness like a knife. A sentry, hearing it, peered through the darkness to see where stood his lord.

===Chinese===
“没事。”西蒙答道，“杰弗里猴急着要攀上那边的城墙。”

“杰弗里是非打不可的。”艾伦点了点头。“我倒觉得，但愿我们能永远留在此地。空气里有安宁，我脑子里还有一首颂歌。”

“空气里还有寒霜。”杰弗里缩了缩脖子。“西蒙既然不肯进兵，我倒真恨不得他们出城来攻，好歹我们也总算有一仗可打。艾伦，西蒙已经立誓担保，圣诞日我们要在贝雷米城里开宴。”

“他总是改不了夸口。”艾伦答道。“我祈求上帝，让我们一同进城，安然无恙。”

“你要是忘了穿铠甲，那就未必了。”西蒙说。他低沉的嗓音像刀子一样划破寂静。一名哨兵听见，隔着黑暗张望，想看清自家大人立在何处。

===Original===
“I wonder, do they starve within?” Alan said, looking towards the black shadow that reared itself before them, and was Belrémy. “No help came to them.”

“When Umfraville drew off to Alençon they revictualled the town, belike,” Geoffrey said.

“The New Year should see their skins stretched across fleshless bones,” Alan insisted. “In the winter starvation and sickness come swiftly. Thou couldst hold the siege, Simon, and waste no lives.”

“I will not.”

Alan looked up at him under his lashes.

“What is thy motive, Simon? In an assault ye must lose men; in a blockade ’tis but the enemy who dies.”

===Chinese===
“不知城里的人是不是已经在挨饿了？”艾伦望着面前拔地而起的黑影——那便是贝雷米。“没有援兵去救他们。”

“乌姆弗拉维尔（Umfraville）拔营退往阿朗松（Alençon）的时候，他们多半补充过粮秣。”杰弗里说。

“到得新年，他们就要皮包着一把无肉的骨头了。”艾伦执意说道。“冬日里饥饿和疾病来势最急。你可以围而不打，西蒙，一条性命也不必枉费。”

“我不。”

艾伦垂着眼帘，抬眼望他。

“你图的又是什么，西蒙？强攻必然折损兵员；围困死的便只是敌人。”

===Original===
Simon gripped his arm above the wrist, and held it so, as in a vice.

“Fool! Were I to hold this town till starvation came, I should enter it over children’s bodies. I war not with babes.”

Alan was silent, abashed. From Simon’s other side spoke Geoffrey.

“It is for this, then, that thou’lt risk an assault, Simon?”

“Ay, but I risk naught. I strike not until the proper time. Go thou to bed, Geoffrey; it is past midnight.”

Geoffrey stretched himself.

===Chinese===
西蒙攥住他手腕上方的手臂，紧紧钳住，如同一把铁钳。

“蠢货！我若围困此城直到粮尽，进城时踏的便是孩童的尸身。我不与娃娃们开战。”

艾伦哑口无言，满面羞惭。西蒙另一侧，杰弗里开了口。

“那么，西蒙，你是为了这个，才肯冒险强攻？”

“是啊，可我并无风险。时机未到，我决不出手。睡觉去，杰弗里，已过午夜了。”

杰弗里伸了个懒腰。

===Original===
“I am weary,” he sighed. “Thy great mine reaches almost to the walls now.”

“It must reach farther,” Simon said grimly, and laughed to himself.

Alan and Geoffrey strolled away together.

“What doth he propose?” Alan wondered. “Some plan he hath, I’ll swear.”

“Ay, but he says naught. Mayhap we are to enter Belrémy through this mine he digs so hard.”

===Chinese===
“我乏了。”他叹道。“你那条大地道，如今已几乎挖到城墙根了。”

“还须挖得更远。”西蒙冷冷说道，暗自笑了。

艾伦和杰弗里一道踱开了。

“他打的什么主意？”艾伦纳罕。“他必有什么计策，我敢起誓。”

“是啊，可他只字不露。没准儿我们是要从他拼命挖掘的那条地道里进贝雷米呢。”

===Original===
“What! And be caught like rats in a trap? That is not Simon’s way.”

“Who knows? When the time comes he will tell us his will. If I read him aright he is as yet undecided. One thing I know.”

Alan yawned.

“And I. That I must sleep or die. What is thy knowledge?”

“That we enter Belrémy by Christmastide. What Simon says, he means.”

===Chinese===
“什么！像陷阱里的老鼠一样被堵个正着？那可不是西蒙的路数。”

“谁知道呢？时候一到，他自会晓谕他的号令。我若没有看错人，他此刻还未拿定主意。只有一件事我确知。”

艾伦打了个哈欠。

“我也知道一件：我若不睡就得死。你确知的是什么？”

“我们知道圣诞前后必进贝雷米。西蒙说出口的话，就是他的本意。”

===Original===
“He speaks not until he is sure,” Alan said. “If he told me he would march into Hell by Christmas and enslave the Devil, I would follow him.”

Geoffrey crossed himself.

“So would we all. Belrémy will be hell enough, God wot!”

“And the Lady Margaret, the Devil,” Alan chuckled.

===Chinese===
“没有十足把握，他绝不启齿。”艾伦说。“他若对我说，圣诞之前他要把大军开进地狱、叫魔鬼俯首为奴，我也会追随他进去。”

杰弗里画了个十字。

“我们谁都会跟着去。贝雷米已够地狱的了，上帝知道！”

“外加那位玛格丽特小姐当魔鬼。”艾伦咯咯轻笑。

## III / 第三章

===Original===
How He Took Belrémy

===Chinese===
他如何攻克贝雷米（Belrémy）

===Original===
He struck a week before the promised date, and the manner of his striking was typical of his policy throughout his career. His mine ran from the camp beneath the town-walls to a corner of waste ground within the town. He had made his calculations exactly, a rough plan of Belrémy as his guide. Two hours’ work would make an outlet from this subterranean passage.

Simon called a council of his captains on the day before his attack, and laid his last commands upon them. Holland was there, a youngster, unskilled in wars, but brave as a lion, and eager; Geoffrey, dark and tall, peerless in attack, and Alan, dreamy and nonchalant, yet ready to obey any order, blessed with the Montlice dash and verve whenever necessity called. They gathered together in Simon’s tent, unwontedly grave. Huntingdon was clad in leather, his armour laid by, and sat upon a rude bench, leaning forward the better to keep his eyes on Simon. Geoffrey stood before the table, fully equipped, but Alan had drawn a stool near to the entrance of the tent, and was dressed in soft cloth and silks. He rested his head in his hand, and his eyes were upon Simon, wide-open, and shining with a childlike innocence. Simon himself sat at the table, plans before him, in such a way that he might look easily from one to the other of his captains. His hands were loosely clasped upon the rough wood; he frowned, but his voice was passionless and even.

===Chinese===
他比约定之期提前了一周动手，而这一击的手法，正是他一生行事的典型路数。他挖的地道从营地起，穿过城墙之下，直通城内一处荒地的角落。他算得分毫不差，手边便是贝雷米的略图为凭。只消两个钟头的功夫，便能从这条地下通道掘出一个出口。

发动的前一日，西蒙召集众将议事，颁下最后的号令。霍兰（Holland）在场——一个年轻后生，于战阵尚属生手，却勇猛如狮，跃跃欲试；杰弗里黝黑颀长，冲锋陷阵举世无匹；艾伦则神思恍惚、漫不经心，却随时乐于奉命，一旦情势所需，蒙利斯家那股闯劲与锐气便应声而至。众人聚在西蒙帐中，神色罕见地凝重。亨廷登（Huntingdon）一身皮革衣装，铠甲早已卸下，坐在一条粗陋的长凳上，身子前倾，好叫目光一刻不离西蒙。杰弗里全副披挂，立于桌前；艾伦却拖了张凳子挨近帐门，一身软缎细布。他手支着头，双眼望定西蒙，睁得大大的，闪着孩童般的天真。西蒙本人坐在桌后，面前摊着图样，坐的位置恰好能从容顾盼每一位部将。他双手松松交叠，搁在粗木桌面上；眉头紧锁，话音却平和沉静，不起一丝波澜。

===Original===
“You, Huntingdon, at the sounding of the horn at seven in the morning, shall fall upon the western gate with all your force, using your three towers of archers, and your breaching-tower. The wall hath crumbled ’neath your cannon. Ye should breach it easily now, and ye must set about the task with much to-do and noise, so that the garrison may think I seek to enter there in full force. Thus ye draw their fire. It will be easy enough, for it is at the western gate that they are most vigilant. There they expect assault. Twelve men will creep along my mine at five o’clock, to break away the earthcrust within the town. When the signal is heard and the townsfolk are thrown into a turmoil by Huntingdon’s sudden attack, those twelve will run swiftly to the southern gates which I now front, and open them. You, Malvallet, with Montlice, shall charge then, and enter. There will be fighting enough to satisfy ye all, but the greater part of the garrison will have flown to defeat Holland. Malvallet, your task then is to ride westward through the town to Holland’s assistance. Montlice will press forward to the centre, where stands the castle. I shall be with you by then.” He paused, and shot a keen glance round. “Ye do understand?”

===Chinese===
“亨廷登，明晨七点钟角声一响，你便率全部人马猛扑西门，用上你的三座弓箭塔和攻城塔。你们的火炮已把那段城墙轰得残破，如今破城易如反掌，但你们动手务必大张旗鼓、闹出偌大声势，好让守军以为我要从那里倾力入城。如此便引开他们的火力。这事不难，因为他们戒备最严的正是西门，料定我们会从那里强攻。五点钟，十二个人沿我的地道潜行，凿穿城内的地表。号令一响、亨廷登的突袭把城民搅得天翻地覆之时，那十二个人便疾奔我此刻正面进逼的南门，将城门敞开。马尔瓦莱，那时你便与蒙利斯一同冲杀入城。够你们厮杀的仗有得是，不过守军大半会奔去西门抵挡霍兰。马尔瓦莱，你的任务便是纵马穿城向西，驰援霍兰。蒙利斯则直取城中——城堡就在那里。到那时我已与你们会合。”他顿了顿，锐利的目光四下一扫。“都听明白了么？”

===Original===
“Ay,” Huntingdon nodded.

“Well enough,” Alan sighed.

“But one thing,” Malvallet said.

Simon’s eyes were upon him.

“And that?”

“I do understand mine own part, for ’tis child’s play. What part do you play, sir?”

===Chinese===
“是。”亨廷登点头。

“还行。”艾伦叹了口气。

“只有一件事。”马尔瓦莱道。

西蒙的目光落在他身上。

“哪一件？”

“我自己的差事自然是明白的，那不过是小孩子把戏。可你本人担的是哪一角，大人？”

===Original===
“I lead those who enter the town by the mine. I am the twelfth man,” Simon answered quietly.

On the word there was an outcry.

“You have assigned to yourself the most difficult task!” Huntingdon exclaimed.

“Nay, Simon, it is not fitting,” Alan said softly.

“At least ye will take me with you!” Geoffrey cried.

===Chinese===
“我亲领由地道入城的人。我自己便是那第十二个。”西蒙平静地答道。

此言一出，帐中顿时大哗。

“你把最难的一担派给了你自己！”亨廷登嚷道。

“不，西蒙，这不合情理。”艾伦柔声说。

“至少你该带我同去！”杰弗里喊道。

===Original===
“Nay.” The word fell heavily, enforcing silence.

“But, Simon!” Geoffrey threw out his hands impulsively. “What comes to us if ye fall?”

“Then shall ye be commander in my room. I fall not.”

Huntingdon smote his knee.

“Beauvallet, take my place, and let me take thine! Indeed, indeed—”

===Chinese===
“不行。”这一个字沉沉砸下，逼得满帐噤声。

“可是，西蒙！”杰弗里忘情地摊开双手。“你若有个闪失，我们怎么办？”

“那便由你接掌指挥。我倒不下去。”

亨廷登一拳砸在自己膝上。

“博瓦莱，把你的差事换给我，让我替了你的！真的，真的——”

===Original===
“Silence, Holland. What I have said I have said.”

Alan rose, stretching himself like a cat. His eyes seemed more childlike than before, his pose more indolent.

“Simon, for the love that lies betwixt us two assign thy task to me.”

Simon came to his feet, and laid his hands on Alan’s shoulders.

“Thou lovesick child! Then were we indeed lost. Be content to do my bidding.”

Alan clasped his hands on Simon’s arm.

===Chinese===
“肃静，霍兰。话既出口，绝无更改。”

艾伦站起身来，像猫一样伸了个懒腰。那双眼睛显得比方才更孩气，姿态也愈发慵懒。

“西蒙，看在你我二人这份情分上，把你的差事派给我吧。”

西蒙霍然起身，双手按住艾伦的肩头。

“你这害了相思的痴儿！那我们才真是完了。乖乖听我号令便是。”

艾伦双手攥住西蒙的手臂。

===Original===
“Simon, I beg of thee!”

“And I.” Malvallet clanked forward, and smote Simon upon the shoulder. “Lad, there is too much danger in thy task. We need thee for other things, and if thou art slain we fall to pieces.”

Simon shook his head indomitably.

“Thou wilt meet me within the gates of Belrémy, Geoffrey. My hand on it.”

Malvallet wrung his hand.

===Chinese===
“西蒙，我求你！”

“还有我。”马尔瓦莱甲叶铿然地上前，一掌拍在西蒙肩上。“小子，你这差事太凶险。旁的事还指着你呢，你若有个三长两短，我们便散了架了。”

西蒙倔强地摇了摇头。

“你会在贝雷米城门里头与我相见，杰弗里。击掌为誓。”

马尔瓦莱紧紧一握他的手。

===Original===
“Simon, if so be they slay thee before thou hast flung open the gates, Belrémy shall have no quarter. That I swear.”

A gleam came into the curious eyes.

“Beauvallet dies not with his task unaccomplished, Geoffrey. Now listen to me, and cease thy plainings. Lie safe and still behind yonder palisade until the gates swing back, and the bridge is down. Then charge swiftly over. Let no movement be seen in my camp that thou canst avoid. Thyself lead the van, and let Alan follow. And come quickly, Geoffrey, for it may be that I shall need thy help.”

“By God!” Malvallet swore, “if I come not at once, may I be damned eternally!”

Simon nodded briefly, and turned to address them all.

===Chinese===
“西蒙，倘或他们赶在你打开城门之前害了你，贝雷米城中便绝不留情。我发誓。”

那双奇特的眼睛里闪过一丝光亮。

“博瓦莱绝不会在大功未成之时死去，杰弗里。现在听我说，收起你的愁容。安安稳稳伏在那道营栅之后，直到城门回旋敞开、吊桥放落。那时便疾速掩杀过来。我的营中但凡能免的动静，一丝也不许让敌人望见。你亲自领前队，让艾伦随继。还有，来要快，杰弗里，兴许我正用得着你的援手。”

“凭上帝起誓！”马尔瓦莱咒道，“我若不即刻赶到，叫我永世沉沦！”

西蒙略一点头，转身面向众人。

===Original===
“And further, let this my command be given out: If any man strike down a woman or child in the fight, or offer injury where none is courted, his life will I take, and that right speedily. I will have no burning or pillaging, but order and chivalry. Ye do understand?”

“Ay.”

“Then that is all. Fare ye well, Huntingdon. I shall not fail you.”

The young Earl gripped his hand for a moment, smiling.

“We meet within Belrémy, Beauvallet. God be with you and keep you in His care.”

===Chinese===
“还有，传下我这道军令：战中若有人击杀妇孺，或对并无相抗之人横施暴虐，我便取他性命，而且就地处决。不许焚掠，只要秩序与骑士之道。都明白了么？”

“明白。”

“那么就说完了。珍重，亨廷登。我断不会误你的事。”

年轻的伯爵握了握他的手，面带微笑。

“贝雷米城中再会，博瓦莱。愿上帝与你同在，庇你平安。”

===Original===
“And you.” Simon watched him swing out of the tent, and turned to his two friends. There was a little warmth in his voice now, and his eyes rested kindly upon them.

“If this be my last fight, my lands go to the King, by this my Will.” He picked up a sealed parchment. “My wealth I have divided equally between you, saving only that which I have left to my Marshal, Maurice of Gountray, and mine other men. I leave this packet with Bernard of Talmayne. One of you will care for Cedric and Edmund for my sake?”

“I will,” Alan answered and turned away, lifting one flap of the tent and gazing out.

But Geoffrey put his hand on Simon’s shoulder.

“Simon, ye have never spoken thus before. Not in all our fights. What ill-omen dost thou feel, my brother?”

===Chinese===
“也愿你如此。”西蒙望着他大步出帐，转面看向自己的两位挚友。此刻他的话音里有了一丝暖意，目光温和地停在他们身上。

“倘若此战是我的最后一战，凭这份遗嘱，我的领地归入王室。”他拾起一卷火漆封缄的羊皮纸。“我的财产在你们二人之间均分，唯有留给纠仪长冈特雷的莫里斯和我其余部众的那些除外。这包文书我交托塔尔曼的伯纳德保管。你们二人，可有谁看在我的份上照拂塞德里克和埃德蒙？”

“我来。”艾伦答着转过身去，掀起一角帐帘，向外凝望。

杰弗里却把手搭上西蒙的肩头。

“西蒙，你从前从没这样说过话。打过的仗再多也没有。你莫不是觉出了什么凶兆，我的兄弟？”

===Original===
“None.” Simon smiled into the anxious eyes. “Yet this will be a stern fight, and I would leave all in order.”

“If thou shouldst be slain,” Geoffrey began, and broke off. “Well, thou dost know.”

“Ay.”

“If thou shouldst be slain,” Alan said slowly, “then shall the vixen Margaret die.”

“Nay. That is folly. I die not. But if any of us be missing tomorrow, when all is done, those that are left will have lost the most faithful and the dearest friend. Go now, Geoffrey, and sleep whilst thou may.”

===Chinese===
“没有。”西蒙望着那双焦灼的眼睛，微微一笑。“只是此战必是一场恶战，我要把一切安排得妥妥帖帖。”

“你若阵亡，”杰弗里开了口，又蓦地住嘴。“罢了，你心里明白。”

“嗯。”

“你若阵亡，”艾伦缓缓地说，“那泼辣货玛格丽特（Margaret）便得死。”

“不。这是傻话。我死不了。但明日事毕之后，我们中间少了一人，剩下的便失去了最忠贞、最亲爱的人。去歇着吧，杰弗里，能睡一刻是一刻。”

===Original===
Geoffrey lingered still.

“And thou?”

“I have to see my captain, Walter of Santoy, and I must attend to some other matters. Remember, Geoffrey, if I fall tomorrow, thou art in command. Subdue Belrémy and invest it under Huntingdon. Then repair at once to the King. I can tell thee no more.”

“If thou dost fall before thou canst open the gates—”

Simon smiled grimly.

“That may not be. Fare thee well, my brother.” He watched Geoffrey walk to the entrance. “Tell thy men to follow the Gilded Armour. I shall wear it.”

===Chinese===
杰弗里还是徘徊不去。

“那你呢？”

“我还得见一见我的统领桑托伊的沃尔特，还有些别的事务要料理。记住，杰弗里，明日我若倒下，便由你接掌全军。平定贝雷米之后，在亨廷登麾下留军围戍此城，随即即刻赶赴王驾。我能说的只有这些。”

“你若来不及打开城门便倒下了——”

西蒙冷峻地笑了笑。

“那不会发生。珍重，我的兄弟。”他望着杰弗里走向帐口。“吩咐你的人跟着‘金甲’走。我会穿上它。”

===Original===
Geoffrey nodded. He paused by Alan and spoke to him.

“Thou wilt be ready, Alan?”

“Ay. I will come to thee when Simon goes, to hear thine orders.”

Geoffrey nodded again and went out. Simon’s secretary entered from the inner tent, and Alan waited until Simon had finished with him. Bernard went softly out to summon Walter of Santoy.

“It grows late,” Alan remarked. “Six of the clock. Thou wilt rest, Simon?”

===Chinese===
杰弗里点点头。他在艾伦身旁停了停，同他说话。

“你会备好的，艾伦？”

“是。西蒙一走，我便到你那儿去，听你的号令。”

杰弗里又点点头，出去了。西蒙的秘书从内帐进来，艾伦等着西蒙同他谈完。伯纳德悄没声儿地出去，请桑托伊的沃尔特。

“天色不早了，”艾伦道。“已交六点。你要歇息么，西蒙？”

===Original===
“Presently.”

“Who goes with thee into the town?”

“Mine own people. Eleven men.”

“Well, they would die for thee,” Alan said, as though he found therein some grain of comfort. “Cedric also?”

“Nay, he is too young. Take the boy with thee, Alan, for he will not be left behind. He is enraged already that I will not take him with me. Have a care to him.”

“I will. I’ll see thee again, lad, when thou art ready.” Alan smiled over his shoulder, and sauntered out to his own quarters.

===Chinese===
“等会儿便歇。”

“谁随你进城？”

“我自家的人。十一个。”

“嗯，他们都肯为你去死，”艾伦说，仿佛从中觅得了一星安慰。“塞德里克也去么？”

“不，他太小了。带那孩子跟你走，艾伦，他是不肯留下的。不许他随我进城，他已经气得不轻。替我看顾着他。”

“好。等你备妥了，我再来看你，小子。”艾伦回头一笑，踱着步子回自己的营帐去了。

---

===Original===
The night was very still and calm, the silence broken only within the camp where men moved stealthily about in preparation. The palisade had been undermined so that it would fall as soon as the supports were withdrawn. Away to the left Huntingdon was moving, with less stealth and more noise.

Simon stood at the entrance to his mine, tall and square, girt in his gilded armour, which glinted in the light of the fires. His great sword hung at his side, but his lance and shield he had discarded to be brought to him in Malvallet’s charge. He was wrapped about in a great cloak, as was each of his men, and he carried his green-plumed helm beneath his arm. Alan stood by his side, while he called the names of each of his followers. Every man answered promptly but softly. Dimly they were outlined against the black sky. Simon cast a quick glance over them, and turned to bid Alan farewell. He wasted no time, but held Alan’s hand a moment in his mailed clasp.

===Chinese===
夜里静谧无风，四下岑寂，唯有营中有人影悄悄往来，做着临战的准备。营栅底下已掏空了根基，只待抽去支撑，便会应声而倒。左翼远处，亨廷登的人马已在调动，不那么隐蔽，反倒弄出不少声响。

西蒙立在地道入口，身形颀长敦实，鎏金铠甲在营火光里熠熠生辉。巨剑悬在腰间，长枪和盾牌早已交下，待马尔瓦莱冲锋时再送到他手里。他和手下每个人都裹着一件大氅，腋下夹着缀绿羽的头盔。他逐一唱名点卯，艾伦侍立一旁。每个人应声既快且轻。黑天幕前，他们只剩隐隐的轮廓。西蒙飞快地扫视一遍，转身与艾伦作别。他不耽搁，戴着铁手套的手握住艾伦的手，握了片刻。

===Original===
“God be with thee, Alan. Follow the Gilded Armour, remember, and have a care to thyself. Give me the torch.”

It was handed to him and he bent, entering the mine, sword in hand. One by one his men crept in behind him, and presently were hidden from Alan’s sight in the gloomy tunnel. Even the glow of the torchlight faded; it was as though the earth had swallowed one and all.

Treading softly, and bent almost double, the line of men went steadily along the dank, earth-smelling passage, following the torch, and trusting implicitly to their leader. And so at length they came to the end of the mine, where they could stand upright. For a moment they stood listening, and then, quietly, Simon gave the order to begin to break upwards. Concealing cloaks were laid aside, and arms bared. Each man was furnished with either a pick or a spade, and with these they set to work, digging upwards as steeply as was possible. Simon himself flung down his cloak and helm, and hampered as he was by his armour, fell to hacking away the earth, his torch stuck in a niche in the earthen wall. There was no word spoken for a long time, but when Simon turned to pick up a spade his eyes fell on the eleventh man, shovelling the earth away with a will. A curly black head met his eye, and a young, strained face down which the sweat rolled in great beads. The boy raised his head at that moment and saw Simon’s stern glance upon him. He paused in his work to send his lord a look of piteous apology, not unmixed with triumph.

===Chinese===
“上帝与你同在，艾伦。记住，跟着‘金甲’走，好生当心自己。把火把给我。”

火把递到他手中，他俯身执剑，钻入地道。他的部下一个个跟在他身后鱼贯而入，不多时便隐没在幽暗的坑道里，艾伦再也望不见。连火把的光晕也淡去了，仿佛大地把这一行人生生吞了进去。

一行人放轻脚步，几乎弯成对折，沿着潮湿土腥的坑道稳稳前行，追随火把，对领队全然信服。末了终于到了地道尽头，那里已能直起身来。他们立定听了片刻，随后西蒙低声下令，开始向上开凿。遮掩用的大氅卸在一旁，露出臂膀。每人领了一柄镐或一把锹，便即动工，朝上掘得尽可能陡直。西蒙自己也掷下大氅和头盔，虽被铠甲累着，仍挥臂刨土，火把插在土壁的一处凹龛里。好半晌无人出声，待西蒙转身去拾锹，目光却落在第十一个人身上——那人正起劲地铲土。一头黑色鬈发撞进眼里，一张年轻绷紧的脸，大颗大颗的汗珠直往下滚。恰在此时，那孩子抬起头来，正撞见西蒙严厉的目光。他停了手，朝主上递去一个又是告饶又是得意的眼色。

===Original===
“You and I shall have a reckoning to settle for this, Cedric,” Simon said softly.

Cedric nodded, flushing.

“Ay, my lord. That I know. I could not let ye come without me. If—if aught befall us—you—you will have—forgiven?”

Simon’s hard mouth twitched.

“It would seem so. Go to now.”

===Chinese===
“这笔账，你我都得好好算一算，塞德里克。”西蒙轻声道。

塞德里克涨红了脸，点头。

“是，大人。我晓得。我不能放您一个人去。倘若——倘若我们遭了不测——您——您会——饶恕的，是么？”

西蒙那张紧硬的嘴微微一动。

“看来会饶。干活去。”

===Original===
Cedric threw him a grateful smile and returned to his digging with renewed vigour. Not another word was spoken; the work was done as silently as possible, and no man shirked his full share of this arduous task, although the tunnel was dank and airless, and the roof seemed to close down upon them. These picked men of Beauvallet would cheerfully have died sooner than fail their lord, or grumble at his strictness in a time of stress.

At last the foremost, one Malcolm Clayton, glanced back over his shoulder, and spoke in a hushed voice.

“Lord, my pick went through.”

Simon scrambled up the crumbling slope of loose earth.

“Then silence now, as you value your lives. Stand back, the others.”

===Chinese===
塞德里克投去一个感激的笑，愈发卖力地掘起土来。此后再没人说一句话；活计做得愈静愈好，坑道里潮闷无风，顶土仿佛正朝他们压拢下来，却没有一个人在这桩苦役上少出一份力。这些博瓦莱麾下精选的汉子，宁可欣然赴死，也不肯辜负主上，或在紧要关头嫌他严苛、口出怨言。

末了，打头的那个——名叫马尔科姆·克莱顿（Malcolm Clayton）——回头望了一眼，压着嗓子说话。

“大人，我的镐凿穿了。”

西蒙手脚并用，爬上那道松土的斜坡。

“那么此刻全都噤声，性命要紧。其余人退后。”

===Original===
He was observed instantly; the panting, sweating men rested on their tools, watching Simon and Malcolm break through the thin crust. It was slowly done, and carefully, but at length a wave of frosty air came down to them, and they drank it in gladly. Still Simon worked, making a hole just large enough to admit a man. Then he set down his pick, and raising himself on Malcolm’s shoulders, peered cautiously above the opening. Down he came again, springing lightly, and nodded.

“Bank the earth to form a step. You, John, and Peter. The dawn is upon us.”

Again they set to work, and soon had fulfilled his behest. A pale grey light filtered down into the tunnel, but overhead the sky seemed still dark and frowning.

Simon gave the order to stack the tools. Wine had been brought in small leathern bottles; they drank deeply of it before they donned their helms and cloaks. Cedric picked up the golden helm and shook its waving plumes free of the dirt. He buckled it on to Simon’s neck-plate, and clasped the long green surcoat upon his shoulders. Then Simon wrapped the dark cloak over all and picked up his great sword. It gleamed wickedly in the torchlight, and the golden helm seemed to glow with an inward fire. Beneath its peak Simon’s eyes looked calmly forth and the green of his plumes seemed to steal into them, so that he appeared as some huge knight all gold and green. His men were nervous through anticipation, but his measured voice quieted them.

===Chinese===
众人的目光立时聚了过去；喘着气、淌着汗的汉子们拄着家伙歇下手，看西蒙和马尔科姆凿穿那层薄薄的地壳。动作又慢又仔细，末了一股寒冽的夜气从缺口泻下来，众人贪婪地畅饮。西蒙仍不停手，把洞口掏得恰容一人钻过。随后他放下镐，踩着马尔科姆的肩膀探身出洞，谨慎地察看上方，又轻巧地一跃落地，点了点头。

“把土堆起来垫成踏级。你，约翰，还有彼得。天快亮了。”

众人再度动手，不多时便如命办妥。一线灰白的天光渗进坑道，头顶的天空却依旧阴沉，像在蹙眉。

西蒙下令收拢工具。酒是装在小皮囊里带来的，众人先畅饮一番，才戴盔披氅。塞德里克捧起那顶金盔，抖去羽翎上的尘土，替西蒙扣系在颈甲上，再把那件长长的绿罩袍在他肩头扣好。然后西蒙把黑大氅整个裹在外头，拾起巨剑。剑身在火光里闪着森森的寒芒，金盔也似有内焰在烧。盔檐之下，西蒙的双眼平静外望，羽翎的绿意仿佛沁入眼底，那模样俨然一员浑身金绿交映的巍巍骑士。部下们因临战的企盼而神经紧绷，他不疾不徐的话音却把他们安抚了下来。

===Original===
“Extinguish the torch.”

It was done, and the golden figure faded to a black silhouette against the faint light. Each man stood very still, and breathed rather fast. Again the cool voice spoke.

“Silence now until I speak. The time should be soon. Follow me close, but keep your swords hidden and show no fight until ye see me draw. Cedric, stay by me throughout.”

A low murmur of assent came and then all was eerily silent. Yet through the chilling darkness and the tense period of waiting Simon’s magnetic personality seemed to spread over his men so that their jagged nerves were soothed. Not one amongst them but placed his whole trust in Simon, believing implicitly that he would lead them to victory.

Time crept by on leaden feet, and bit by bit the grey light grew stronger. Above, all was quiet as the grave, so that the very silence seemed to din in the waiting men’s ears.

===Chinese===
“灭掉火把。”

火把应声而灭，那尊金色的身影隐入微明，只剩一道黑影。每个人都立定不动，呼吸却颇为急促。那个冷静的声音再度响起。

“在我开口之前，全都噤声。时辰该快到了。紧紧跟上我，兵刃藏好，不见我拔剑，谁也不许露出战意。塞德里克，自始至终跟着我。”

一片低低的应诺过后，四下静得叫人心里发毛。然而在这砭骨的黑暗与紧绷的等候里，西蒙那股摄人的气度仿佛漫过他的部众，把人人竖起的神经一一抚平。他们无一例外地把性命整个托付给西蒙，笃信他必领着他们走向胜利。

时间拖着铅一样沉的步子爬行，灰光一分一分亮起来。头顶上万籁俱寂，寂如坟墓，那寂静反倒像在等候者的耳中嗡嗡作响。

===Original===
Presently one fidgeted unconsciously, and drew a deep, sobbing breath. Against the light they saw Simon raise his hand, and once more there was quiet.

Then, as from a long way off, a horn sounded, wailing across the land. Thrice came the call, and something like a gasp of relief broke from eleven tense throats. Away in the camp, Geoffrey of Malvallet had given the signal for attack. Still Simon moved not, but stood rocklike, waiting.

Faintly came the noise of a great shout. Holland had obeyed the signal. Eleven men fixed their eyes upon their lord, muscles taut, to move at his least command. He stood immobile, his head slightly tilted, listening.

Gradually the noise grew, though it came muffled into the mine. An explosion rent the air; Holland had trained his one cannon on to the western wall the better to attract attention.

Nearer at hand turmoil sounded, subdued at first, but increasing in volume. The town was awake, and plunged into sudden and desperate activity.

===Chinese===
不多时，有一人不自觉地动了动，抽了一口气，压抑得近乎呜咽。映着微光，他们望见西蒙抬起一只手，于是重归寂静。

随后，仿佛自极远处，号角呜咽着掠过大地。角声三叠，十一条绷紧的喉咙里不约而同迸出一声如释重负的吐息。远处营中，马尔瓦莱的杰弗里已发出进攻的信号。西蒙依旧纹丝不动，磐石般立着，等待。

隐隐传来山呼海啸般的呐喊。霍兰已依号令而动。十一人目光钉在主上身上，筋肉绷紧，只待他最小的手势便要发难。他立在原处一动不动，头微微偏着，谛听。

喧声渐渐涨大，透进地道时仍闷闷的。一声爆炸撕裂空气；霍兰把他仅有的那门火炮对准了西墙，好把动静闹得更大。

近处的骚动也响起来，起初低抑，继而愈闹愈烈。全城惊醒，骤然陷入孤注一掷的忙乱。

===Original===
At last Simon moved, and spoke one word.

“Follow.” He mounted the rude step and scrambled through the hole with surprising agility. Quickly his men followed, and found themselves on a patch of waste ground behind some rude houses, amidst rubbish and garbage. They closed up behind Simon and strode after him across the uneven ground.

“Remember, ye are soldiers of Belrémy,” he reminded them. “Spread a little, but follow me.”

On they went, and broke into a trot as they emerged upon a narrow street. It was thronged with hurrying men, and from the windows and doors of the houses women called, some hysterical, others calm. Soldiers were running towards the western ramparts, buckling on their swords or mailed gloves. Simon’s little band separated quickly and ran after him, to the south, pushing and jostling the excited townsfolk. From behind came the roar of Holland’s attack, but they tarried not to listen. On they sped, out into the main street and down it towards the gates, always keeping the green plumes in sight, and gradually drawing near to Simon again.

===Chinese===
西蒙终于动了，只吐出一个字。

“跟上。”他踏上那级粗陋的土阶，以惊人的敏捷钻出洞口。部下们迅速跟进，发觉自己已置身几间破屋后的一片荒地，满地垃圾秽物。他们紧贴在西蒙身后，踏着高低不平的地面紧随而去。

“记住，你们是贝雷米的守军，”他提醒道。“稍稍散开，但跟我来。”

他们一路疾走，踏上狭窄街衢时已转为小跑。街上挤满奔忙的人，住宅的门窗里传来妇人的呼喊，有的歇斯底里，有的倒还镇定。兵士们一边扣紧佩剑或铁手套，一边向西面城垣奔跑。西蒙这支小队迅速散开，推搡着激愤的城民，追随他向南而去。身后传来霍兰攻势的喧嚣，他们却无暇去听。他们放步飞奔，冲上大街，沿街直趋城门，眼里始终不离那撮绿羽翎，渐渐又靠近了西蒙。

===Original===
Through the rapidly filling street the gates loomed large ahead, and from them came part of the garrison, mounted, and galloping to save the western walls, heedless of the scattered humanity flying from before the plunging hoofs.

They were upon the gates now, and Simon’s voice rang out, clarion-like above the din.

“To me, and do what I do!”

Full upon the startled sentries he rushed, and cried:

“The Seneschal! The Seneschal!”

They fell back instantly, thinking he came from the Marshal, and he swept on, his men at his heels, to the gate-tower. There again they were accosted, but this time the sentry but asked for news.

===Chinese===
在愈来愈拥挤的街上，城门已在前方高高耸现，一部分守军正从那里纵马而出，疾驰去救西墙，任凭四散奔逃的人影在扬起的马蹄前东躲西闪，全不顾惜。

他们已到城门前，西蒙的声音穿破喧嚣，如号角般嘹亮。

“随我来，照我做的做！”

他直冲那几名惊愕的哨兵，扬声喝道：

“总管（Seneschal）！总管！”

哨兵们立时退开，只当他是总管差来的，任他一行人席卷而过，直扑门楼。那里又有人上前盘问，不过这一回，哨卒只是打听消息。

===Original===
“They are through on the western side!” Simon shouted, and thundered up the stairs, sword drawn. At the top some fifteen men were fretting, trying to hear or see what was toward. They fell upon Simon.

“What news? What news? Are they through? Bring ye commands?”

Before they had realised he was a stranger, he had struck, and with a quick movement, he had flung his cloak about the foremost, muffling and blinding him. The room was suddenly full of armed men, and they hacked down the tiny garrison with deadly precision. Swords were wrenched from scabbards, daggers drawn; all was confusion in that desperate fight. Then again Simon’s voice rang out, and they saw him wrench at the lever which let down the bridge.

“John, Malcolm, Frank, guard me this!” he called, and was lost again amid the scuffling fight.

A cry went up for help; someone reached the great bell-rope, and set the iron bell clanging a wild alarm; dead and wounded lay upon the floor, but Simon’s eleven men were whole, three of them guarding the drawbridge lever as he had commanded. Simon plunged forward to the door, waving a huge key.

===Chinese===
“西边已经破城了！”西蒙大喝，拔剑出鞘，雷鸣般抢上楼梯。梯顶有十五六个人正焦躁地团团转，想听一听、看一看外头的动静。他们一拥而上，围住西蒙。

“什么消息？什么消息？破城了么？可有将令？”

不等众人认清他是个生面孔，他已出手，闪身一旋，把大氅兜头罩住最前面的一个，缠住他，蒙住他的眼。屋里霎时挤满持刃的汉子，手法又狠又准，把这小股守军尽数砍翻。剑被拔出鞘，短刃出袖，那场困兽之斗乱作一团。西蒙的声音再度响起，众人望见他正猛扳那根放下吊桥的绞杠。

“约翰、马尔科姆、弗兰克，替我把住这里！”他喊了一声，又扎进扭杀的人堆里去了。

有人呼喊求援；有人抢到巨钟的钟绳跟前，把那口铁钟敲得狂鸣报警；地上已横着死伤，西蒙的十一个人却个个完好，其中三人奉命守着吊桥绞杠。西蒙挥着一把巨大的钥匙，猛扑向门口。

===Original===
“The rest follow me!” he cried, and was gone down the winding stairs. Out they raced, pell-mell, to the barred gate.

The bell had stirred the garrison station nearby to action. From a little way off came shouts from the oncoming soldiers.

“Guard my back!” Simon gasped, and struck down a man who sought to stand against him. He leapt over the body and fumbled with the key. Cedric was at his side; behind them, his men were engaging with the startled enemy. Slowly, slowly the bolts were pushed back, and the iron bars removed. The gates swung back.

Simon swerved round on his heel to meet the attackers. Some dozen men-at-arms were striving desperately to reach the gate, but Simon’s men had the advantage of them and could hold them in check till Geoffrey came. Simon hacked a way through for himself and Cedric, intent on reaching the gate-tower before the soldiers, who were even now in sight, some mounted, and charging down the narrow street. He was just in time, for a small body of men rushed to the tower to draw up the bridge before it should be too late. They came upon a great knight in golden armour, who stood within the doorway, and met their charge like a rock. His sword slashed and thrust mercilessly, his brow was lowering.

Then a welcome sound fell upon Simon’s ears, a roar and the thunder of hoofs on the wooden bridge. He heaved a short sigh of relief, for the men who guarded the gate for him were hard-pressed, and could hold out no longer. His voice rang out above the medley of sound.

===Chinese===
“其余的跟我来！”他一声喊，已旋下盘梯。众人乱哄哄地疾冲而出，直扑那扇上了闩的城门。

钟声已惊动了近旁的卫所。不远处传来迎面而来的兵士的喊杀声。

“护住我背后！”西蒙喘着气，一剑劈翻一个想要阻拦的人。他跃过尸身，摸索着开锁。塞德里克紧贴在他身旁；身后，他的部下正与惊起的敌人缠斗。门闩一寸一寸地退开，铁杠卸落，城门訇然洞开。

西蒙脚跟一旋，回身迎敌。十来名甲士拼死想抢到城门跟前，但西蒙的人占着上风，足以把他们绊住，直到杰弗里赶来。西蒙为自己和塞德里克杀开一条路，一心要赶在士兵前头夺回门楼——那些士兵眼下已遥遥在望，有些还骑着马，正沿窄街冲杀而下。他赶得恰是时候：一小队人正扑向门楼，要抢在来不及之前把吊桥绞起来。迎接他们的，是门洞里立着的一员金甲伟岸骑士，如磐石般当住了他们的冲锋。他的剑劈刺无情，眉头紧锁。

这时一个可喜的声音撞进西蒙的耳朵——一声呼喝，加上马蹄踏得木桥隆隆如雷。他短促地舒了一口气，替他把守城门的人已经吃紧，撑不了多久了。他的声音压过万千嘈杂，激荡而出。

===Original===
“Stand aside! Stand aside! Let Malvallet finish!”

Even as he shouted to them they had sprung away from the gateway, pressing back against the walls to let Malvallet through.

Plunging into Belrémy came the English, Malvallet at their head, unmistakable by his black plumes and surcoat. He held his lance in one hand, his shield in the other, with the bridle of his own horse, and that of Simon’s huge black charger. Behind him came his own men, and such was the force of their charge that they bore the French backwards into the town, so that they broke, and fled in confusion. In that brief respite Geoffrey wheeled about and came back to the gate. He saw Simon at the entrance of the tower, and charged down upon his assailants, scattering them.

“All safe?” he cried.

Simon caught his horse’s bridle, and the shield from the saddle.

“Ay. I wait to see all in. Ride to the western ramparts now.”

===Chinese===
“闪开！都闪开！让马尔瓦莱来收场！”

话音未落，他们已从门洞弹开，紧贴两壁，放马尔瓦莱进来。

英格兰人涌入贝雷米，马尔瓦莱一马当先，黑羽黑袍，一眼便认得出。他一手挺枪，一手执盾，同时还挽着自己坐骑的缰，和西蒙那匹乌黑巨马的缰。他身后是他自己的部众，这记冲杀势道之猛，把法军一路顶回城中，法军阵脚立溃，四散奔逃。就在这短短的喘息里，杰弗里拨转马头，驰回城门。他望见西蒙立在门楼入口，便朝围攻西蒙的敌兵直冲过去，把他们冲得七零八落。

“都无恙？”他喊道。

西蒙接住马缰，又从鞍上取过盾牌。

“无恙。我留着看全军入城。这就去西面城垣。”

===Original===
Geoffrey turned again, and galloped back into the open street. An order was shouted, and the vanguard closed in behind him, horse and foot, orderly in an instant, the archers with their crossbows held ready. The cavalcade streamed down a side street, making for the western gate.

Again the bridge shook, this time beneath the weight of Alan’s onslaught. In he came, red plumes waving, and his brilliant surcoat stretched out behind him by the wind. Close behind him, riding three abreast, were his horse-archers, skilled warriors every one, mounted on trained chargers. As Alan rode past, Simon shouted to him above the clatter of hoofs on the cobblestones.

“On to the marketplace! I join thee there! ’Ware men from the right!”

Alan glanced quickly over his shoulder, and waved his sword gaily in token that he had heard; then he was gone down the main-street to where the French had gathered, ready to defend their own.

In silence Simon watched his soldiers come running through the gateway, pikes levelled, and every foot striking the ground as one. His eyes glinted as he observed their shining armour and their disciplined appearance. There was no semblance of riot in their attack; they came swiftly and orderly, fine men all of them, and well equipped.

At last came Walter of Santoy, in green-and-russet, Beauvallet colours, riding at the head of the rearguard, some score and a half men-at-arms mounted. They halted within the town, and spread quickly to guard the bridge at a sharp command from Santoy. Eleven of them rode on to where Simon stood, and saluted, dismounting, and holding their steeds in readiness for the men who had entered the town with Simon. It was all done as if by machinery, without fluster.

===Chinese===
杰弗里再度拨马，奔回开阔的大街。一声令下，前队在他身后收拢，步骑瞬间成列，弩手托着弩机引满待发。这一队人马沿着一条侧街流泻而去，直奔西门。

吊桥再度震颤，这一回承的是艾伦突击的重量。他进来了，红羽翻飞，鲜艳的罩袍被风扯得在身后舒卷。紧随其后的是他的骑射手，三骑并行，人人都是训练有素的战士，胯下皆是调教娴熟的战马。艾伦驰过之时，西蒙踏着鹅卵石上杂沓的蹄声，扬声向他喊话。

“直取市场！我随后到那里与你会合！当心右手边来敌！”

艾伦飞快地回头一瞥，兴冲冲地挥了挥剑，示意听见了；随即沿大街疾驰而去，直奔法军聚拢之处——他们已在那儿摆开了死守自家街巷的架势。

西蒙默默看着他的士兵们从门洞奔涌而入，长矛放平，千百只脚踏地，齐整如一人。望着他们明亮的铠甲和严整的军容，他眼中精光一闪。他们的攻势里没有半点乱象；来得分迅疾而有序，个个都是好汉，装备精良。

桑托伊的沃尔特终于到了，一身绿褐二色——那是博瓦莱的旗色——领着后卫队驰来，随行的甲士约有二十五骑。他们在城内勒马，听桑托伊一声令下，迅速散开守住吊桥。其中十一骑驰到西蒙立处，行礼，下马，牵定坐骑，候着随西蒙先入城的那班人马。一切如机械般运转，没有半点慌乱。

===Original===
Then at last Simon moved. He turned, and called up the stairs of the gate-tower.

“All in! Down now to me!”

Down the stairs clattered the three men he had left aloft, wounded every one, but dauntless. Six of Santoy’s men went up to hold the tower in their place, and the three tired warriors mounted their waiting chargers, for they were to form Simon’s bodyguard. One man of the eleven was too badly wounded to move, but the others swung themselves into their saddles. Simon looked them over.

“It was well done,” he said, and from him that was praise enough to set them blushing. He glanced towards the one who was wounded, and raised his hand to his helm in stiff salute. “God be with you, Malcolm.”

“And with you, lord!” Malcolm gasped, and fell back into the arms of the surgeon who had come with Santoy.

===Chinese===
这时西蒙方才动了。他转身，朝门楼的梯上唤道：

“都进城了！下来，到我这里来！”

他留在上面的那三个人咚咚咚奔下梯来，个个带伤，却毫无惧色。桑托伊的部下上去六人接防门楼，那三名倦卒翻上候着的战马——他们要充作西蒙的贴身护卫。十一人里有一个伤重难行，其余的都翻身上了鞍。西蒙把他们逐一打量。

“干得好。”他说。出自他口，这三个字便足够叫他们红透耳根。他朝那个伤者瞥了一眼，抬手向盔檐僵硬地一敬。“上帝保佑你，马尔科姆。”

“也保佑大人！”马尔科姆喘着应了一句，向后倒进随桑托伊同来的军医怀里。

===Original===
Simon mounted his coal-black horse, and watched Cedric fling himself into the nearest horse’s saddle.

“Onward!” he said, and spurred forward down the street in Alan’s wake.

The English had pressed on to the wide marketplace, but there the French were gathered, soldiers and townspeople and there they made a determined stand.

“Way for Beauvallet!” Simon roared, and pressed through to the fore. A hundred voices took up the cry; a wave of relief seemed to sweep through the English ranks.

===Chinese===
西蒙跨上他那匹乌黑发亮的坐骑，看着塞德里克一头扎进最近的一副马鞍。

“前进！”他说着，一夹马腹，沿街疾驰而去，追赶艾伦。

英格兰人已推进到宽阔的集市广场，但法军——兵士与城民——正聚集在那里，摆出了死守的阵仗。

“博瓦莱来了，让路！”西蒙吼道，径直挤到阵前。上百个声音跟着高喊；一浪宽慰仿佛扫过英军的队列。

===Original===
“Way for Beauvallet! Follow the Gilded Armour! The Lion, the Lion! Follow the Gilded Armour!”

The marketplace was a medley of fighting men, a blaze of colour, with here and there the red-and-gold of Montlice showing, fighting shoulder to shoulder with the Malvallet black. Green-and-russet men were scattered all over, and away to the right, the King’s men hacked and hewed with Alan at their head.

Simon pressed on towards one of his captains, rapped out a sharp command, and rode to the left. The captain wheeled about to the right, shouting Simon’s order as he went. In a moment it seemed the English fell into two divisions, and the left flank charged after the great golden figure ahead, bearing down upon the enemy like a battering ram. Back and back fell the French till the marketplace was left behind, and the mad fight swept on into the narrow streets beyond.

Women shrieked from doors and windows, hysterical at the sight of blood, and the sound of steel on steel and the roar of voices. Children who had slipped out into the road, fled hither and thither, terrified at this sudden invasion of fighting men. One babe ran right out into the road almost beneath the plunging hoofs of Simon’s horse. He wrenched the animal back upon its haunches and swung it deftly to one side, stooping to hoist the child up by its mud-spattered skirts.

An agonised, sobbing scream came from the side of the road, where the mother had flattened herself against the wall. Simon cut his way towards her, the babe held safe behind his shield, its face buried in the folds of his surcoat. He handed it down to the woman.

===Chinese===
“给博瓦莱让路！跟紧‘金甲’！狮子，狮子！跟紧‘金甲’！”

集市上厮杀的人群搅作一团，五色斑斓，蒙利斯家的红金二色随处可见，与马尔瓦莱家的黑并肩而战。绿褐衣的部众散布各处，右翼远处，国王的人马正挥砍冲杀，艾伦一马当先。

西蒙逼近他的一员部将，短促地下了一道军令，随后驰向左路。那部将转身向右，一路高传西蒙的将令。转瞬之间，英军仿佛分作两支，左翼随着前方那尊金色巨影掩杀过去，如攻城锤般向敌军碾压。法军一退再退，直退出集市广场，疯狂的厮杀又卷进那一头的窄街深巷。

妇人们在门窗后尖声惊叫，见了血光，听了刀兵相击与鼎沸人声，已近癫狂。溜到街上的孩子们四散乱窜，被这群骤然杀到的武人吓得魂飞魄散。一个娃娃直跑到街心，几乎撞在西蒙坐骑扬起的前蹄下。他把马硬生生拽得人立而起，利落地拨向一旁，俯身拎起那孩子沾满泥点的裙角，把人提上了马。

路旁传来一声撕心裂肺、泣不成声的尖叫——孩子的母亲正贴墙瘫在那里。西蒙一路杀到她跟前，那娃娃稳稳藏在他的盾后，小脸埋在他罩袍的褶皱里。他把孩子递下马，交到妇人怀中。

===Original===
“Get ye within doors,” he told her sternly, and was gone again into the melee.

From the other end of the street enemy reinforcements came running, and the French retreat was checked and the English fell back a little.

Simon rose in his stirrups; his voice blared forth, and at the sound of it his men rallied round him again, and put new zest into their blows.

“For St. George and the King!” Simon cried, and someone behind him started to roar out the song of Agincourt.

A score of voices took it up, and again the English pressed forward.

A burly fellow at Simon’s side smote down one Frenchman who would have hamstrung his horse, and as he did so he sang jovially.

===Chinese===
“躲进屋里去。”他声色俱厉地嘱咐一句，又翻身扎回乱军之中。

街那头敌方的援兵跑到，法军的溃退止住了，英军被逼得稍稍后却。

西蒙在马镫上挺身而起；他的嗓音洪钟般炸响，众人闻声重新聚拢到他身边，出手又添了三分狠劲。

“圣乔治在上，为了国王！”西蒙高呼，身后有人扯开嗓子，吼起了阿金库尔（Agincourt）之歌。

二十来个声音接了上去，英军再度向前压进。

西蒙身旁一条铁塔般的汉子，劈翻了一个想要斩断他马腿筋的法国兵，手上动作不停，嘴里还兴高采烈地唱着。

===Original===
“ ‘Our King went forth to Normandy’—have at ye now! ‘With grace and might of chivalry’—So, so! That for thy pains! ‘The God for him’—Would ye, would ye? ’Ware, lord! ’ware!—‘wrought marv’lously’—Oh, brave, brave, my lord! On, on! ‘Wherefore England’—Hey, John Dawlish, Peter Westmere, take it up!—‘may call and cry: De-o Gratias! De-o Gratias!’ ”

“Deo Gratias, Deo Gratias!” came the roar from all around, and on the words the English swept the French backwards, pressing on and on, down the street.

For fully an hour the fight lasted, all over the town, but at length, first in one place, and then in another, the French cried for quarter. In a little while the truce was called, and comparative silence fell, the battle-yells dying away. Quarter was granted everywhere, and soon the sheriff sent to Simon, who had pushed his way back to the marketplace, surrendering the keys of the town.

===Chinese===
“‘吾王出征诺曼底’——看枪！‘挟骑士之威仪与豪勇’——就这样，就这样！这一下够你消受！‘上帝为他’——还要打，还要打？当心，大人！当心！——‘行下神迹’——好啊，好啊，大人！冲，冲！‘故此英格兰’——喂，约翰·道利什（John Dawlish），彼得·韦斯米尔（Peter Westmere），接着唱！——‘当高呼高唱：感谢上帝（Deo Gratias）！感谢上帝！’”

“感谢上帝！感谢上帝！”四下里吼声如雷，就着这句词，英军把法军一路反卷回去，沿街步步进逼。

这场鏖战足足打了一个钟头，蔓延全城，末了，法军先是一处，继而是处处，纷纷乞降。不多时休战令传下，四下相对沉寂，喊杀之声渐次平息。降者一律得饶，不多时，城守（sheriff）遣使来见已杀回集市广场的西蒙，献上城门的钥匙。

===Original===
Dead and wounded lay upon the ground, but already the women and the noncombatants were out, tending the wounded, whether they were French or English.

Simon found one of his captains in the crowd, and delivered his orders. Most of the French soldiery, it seemed, had fled north to the castle, which still held firm, and wherein lay the Lady Margaret.

Across the square came Malvallet, his armour dented and battered, his surcoat torn.

“God be thanked! Thou art alive!” he cried, and reined in beside Simon. “Huntingdon is in long since. Where is Alan?”

“I have not seen him. To the right, I think, down the street. Holland hath his men in hand?”

===Chinese===
死者伤者横陈于地，妇人和不执兵器的百姓却已出门照料伤员，无论法英，一视同仁。

西蒙在人丛里找到一员部将，下达了命令。法军大部看来已北逃入堡，城堡仍在死守，玛格丽特小姐就在其中。

马尔瓦莱穿过广场而来，铠甲上坑坑洼洼，罩袍撕裂。

“谢天谢地！你还活着！”他喊道，勒马停在西蒙身旁。“亨廷登早进城了。艾伦呢？”

“没见着他。好像在右边，顺这条街下去。霍兰的人马还受他节制么？”

===Original===
“Ay. They tend the wounded, some of them. We hold each gate. I’ll go seek Alan.” He turned, and picked his way across the square.

When he came back it was full half-an-hour later, and the marketplace was almost cleared.

“Simon, Simon!” Malvallet cried, and Simon turned sharply, waiting for Geoffrey to come up to him. “Alan is taken! Taken by that she-devil, and carried into her stronghold!”

“What!” Simon glared into Malvallet’s haggard face. For a moment he was silent, and then his upper lip curled back, showing his teeth in that famous tiger-snarl.

“If I have not Alan by nightfall, may my soul wither in hell!” he said softly.

===Chinese===
“在。有些人在照料伤员。各处城门都在我们手里。我去寻艾伦。”他转过身，择路穿过广场。

他回来时足足过了半个钟头，集市广场已差不多扫荡干净。

“西蒙，西蒙！”马尔瓦莱喊道，西蒙霍然转身，等着杰弗里奔到跟前。“艾伦被掳了！被那母夜叉掳了去，带进她的老巢了！”

“什么！”西蒙瞪住马尔瓦莱那张憔悴的脸。他沉默了片刻，随即上唇朝上一掀，龇出牙齿，露出那副出名的虎啸怒容。

“入夜之前若要不回艾伦，叫我灵魂在地狱里枯烂！”他轻声说。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
How He Saw the Lady Margaret

===Chinese===
他如何见到玛格丽特夫人

===Original===
By noon he had brought some semblance of order into Belrémy and had held a long parley with the sheriff. The usual proclamations were posted up, in the King’s name, promising fair treatment and protection to all who would swear allegiance to Henry. For the most part the townsfolk availed themselves of this clemency, for they were tired of the long siege, and anxious to revictual the town. Simon’s men were stationed round the town and in it, and at length he had leisure to consider Alan’s predicament. It was rumoured that Montlice was first wounded, and then overcome by the Lady Margaret’s men-at-arms.

“Simon, thou’lt rescue him?” Geoffrey said anxiously. They were in the justice-house, which Simon had made his temporary headquarters.

“Ay,” Simon answered. “She will look to hold him as hostage, but I have her in a vice. I hold her uncle prisoner.”

“Her uncle? He fought this morning?”

“He is her Marshal. The Sire de Galledemaine. Huntingdon took him. Bernard, bring thy quill, and parchment.”

The secretary collected them, and sat waiting for further orders.

===Chinese===
到正午，他已将贝雷米（Belrémy）整治得粗具秩序，并同城守（sheriff）盘桓长谈了一番。照例的文告以国王之名张贴出去，许诺凡向亨利（Henry）宣誓效忠者，必以公道相待、加意庇护。城中百姓大多领受这份宽仁——他们久困围城，早已疲敝，只盼早些重整粮秣。西蒙（Simon）的人马布防于城外四郊，也分驻城内，他这才得空来思量艾伦（Alan）的困境。据传蒙利斯（Montlice）先已负伤，随后便被玛格丽特夫人（Lady Margaret）的甲士拿了。

“西蒙，你会去救他的，对么？”杰弗里（Geoffrey）忧心忡忡地问。他们此刻正在审判厅里——西蒙已把那里辟作临时行辕。

“自然，”西蒙答道。“她指望拿他当人质，可我已把她夹进了钳子——她的叔父正在我手中。”

“她叔父？他今早还上阵了？”

“他是她的纠仪长（Marshal），加勒德曼（Galledemaine）老爷。亨廷登（Huntingdon）擒的他。伯纳德（Bernard），把你的鹅毛笔和羊皮纸取来。”

秘书取来笔墨纸张，坐候下一步吩咐。

===Original===
“Write,” Simon said slowly. “ ‘To the Lady Margaret of Belrémy. In the name of His Most Gracious Majesty, King Henry the Fifth of England and France, I, Simon of Beauvallet, command that ye surrender the keys of the Castle of Belrémy within the hour, swearing fealty to His Majesty King Henry, and delivering the knight, Sir Alan of Montlice, into my hands.’ Thou hast that?”

“Ay, my lord.”

“Dispatch it by my herald at once, then, and bid him await the lady’s answer.”

“What folly is this?” Malvallet asked, when Talmayne had withdrawn. “She will laugh at thy message.”

“Perchance. It is my formal command. If she laughs now, she will weep later.”

===Chinese===
“写，”西蒙缓缓口授。“‘致贝雷米的玛格丽特夫人：兹以至尊至仁的君王、英格兰与法兰西国王亨利五世陛下之名——我，博瓦莱的西蒙，限你在一个钟头之内交出贝雷米城堡的钥匙，向亨利国王陛下宣誓效忠，并将骑士蒙利斯的艾伦爵士交入我手。’记下了么？”

“是，大人。”

“那么即刻命我的传令官送去，叫他候着夫人的回音。”

塔尔曼（Talmayne）退下之后，马尔瓦莱（Malvallet）问道：“这是什么荒唐名堂？她只会笑你的信。”

“或许。这是我的正式敕令。她此刻笑，日后便会哭。”

===Original===
The herald returned within the hour, and knelt to give Simon the Lady Margaret’s packet.

Simon broke the seals and spread the crackling parchment sheets before him. Over his shoulder Geoffrey read:

“To Simon of Beauvallet.

“If ye depart not from this my city within the space of twelve hours, surrendering the keys unto Ferdinand de Valmé, my Sheriff, the knight, Sir Alan of Montlice, swings from the ramparts in thy sight.

“Written at my Castle of Belrémy this twenty-first day of December.”

===Chinese===
传令官不出一个钟头便回来了，跪呈玛格丽特夫人的回函。

西蒙拆下火漆，把窸窣作响的羊皮纸在面前铺开。杰弗里从他肩后读道：

“致博瓦莱的西蒙：

“你若不在十二个钟头之内退出我这座城、把钥匙交还我的城守费迪南德·德·瓦尔梅（Ferdinand de Valmé），蒙利斯的艾伦爵士便当着你的面吊死在城头。

“十二月二十一日，书于我贝雷米城堡。”

===Original===
Geoffrey let fly a great oath, and clapped his hand to his sword-hilt.

“Thou wilt storm the place, Simon?”

Simon smiled.

“Nay. That would surely bring death to Alan, thou hothead. Write again, Bernard: ‘If my commands be not obeyed, I, Simon of Beauvallet, do swear by the Rood and by all the blessed Saints that the Marshal, Jean de Galledemaine, dies before the Castle of Belrémy with the other prisoners in my hold, and every third breadwinner of this town. And further if any harm be done unto the knight, Sir Alan of Montlice, I do swear by God that I will raze this city to the ground, slaying all who dwell therein and sparing neither woman nor child. And that ye may see that I swear it not idly, six of the children will I slay before the castle if ye surrender not at once.’ ”

Malvallet laughed.

“Oh, ay! With thine own hand, belike!”

===Chinese===
杰弗里骂出一声粗话，一把按住剑柄。

“你要强攻那地方么，西蒙？”

西蒙微微一笑。

“不行。那样艾伦必死无疑，你这急性子。再写一封，伯纳德：‘我的号令若不得遵行，我，博瓦莱的西蒙，谨指圣架与诸真福圣徒立誓：纠仪长加勒德曼的让（Jean de Galledemaine），必与我所押的其他俘虏一同死于贝雷米城堡之前；本城养家之人，每三名处死其一。再者，蒙利斯的艾伦爵士若受丝毫伤害，我谨指上帝立誓，必把此城夷为平地，尽屠城中生灵，妇孺一个不留。为使你们知道我并非空言立誓，你们若不即刻归降，我便在城堡之前处死六个孩童。’”

马尔瓦莱放声大笑。

“哦，那是自然！多半还要你亲手来杀喽！”

===Original===
“It will not come to that,” Simon answered. He waited until Bernard had sealed the parchment and given it to him. He handed it to the herald. “If the Lady Margaret should speak with thee, asking what manner of man I may be, thou wilt tell her that what I say I will do, I do. Thou didst deliver mine other message into her hands?”

“Ay, my lord.”

“She spake not?”

“Nay, sir. She withdrew with her gentlemen, and was closely veiled.”

Simon nodded.

“Go then.”

===Chinese===
“闹不到那一步。”西蒙答道。他候伯纳德在羊皮纸上加了封，把文书递来，又转手交给传令官。“玛格丽特夫人若同你攀谈，问起我是何等样人，你就告诉她：我说要做的事，必定做到。先前那道文书，你可亲手交到她手上了？”

“是，大人。”

“她没说话？”

“没有，先生。她带着众绅士退了进去，蒙着厚厚的面纱。”

西蒙点头。

“去吧。”

===Original===
When the herald returned again it was with a verbal message.

“ ‘Tell my lord of Beauvallet,’ ” he recited, “ ‘that the Lady Margaret, Countess of Belrémy, will treat with him within her castle of Belrémy if he comes alone, and under the laws of truce.’ ”

“Thou’lt not go alone into that trap!” Geoffrey exclaimed.

“No trap is it,” Simon said.

“What! Thou wilt trust to a woman’s honour?”

“Nay.” Simon smiled unpleasantly. “She dare not harm me, or detain me. If I return not within the hour lead out the Sire de Galledemaine, and slay him before the castle. Then if I still make no sign, thou mayst sack the town, to show that I lied not, and storm the castle, for I shall be dead.”

===Chinese===
传令官再回来时，带回的是一句口信。

“‘回禀博瓦莱大人，’他一字一句背诵道，‘贝雷米女伯爵玛格丽特夫人愿在她的贝雷米城堡之内与他会商议和，只须他独自前来，并依休战之法。’”

“你不会孤身走进那道陷阱吧！”杰弗里失声道。

“算不得什么陷阱。”西蒙道。

“什么！你要信一个女人的名誉？”

“不。”西蒙笑得叫人不快。“她不敢伤我，也不敢扣我。我一个钟头之内若不回来，就把加勒德曼老爷押出去，杀在城堡门前。届时我若仍无音讯，你尽可洗劫全城，证明我言而有信，再强攻城堡——反正我已然死了。”

===Original===
“What dost thou propose?” Geoffrey asked curiously. “Once within her stronghold thou art lost.”

Simon laughed.

“Am I so? Once within the castle, and I may crush the she-devil at will.” He rose. “Thou art lord in mine absence, Geoffrey, but look to it that ye obey mine orders.” He went out to his own quarters, where he found Cedric resting on his pallet, relating his glorious adventures to Edmund, who listened curiously, drinking in every word. When Simon came in, they both started up.

===Chinese===
“你打的什么算盘？”杰弗里好奇地问。“进了她的老巢，你就完了。”

西蒙朗声大笑。

“是么？进了城堡，那母夜叉便任我拿捏。”他起身。“我不在时，你便是主事之人，杰弗里，但切记，务必依我的将令行事。”他走回自己住处，见塞德里克（Cedric）正歪在床榻上，向埃德蒙（Edmund）讲述自己光辉的历险；埃德蒙听得入神，一字一句都吞进肚里。西蒙进来时，两个孩子一齐跳了起来。

===Original===
Simon looked Cedric over keenly.

“Thou wert wounded?”

“It is naught, sir,” Cedric blushed. His arm lay in a sling.

“The surgeon hath seen to it?”

The boy fidgeted.

“Nay, my lord. I asked him not, for he was busy with others, and indeed my wound is trifling.”

===Chinese===
西蒙锐利地上下打量塞德里克。

“你受伤了？”

“不碍事，先生。”塞德里克红着脸。他的手臂吊在绷带里。

“医士瞧过了么？”

孩子局促地扭动起来。

“没有，大人。我没去请他，他忙着照看旁人，再说我的伤实在轻微。”

===Original===
Simon went to him and unbound his arm. An ugly flesh wound met his eye, which still bled sluggishly.

“Fetch me water and clean linen,” Simon ordered briefly, and Edmund ran out. He came back with the water, and watched his lord wash Cedric’s wound quickly and deftly. Simon bound it up again, and Cedric’s teeth slowly unclenched. He was rather pale, for Simon’s methods were rough and ready.

“Get thee to bed,” Simon said, “and stay there. Edmund, bring mine armour. Ye have cleaned it?”

“Ay, my lord.”

“Fetch it then, and get thee ready. I go to the castle.”

Cedric, who had retired to his pallet, raised himself on one elbow.

===Chinese===
西蒙走到他跟前，解开绷带。一道难看的皮肉伤映入眼中，还在慢慢地渗血。

“拿水和干净麻布来。”西蒙简短吩咐。埃德蒙飞奔出去，打水回来，站在一旁看主人利落干脆地洗净塞德里克的伤口。西蒙重新包扎妥当，塞德里克咬紧的牙关才慢慢松开。他脸色白得厉害——西蒙的手法实在又粗又猛。

“上床去，”西蒙说，“老老实实待着。埃德蒙，把我的铠甲拿来。擦过了么？”

“擦过了，大人。”

“那就取来，伺候穿戴。我要去城堡。”

塞德里克已回到床榻，闻言用一肘撑起了身子。

===Original===
“My lord!”

The hard eyes looked down upon him coldly.

“Well?”

“Take﻿—take me!”

“Edmund goes with me. Lie thou still.”

“But, sir!﻿—”

===Chinese===
“大人！”

那双冷硬的眼睛凉凉地俯视着他。

“怎么？”

“带——带我去！”

“埃德蒙随我去。躺着别动。”

“可是，先生！——”

===Original===
“It shall be thy punishment for defying me today,” Simon said inexorably.

“Oh, my lord, no! I cannot let ye go to the castle without﻿—”

“Let? Let? What is this talk? Thou wilt be silent, Cedric, an ye desire not my displeasure.”

Cedric’s eyes filled with tears.

“My lord, punish me how you will, but take me with you now! If﻿—if aught should befall you﻿—”

“What help could ye give me?” Simon said scathingly.

===Chinese===
“今日违逆于我，这便是给你的责罚。”西蒙不容分说。

“哦，大人，别这样！我不能让您就这么去城堡，连个——”

“不能让？让？这是什么话？塞德里克，你若不想惹我动气，就给我住口。”

塞德里克的眼里蓄满了泪。

“大人，您要罚怎么罚都行，可这一回带我去吧！若——若您有个三长两短——”

“你帮得上我什么忙？”西蒙语带讥诮。

===Original===
Cedric plucked at his blanket with trembling fingers.

“I﻿—I should﻿—at least be﻿—with you. If﻿—if ye should be slain, I﻿—I﻿—”

“Ye will have learned a lesson. I am not lightly defied, Cedric.”

The boy turned his face to the wall without another word. Not until Simon was fully clad in his shining armour, did he speak again, and then it was to Edmund, who stood preening himself in his green-and-russet dress.

“If harm comes to my lord, I will beat thee senseless!” he whispered savagely.

Simon strode out, an amused glint in his eyes.

===Chinese===
塞德里克颤抖的手指揪扯着毯子。

“我——我至少——能——在您身边。若——若您遭了不测，我——我——”

“那你也就学到教训了。我，可不是轻易容人违逆的，塞德里克。”

孩子把脸转向墙壁，再没说一个字。直到西蒙披挂齐整、甲光锃亮，他才重新开口，说的对象却是埃德蒙——那孩子正穿着绿褐相间的号衣，自鸣得意地顾影自盼。

“主人若有个好歹，我揍得你人事不省！”他咬牙切齿地低声道。

西蒙大步而出，眼中闪着一丝有趣的光。

===Original===
He rode through the town with Edmund close behind him, and came quickly to the castle. The bridge was let down for them, and they went across at a walk-pace. In the courtyard Simon dismounted and gave his horse into Edmund’s charge. Unattended, he followed the steward into the castle.

The great hall was empty, and the steward led Simon across it, to the Countess’s audience-chamber. He swung back the curtain, and sonorously announced, “My Lord of Beauvallet!”

Simon entered, stepping firmly, yet panther-like. Within the room he paused, hand upon his sword-hilt, and sent a swift glance round.

Upon a dais, seated on a throne-like chair, was the Lady Margaret, like a pillar of ice. Her regal head, crowned by a cloud of black locks, and a great horned headdress, from which hung a veil of gold net, pearl embroidered, was held high. Not a muscle in her long white throat quivered; her face was mask-like, oval and pale, with thin, disdainful lips, and black eyes that shone between lowered lids. The lashes, long and curling, seemed to cast a shadow on the perfect skin beneath them. Her nose was short and straight, the nostrils finely carved, and slightly pinched. She was clad in a gown of wine-red silk, which moulded itself to her superb form, showing the swell of her breasts, and the long line to her hips. It fell about her feet in a great train, hiding them, and clung close to her rounded arms till it widened at the wrists in huge sleeves which brushed the ground as she walked. Her white hands lay along the arms of her chair, the nervous fingers gripping the carved wood tensely. On her bosom a great ruby glowed, the only living thing about her.

Beside her stood a dark gentleman, foppishly clad, who regarded Simon with a faint sneer upon his full lips. He twirled a rose between his fingers, and raised it to his nose now and again. Other gentlemen were scattered about the room, all in court-dress, and all watching Simon curiously. Behind the Countess stood three of her ladies, still as was their mistress.

===Chinese===
他策马穿过全城，埃德蒙紧紧跟在身后，不多时便到了城堡。吊桥为他们放下，二人缓辔而过。院子里，西蒙翻身下马，把坐骑交给埃德蒙照管，只身随总管（steward）步入城堡。

大厅空空荡荡，总管领着西蒙穿行而过，来到伯爵夫人的觐见厅。他掀开帷幔，声音洪亮地通传：“博瓦莱大人到！”

西蒙举步入内，步子沉稳，却又矫捷如豹。进得房中，他手按剑柄，略一定住，目光向四下里疾扫一圈。

高台上一张御座般的椅子里，坐着玛格丽特夫人，宛似一根冰柱。她仪态威严的头颅高高扬起，乌黑的鬈发堆云一般，上覆一座华丽的角式头冠，冠垂金网纱帷，绣满珍珠。修长白皙的颈喉纹丝不动；那张面孔如同假面，椭圆而苍白，薄薄的嘴唇透着轻蔑，一双黑眸在低垂的眼睑间灼灼放光。那睫毛又长又翘，仿佛在底下无瑕的肌肤上投下一弯暗影。鼻梁短而挺直，鼻翼雕琢精细，微微收紧。她一身酒红色丝袍，紧贴着她曼妙绝伦的身段，衬出胸前的丰盈和直贯臀际的修长线条。袍裾在足下铺成宽宽的裙裾，将双足掩住，又紧裹着浑圆的手臂，至腕处方才放宽成大大的广袖，行走时衣摆扫地。她雪白的双手搭在椅臂之上，紧张的手指死死扣着雕花的木头。胸前一颗大红宝石熠熠生辉——那是她周身唯一有生气的东西。

她身旁立着一位肤色黝黑的绅士，穿得脂粉气十足，饱满的唇上挂着一丝讥诮，正上下打量西蒙。他指间捻着一枝玫瑰，不时举到鼻尖轻嗅。房中各处还散立着别的绅士，一色宫廷装束，都好奇地望着西蒙。伯爵夫人身后立着三名女官，同她们的主人一般纹丝不动。

===Original===
Simon walked forward deliberately. He seemed to tower above the men present, an incongruous figure in the midst of this elegant assembly, Saxon-fair, and all in gold save for his waving plumes, and long green surcoat. Before the dais he halted, and glanced calmly at the Countess from beneath his helm.

“Madame,” he said in blunt French, “I am here to receive your submission.”

The haughty lips curved in a pitying smile. The Countess made a gesture with her right hand, and the foppish gentleman stepped forward. He answered Simon in lisping English.

“You are a leetle brusque, milor’, is it not so? Madame my cousin desires to make terms with you.”

===Chinese===
西蒙不疾不徐地走上前去。他仿佛比在场的男人们都高出一头，置身这群优雅之士中间，俨然是个异数——一张撒克逊人的白皙面孔，通身金甲，唯有飘扬的羽饰和长长的绿袍不在此列。他在高台前站定，自盔沿下平静地瞥了伯爵夫人一眼。

“夫人，”他操着一口生硬的法语说道，“我此来，是接受你的归降。”

那倨傲的嘴唇弯出一抹怜悯的笑。伯爵夫人抬起右手做了个手势，脂粉气的绅士便跨步上前。他操着咬舌的英语回答西蒙。

“大人，您未免有——有点儿失之唐突了，可不是么？我的表姐夫人有意与您讲和。”

===Original===
“My terms are these,” Simon said, addressing her. “If ye do surrender unto me the keys of this castle, and do swear fealty to my master, King Henry”﻿—he raised his hand to his helm a moment﻿—“I can offer you his gracious protection and clemency.”

A pulse on her temple throbbed angrily.

“My cousin,” she said, also in English, “tell him that it is for me to make terms.” Her voice was clear and cold. She did not look at Simon.

The dapper gentleman seemed to deprecate this harshness.

“Ah, *oui*! You will agree, milor’, that Madame la Comtesse is in a more fit position to treat than are you.”

===Chinese===
“我的条件如下，”西蒙径直对她说道。“你若将这座城堡的钥匙交到我手，并宣誓效忠我的主君亨利王”——他抬手向头盔略一致意——“我便能给你他的庇护与恩典。”

她太阳穴上的一根血管怒跳了一下。

“表弟，”她也用英语说道，“告诉他，条件该由我来开。”她的声音清冷。她并不看西蒙。

那位衣冠楚楚的绅士似乎觉得这话未免太硬。

“啊，是啦！您总得承认，大人，伯爵夫人阁下比您更有资格谈条件。”

===Original===
Simon’s mouth was grim.

“Nay, sir. I cannot agree. I hold Madame and you all in a vice.”

The Frenchman smiled.

“Aha?” He raised the rose gracefully. “One man against﻿—shall we say five score?”

Simon shot him that rapier-glance, and despite his effrontery, the Frenchman involuntarily stepped back.

===Chinese===
西蒙嘴唇绷得铁紧。

“不，先生，我不能承认。夫人连同诸位，尽在我的钳握之中。”

法国人笑了。

“哦——？”他优雅地举起玫瑰。“一个人对——姑且算他五十人？”

西蒙剑锋似的一眼射向他。任他脸皮再厚，法国人也不由自主地退了半步。

===Original===
“I came under the laws of truce,” Simon said harshly.

The Chevalier de Fleurival recovered himself. He raised his shoulders nonchalantly.

“In times of stress, milor’﻿ ﻿… *eh bien*! You walked in so﻿—so﻿—without guile, is it not so?”

“And if I walk not out within the hour, the Sire de Galledemaine dies before your gates.”

The Chevalier paled a little, but still he smiled.

===Chinese===
“我是依休战之法而来。”西蒙厉声道。

弗勒里瓦尔骑士（the Chevalier de Fleurival）定过神来，满不在乎地耸了耸肩。

“非常时期嘛，大人……唉！您方才走进来时，就那样——那样——不设防备，是不是？”

“我若在一个钟头之内走不出去，加勒德曼老爷就死在你们城门跟前。”

骑士微微失色，却仍在笑。

===Original===
“So you think, milor’, to take this castle single-handed?”

“Within the hour.”

“*Est-ce possible?*” The Chevalier laughed gently. “My father, the Sire de Galledemaine, is old, milor’. Death comes easily to the old.”

“And to the young.” The words fell heavily, and again the Countess stirred in her chair.

“That foolish threat!” The Chevalier shook with supercilious merriment. “We are not fools, milor’.”

“If ye surrender not this castle, and Sir Alan of Montlice, then will ye indeed be fools,” Simon said calmly. “Ye will see my soldiers burn Belrémy to the ground, and slay all those who dwell therein. I threaten not.”

===Chinese===
“所以您以为，大人，单枪匹马便夺得下这座城堡？”

“一个钟头之内。”

“这可能么？”骑士轻声笑道。“家父加勒德曼老爷年事已高，大人。老人家嘛，死总是来得容易。”

“对年轻人也一样。”这话掷地千钧，伯爵夫人又在椅中动了一动。

“愚蠢的恫吓！”骑士笑得浑身直抖，满脸倨傲。“我们可不是傻子，大人。”

“你们若不交出这座城堡、不交出蒙利斯的艾伦爵士，那才真真做了傻子。”西蒙平静道。“你们将亲眼看着我的士兵把贝雷米烧成白地，杀尽城中之人。我可不是虚声恫吓。”

===Original===
The Chevalier smelt his rose delicately. Over it, his eyes never left Simon’s face.

“But if, milor’, you are dead, to what avail? I have heard such threats before.”

Simon smiled.

“Ye know not me, sir, if ye think my captains obey not my word, whether I am quick or dead.”

“Yes? But ye grow discourteous, milor’. Be sure the Comtesse desires not your life. Her terms are that if ye will withdraw your men from Belrémy, swearing never to return, she will deliver Sir Alan of Montlice into your care as soon as ye have left the town.”

“I thank Madame la Comtesse!” Simon’s voice grated. “But she is overproud, methinks.”

===Chinese===
骑士文雅地嗅着他的玫瑰，目光却越过花枝，一刻不离西蒙的脸。

“可您若死了，大人，这一切又有何用？这样的恫吓，我从前听得多了。”

西蒙微微而笑。

“先生，您若以为我或生或死，麾下将佐便敢抗我号令，那您就是不认得我。”

“哦？可您越来越失礼了，大人。放心，伯爵夫人并不想要您的性命。她的条件是：您若肯把人马撤出贝雷米，立誓永不回犯，那么您一出城，她便将蒙利斯的艾伦爵士交到您手上。”

“多谢伯爵夫人阁下了！”西蒙的声音磨得人耳朵发涩。“不过依我看，她骄傲得过了头。”

===Original===
“In a word, milor’, you refuse?”

“I ignore.”

The clear voice from the throne spoke again.

“Tell him, my cousin, to consider well. If he refuse my terms, then will I send to dispatch Sir Alan of Montlice right speedily, and will send him the same road.”

Simon stood silent, and a gleam of triumph came into the Chevalier’s eyes.

“That gives food for thought, milor’?”

===Chinese===
“一句话，大人，您是回绝了？”

“我不予理会。”

御座上又传来那清冷的声音。

“告诉他，表弟，教他好生思量。他若不依我的条件，我便即刻打发蒙利斯的艾伦爵士上路，再送他走同一条路。”

西蒙默然立着，骑士眼中闪出一丝得色。

“这话可够您思量一阵了吧，大人？”

===Original===
Simon heeded him not, but looked at the Lady Margaret.

“That is your last word, madame?”

“My last word,” she answered.

Then Simon moved. In a flash he had torn his sword from the scabbard and was upon the dais, holding the weapon shortened, the point touching the Countess’s white breast.

There was a horrified cry; the men sprang forward, but stopped short as Simon drew his arm back to thrust. His left hand gripped the Countess’s wrist; he looked over his shoulder at the room.

“One step more, and your mistress dies,” he said softly. “The truce is at an end.”

===Chinese===
西蒙不睬他，只望着玛格丽特夫人。

“夫人，这是你最后的话？”

“是我最后的话。”她答道。

接着，西蒙动了。眨眼之间，他已拔剑出鞘，跃上高台，短握剑身，剑尖抵住伯爵夫人白皙的前胸。

满堂惊呼；众绅士纷纷扑上前来，却在西蒙扬臂欲刺的一刻齐齐钉在了原地。他左手扣住伯爵夫人的手腕，回头环视满屋之人。

“再迈一步，你们的主母就没命。”他轻轻地说。“休战到此为止。”

===Original===
The Countess sat rigid, braving Simon with her dark eyes. The Chevalier had dropped his rose. He spoke uncertainly, ashen-cheeked.

“Milor’, milor’! One does not offer violence to a lady.”

“But a she-devil one burns,” Simon barked, “as I will burn this Amazon if I find not Sir Alan, alive and unhurt.”

A shudder went through the Chevalier; one of the ladies-in-waiting started to sob wildly.

Simon looked down into the proud face that defied him so bravely.

“Those six children, madame, my captain holds in safe custody,” he said. “Ye shall see them die.”

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人僵坐不动，一双黑眸毫不退让地迎住西蒙。

骑士手里的玫瑰落了地。他脸色灰败，说话的声音都发了虚。

“可母夜叉是要烧死的，”西蒙厉声道，“找不着毫发无伤的艾伦爵士，我就烧了这亚马逊悍女。”

骑士浑身一颤；一名女官失声痛哭起来。

西蒙低头望着那张如此勇敢地抗辩着他的骄傲面孔。

“夫人，那六个孩子，我的统领已严密看管。”他说道。“您会亲眼看着他们死。”

===Original===
Her eyelids flickered uncontrollably, and he saw the muscles of her throat contract.

“You would not dare!”

Simon laughed.

“An ye fail to order your men to submit, madame, ye will see how much I dare.”

“Cur!” She spat the word at him, breathing short and fast. “Ye would kill babes? Cur that ye are!”

“Nay, ’tis you who will kill them, madame.”

===Chinese===
她的眼睑不由自主地颤了一颤，他看见她喉间的肌肉收紧了。

“你不敢！”

西蒙笑了。

“您若不下令叫你的人归降，夫人，您就会知道我有多敢。”

“恶狗！”她把这个字啐到他脸上，呼吸又短又促。“你竟要杀害婴孩？你这恶狗！”

“不，夫人，杀他们的将是你。”

===Original===
Her fingers clenched together.

“I will first kill Sir Alan of Montlice!” she flashed, and turned her head. “Go, Henri de Malincourt! Slay me this English Alan!”

“Ay, go,” Simon said, and brought his sword to her breast. Under its point a tiny red speck appeared, but the Countess flinched not. Only she stamped her foot.

“Go, I say!”

One man stepped forward a pace.

“Madame, I dare not,” he said humbly.

===Chinese===
她十指攥紧。

“那我先杀了蒙利斯的艾伦爵士！”她厉声抢白，转过头去。“去，马兰库尔的亨利（Henri de Malincourt）！替我杀了那个英格兰人艾伦！”

“好，去啊。”西蒙说着，剑锋又向她胸前逼进。剑尖之下渗出一点殷红，伯爵夫人却连缩也不缩，只是一跺脚。

“去！我说了，去！”

一名男子上前了半步。

“夫人，小人不敢。”他怯声说道。

===Original===
“Craven! Will not one of you do my bidding? Call me not mistress again if ye defy me now!”

The Chevalier raised one shaking hand.

“Let no man stir. Milor’, this is between men. Release my cousin.”

Simon’s hold on the lady’s wrist tightened till she bit her lip with the pain of it.

“Bid thy men swear before God to submit themselves,” he said.

Her teeth were tightly clenched.

===Chinese===
“懦夫！你们竟没有一个肯听我号令么？今日违逆于我，往后就再别唤我主母！”

骑士举起一只颤抖的手。

“谁都不许动。大人，这是我们男人之间的事。放开我的表姐。”

西蒙扣着夫人手腕的手收紧，紧得她痛得咬住了嘴唇。

“叫你的人当着上帝起誓归降。”他说。

她牙关紧咬。

===Original===
“Thou shalt slay me first!”

Tighter and tighter grew his hold on her arm.

“And thy people?﻿—the children of Belrémy?”

For a long minute she glared up into his strange eyes, but try as she might she could not read his mind.

“Ye seek to force me to yield through pity!”

“God wot, not I! Hast thou any, thou breaker of truces?”

===Chinese===
“除非先杀了我！”

他钳着她手臂的手越收越紧。

“那你的人民呢？——贝雷米的孩子们呢？”

好长一刻，她仰头逼视着他那双莫测的眼睛，可无论怎样用力，也读不出他的心思。

“你想拿恻隐之心逼我就范！”

“上帝知道，我可没有！你呢，践踏休战之约的人，你有么？”

===Original===
Again she spoke to the men who stood rooted to the ground before her.

“Ye are ten to his one! Think ye he would dare to slay me? On to him, I command!”

A little deeper pressed the sword, and the red speck grew. Simon smiled grimly down upon his foes.

The Chevalier’s eyes shifted from face to face; all the smiling insolence had gone out of them. They came at last to his cousin. His mouth worked a little.

“Cousin, thou must yield! I implore thee, be not foolhardy!”

“Ye would be wise to listen to your cousin,” Simon said. “I will give ye one minute, and then I will strike home.”

===Chinese===
她又转向面前钉在原地的众人喊话。

“你们十个对他一个！你们当他真敢杀我？上，拿下他！这是我的命令！”

剑锋又压深一分，那点红扩大了。西蒙居高临下，冷冷笑视着他的敌人们。

骑士的目光一张张脸扫过去，先前那股含笑的傲慢荡然无存。最后，目光落到他表姐脸上。他的嘴唇抽动了几下。

“表姐，你只能屈服了！我求求你，别再逞强了！”

“听你表弟的话，才是聪明。”西蒙说道。“我给你一分钟，过后我便一剑刺到底。”

===Original===
“Thus you seal your own doom!” she cried. “Once I am sped, there are ten men ready to fall upon thee!”

“It matters not,” Simon said. “If I die no Frenchman will live in this town by sunrise tomorrow. The minute passes, madame. Think well.”

“Cousin, thou art distraught! I stand as regent during thy madness. Is there a man here will refuse to recognise me as lord?”

A low murmur of approval went up.

“Then I submit, milor’, in the name of the Countess Margaret.”

The Countess lashed round in her chair.

===Chinese===
“你这是自寻死路！”她喊道。“我一死，便有十个人扑上来要你的命！”

“无关紧要。”西蒙道。“我若死，明日日出之前，这城里便不会再有一个活的法国人。一分钟快完了，夫人。好生想想。”

“表姐，你神志昏乱了！你疯癫期间，权柄由我代掌。此处可有哪一位男子，不肯奉我为主？”

一阵低低的附和声四下响起。

“那么我归降，大人——以玛格丽特伯爵夫人的名义。”

伯爵夫人在椅中猛地转过身来。

===Original===
“Ah, never!” she cried, and would have flung herself upon Simon’s sword, had he not drawn it swiftly back. He bowed slightly to the Chevalier.

“Ye do swear before God to offer no violence nor obstruction either now, or later?”

The Chevalier was biting his nails, seeking feverishly for some outlet. He sent Simon a look of hatred.

“I swear before God to offer no violence nor obstruction now or later.”

“And for thy men?”

“And for my men.”

===Chinese===
“啊，决不！”她喊道。若非西蒙剑锋疾撤，她已向那剑尖扑了上去。他向骑士微微欠身。

“你可当着上帝起誓——无论此刻或日后，决不动武，决不阻挠？”

骑士咬着指甲，狂乱地寻思脱身之计。他向西蒙投去恨毒的一眼。

“我当着上帝起誓——无论此刻或日后，决不动武，决不阻挠。”

“你的人呢？”

“我的人也一样。”

===Original===
“Good.” Simon jerked the Countess to her feet. “Ye will lead me now, madame, to Sir Alan of Montlice. These gentlemen will go before.”

“Milor’!” The Chevalier was livid with rage. “Is that necessary? Unhand my cousin! You have mine oath!”

“I would sooner have thy cousin, for thus shall I also have thine oath,” Simon answered.

The Chevalier quivered with outraged dignity.

“It seems ye trust us not, sir!”

The green-blue eyes narrowed.

===Chinese===
“好。”西蒙猛地把伯爵夫人拽了起来。“夫人，现在你带我去见蒙利斯的艾伦爵士。诸位先生在前面走。”

“大人！”骑士气得脸色铁青。“这有必要么？放开我表姐！您已有我的誓言！”

“我宁可要你的表姐——这样，你的誓言也才真正攥在我手里。”西蒙答道。

骑士气得浑身发抖，尊严扫地。

“看来您是不信我们喽，先生！”

那双蓝绿的眼睛眯了起来。

===Original===
“Fair sir, were I a fool, then should I trust to your word. I am not a fool.”

The Chevalier’s hand flew to his sword-hilt.

“Ye shall answer to me for that insult!” he choked.

Simon spoke sternly.

“When I entered this place, sir, I entered it alone, as the Countess desired, under the laws of truce. Those were her words. But once within these portals it pleased the Countess, and ye all, to forget the laws of truce. Ye did threaten me with violence, who had come to treat. I fight clean, sir, when I may, but I choose my foe’s weapons, and when the foe seeks to fight me foully, why, then, the time for chivalry is past. Lead on, Sir Chevalier.”

===Chinese===
“俊美的先生，我若是傻子，才会信你的空口白话。我不是傻子。”

骑士的手霍地按上剑柄。

“这句侮辱，你得向我讨个交代！”他哽声道。

西蒙沉声说道。

“先生，我进这道门时，是孤身一人，如伯爵夫人所请，依休战之法而来——那是她亲口的话。可才进门内，伯爵夫人同诸位便都忘了休战之法。我是前来议和的，你们却拿暴力相胁。先生，能行得正时，我打得干净；可兵器由敌人来选，敌人既要使阴的，那骑士风度便到此为止。前面带路吧，骑士先生。”

===Original===
The Chevalier went blindly to the door, and the courtiers followed him, one by one. Last of all came Simon, holding the Countess a little before him. She struggled once, striking up at his face with her free hand, but Simon forced her onward. She went proudly then, her head held high, carrying herself with queenly dignity, her skirts sweeping behind her.

Out into the great hall they went, past startled menials, to the narrow stairway. The Countess went forward, for two could not walk abreast, and Simon had released her. Up they went to a room in the tower. There Simon took her wrist in his hold again, and as she winced, loosened his clasp a little.

Alan lay upon a couch beneath the narrow window; he was resting on his elbow, and his head was supported in his hand. A bandage crossed his forehead, and one arm was in a sling. He glanced up as the cavalcade came in, and his lips set firmly.

“So my Lord of Beauvallet would not yield?” he said faintly. “Ye were all so certain!” He laughed, and withal his weakness there was a ring of pride in his voice. “Beauvallet is made of sterner stuff, and well he knows that life to me, under thy conditions, is disgrace!”

===Chinese===
骑士跌跌撞撞走向门口，廷臣们一个接一个跟在后面。走在最后的是西蒙，伯爵夫人被他挟在身前半步。她挣了一次，用空着的手朝他脸上掴去，西蒙逼着她继续走。随后她便昂然前行，头颅高扬，仪态雍容如女王，裙裾在身后窸窣曳地。

他们穿过大厅，掠过一群惊惶失措的仆役，来到狭窄的楼梯口。楼梯窄不容二人并行，伯爵夫人走在前头，西蒙已松了手。二人上到塔楼中的一间居室。在那里，西蒙重新扣住她的手腕，见她痛得一缩，便松了些力道。

艾伦躺在窄窗下的一张卧榻上，一肘支着，一手托着头。一条绷带缠过前额，一条手臂吊在颈间。这一行人进来时，他抬眼一扫，双唇抿得紧紧的。

“怎么，博瓦莱大人终究没有屈服？”他声音微弱地说。“你们方才可是笃定得很！”他笑了，虚弱之中，嗓音里仍透着一股傲气。“博瓦莱天生一副硬骨头。他也深知，照你这条件苟活，于我是奇耻大辱！”

===Original===
Then Simon clanked in, and Alan gave a great start.

“Simon!” A look of horror came into his wan face. “Ah, no, Simon! Not thou! Death were easier!”

“Didst thou think that I would leave thee to die?” Simon asked him gently. “I hold this castle﻿—alone.”

Alan sank back against his pillows. A laugh shook him.

“Oh, thou indomitable one!” he chuckled. “I doubted thee not until this moment! Geoffrey is safe?”

===Chinese===
这时西蒙甲叶铿然走了进来，艾伦大惊。

“西蒙！”他苍白的脸上浮起惊骇之色。“啊，不，西蒙！偏偏是你！死了倒还干净些！”

“你当我会撇下你任你死去？”西蒙温声问他。“这座城堡——我已只身握在掌中。”

艾伦靠回枕上，笑得身子直颤。

“哦，你这压不垮的人！”他咯咯笑道。“直到此刻之前，我不曾疑你分毫！杰弗里平安么？”

===Original===
“Ah. I came but to see that thou wert alive, and well-tended. I go now, and the Lady Margaret goes with me, as hostage for thy safety.”

“Ah no, by God!” the Chevalier exploded. “Would ye put my cousin to this shame?”

“Oh, brave to war on women!” the Countess snapped. “Do with me as ye will, but take heed lest I strike thee one day when thou art grown careless! Thou shalt pay in full, I swear!”

“Whither go ye, Simon lad?” Alan asked.

“To Malvallet. If I return not, he will sack the town. I shall come again with my men, never fear. Thou art safe, for if harm befall thee, the Lady Margaret dies by my sword.”

===Chinese===
“嗯。我来，只为看你活着、得着照应。这便走了。玛格丽特夫人随我同去，作你平安的抵押。”

“啊，不行，上帝在上！”骑士炸了。“你要把我表姐置于这般羞辱之中么？”

“好啊，对妇道人家宣战，好威风！”伯爵夫人冷冷讥刺。“你要把我怎样都随你，可当心有朝一日你疏于提防，我会给你一下！这笔账你终要如数偿还，我发誓！”

“你去哪里，西蒙老弟？”艾伦问。

“去马尔瓦莱那里。我若不回，他便洗劫全城。我会带人再来，万勿担忧。你平安无事——你若受了伤，玛格丽特夫人便死在我剑下。”

===Original===
The Countess drew herself up. Her bosom rose and fell quickly. Full into Simon’s eyes she looked, her own blazing with anger.

“I will not rest until I have avenged myself,” she said very quietly. “Thou English beast!”

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人挺直了身子，胸脯急促起伏。她直直望进西蒙的眼底，自己的一双眼烧着怒火。

“此仇不报，我誓不罢休。”她说得极轻极静。“你这英格兰畜生！”

## V / 第五章

===Original===

How He Brought the Lady Margaret to the Justice-House

===Chinese===

他如何将玛格丽特夫人带往审判厅

===Original===

Through the streets of Belrémy, past staring townsfolk and saluting men-at-arms, Simon rode, a veiled and cloaked lady on a white palfrey beside him, sitting very upright in her saddle. Behind came Edmund with another lady, veiled also, and speaking never a word. To the justice-house they went, walking sedately, and there dismounted.

Out came Malvallet, armour-clad but bareheaded, his dark eyes eager.

“God be thanked, Simon!” he said fervently. Then he saw the tall woman beside Simon, and stepped back a pace. “What’s to do now, lad?”

===Chinese===

西蒙（Simon）策马穿过贝雷米（Belrémy）的街道，两旁尽是张目围观的市民与举手行礼的甲士；他身侧并辔而行的是一位蒙纱着氅的夫人，骑一匹白色驯马，端端正正立在鞍上。埃德蒙（Edmund）殿后，陪着另一位夫人，同样蒙着面纱，自始至终一言不发。一行人缓辔徐行，来到审判厅，在那里下了马。

马尔瓦莱（Malvallet）迎了出来，一身铠甲却未戴盔，一双黑眼睛热切得很。

“感谢上帝，西蒙！”他恳切地说。随即他瞥见西蒙身旁那个高挑的女人，倒退了一步。“这又是唱的哪一出，小子？”

===Original===

Simon did not answer him, for the guard-men were eyeing him cautiously. He bowed stiffly to his charge.

“Enter, madame.”

Marvelling, and all perplexed, Malvallet stood aside to let the lady pass. She swept by him, into the lofty hall where Simon transacted all his affairs, and where sat Bernard of Talmayne, busily writing. Bernard stood up, astonished at the sudden entrance of two ladies in the company of his lord.

===Chinese===

西蒙没有答话，因为卫兵们正警惕地打量着他。他朝自己护送的贵客僵硬地一躬身。

“请进，夫人。”

马尔瓦莱又惊又惑，闪身给夫人让路。她裙裾生风地从他身旁掠过，径入那座高敞的大厅——西蒙在那里料理一切公务，塔尔曼的伯纳德（Bernard of Talmayne）正伏案疾书。伯纳德站起身来，惊见两位夫人竟随他家大人联袂而至。

===Original===

With a quick, impatient movement the Lady Margaret flung back her veil. Geoffrey caught his breath at the sight of her proud beauty. Her companion also unveiled. She was a little lady with brown curls and big blue eyes. Just now those eyes were exceedingly haughty, but at the back of them Geoffrey thought he discerned a twinkle.

“My captain, madame,” said Simon. “Sir Geoffrey of Malvallet. The Countess Margaret, Geoffrey.”

Geoffrey started, and threw Simon an amazed glance. But in a moment he had hidden his surprise and pulled forward a chair.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特夫人（Lady Margaret）动作干脆而不耐烦，一把掀开头纱。杰弗里（Geoffrey）一见她那高傲的美，不由屏住了呼吸。她的同伴也揭了面纱：是位娇小的夫人，棕色鬈发，一双蓝色的大眼睛。此刻那双眼睛傲气逼人，可杰弗里觉得，傲气的深处隐约闪着一星狡黠。

“我的统领，夫人。”西蒙说。“马尔瓦莱的杰弗里爵士。这位是玛格丽特女伯爵（the Countess Margaret），杰弗里。”

杰弗里一怔，朝西蒙投去诧异的一瞥。但转眼间他便掩去了惊色，拉过一把椅子。

===Original===

“Pray, madame, will you not be seated?” he bowed, all his courtier instincts to the fore.

The Countess hesitated a moment, looking at Simon. Then she sat down, allowing her cloak to fall away from her gleaming shoulders. Her foot tapped the ground imperiously.

“Well, sir? What now?”

===Chinese===

“夫人，请坐？”他躬身相让，浑身的朝臣本能尽数使了出来。

伯爵夫人望着西蒙，迟疑了片刻。随后她坐了下来，任斗篷从光华流转的肩头滑落。她的脚尖威仪凛然地叩点着地面。

“怎么样，先生？现在又如何？”

===Original===

The little lady went to her and stood behind her chair. She smiled upon Geoffrey graciously, as if to thank him for his consideration.

Simon clanked to the table behind which Bernard stood, spellbound.

“Go prepare me two rooms above,” he said. “Let Walter of Santoy set a guard of mine own men upon them, so that not a mouse may creep out unseen. Hasten.”

Bernard stammered something unintelligible and hurried out. Simon turned to his prisoner.

===Chinese===

娇小的夫人走到她跟前，立在椅后。她雍容地向杰弗里嫣然一笑，似在谢他这一番周到。

西蒙甲叶铿然，走向桌子；伯纳德还立在桌后，看得出了神。

“上楼替我备两间房，”他说。“叫桑托伊的沃尔特（Walter of Santoy）派我自己的部下看守，不许一只老鼠溜出去没叫人瞧见。快去。”

伯纳德结结巴巴咕哝了几句谁也听不清的话，匆匆去了。西蒙转向他的俘虏。

===Original===

“Madame, rest assured that I shall look well to your housing, that ye may suffer no discomfort during your sojourn here. Alan is safe, Geoffrey.”

Geoffrey nodded. He clapped his hands vigorously, and when a lackey appeared, ordered wine to be brought. This he offered to the Countess, on one knee.

“I need naught,” she said coldly.

Her companion rustled forward, taking the horn from Geoffrey’s hand.

===Chinese===

“夫人，请放宽心，我必妥善安置您的住处，您在此小住，不致受半分委屈。艾伦（Alan）平安无事，杰弗里。”

杰弗里点点头。他用力拍了拍手，待一名侍仆应声而来，便吩咐取酒。酒来了，他单膝点地，把酒奉到伯爵夫人面前。

“我什么也不要。”她冷冷地说。

她的同伴窸窸窣窣抢上前来，从杰弗里手里接过角杯。

===Original===

“Nay, madame, but taste a little!” she coaxed, and whispered something in the Countess’s ear.

The Lady Margaret smiled faintly and took the horn. Geoffrey made haste to fill another for her lady, and was rewarded by a smile, and a curtsey.

“I thank you, m’sieur.”

“My Lord of Beauvallet,” said the Countess coldly, “for how long do ye seek to detain me?”

===Chinese===

“哎呀夫人，您就略尝一口嘛！”她哄劝着，又凑到伯爵夫人耳边低语了几句。

玛格丽特夫人唇边浮起浅笑，接过了角杯。杰弗里赶忙又斟满一杯，献给她那位夫人，换来盈盈一笑，外加一个屈膝礼。

“多谢您，先生。”

“博瓦莱大人（my Lord of Beauvallet），”伯爵夫人冷冷开口，“你打算把我扣留多久？”

===Original===

“Ye shall be within your castle by noon tomorrow, madame,” Simon answered shortly.

The smouldering eyes challenged him.

“As mistress or prisoner, sir?”

“The decision rests with you, madame. Mistress shall ye be if ye will swear allegiance to King Henry.”

“I bend not so easily, milor’,” she sneered.

===Chinese===

“明日午前，您便回到您的城堡里了，夫人。”西蒙简短地答。

那双郁燃的眼睛向他挑衅。

“是以女主人的身份，还是作阶下囚，先生？”

“这由您定夺，夫人。您若肯向亨利国王（King Henry）宣誓效忠，便是女主人。”

“我才不会这么轻易弯折呢，英国老爷。”她讥诮道。

===Original===

Simon’s lips tightened.

“Maybe ye will break then, madame.”

She laughed at him, but her little teeth were clenched.

“Ye know not Margaret of Belrémy, sir!”

“I think it is you who know not Simon of Beauvallet,” Simon said, with the glimmering of a smile.

===Chinese===

西蒙抿紧了嘴唇。

“那也许就得折断了，夫人。”

她朝他笑了，一口细齿却咬得紧紧的。

“你还不识得贝雷米的玛格丽特呢，先生！”

“我看，是你还不识得博瓦莱的西蒙。”西蒙说着，唇边掠过一丝隐约的笑意。

===Original===

Bernard came back into the room.

“It is done, my lord. Santoy was here.”

The Countess rose, drawing her cloak about her. She addressed Simon softly.

“Let there be an understanding between us, sir.”

“I desire naught better, lady.”

===Chinese===

伯纳德回到厅里。

“办妥了，大人。桑托伊来过。”

伯爵夫人立起身来，裹拢斗篷。她柔声对西蒙说道：

“咱们之间，得先立个明白，先生。”

“求之不得，夫人。”

===Original===

“Then mark well what I say. I give no parole, I swear no allegiance. It is war between us to the death, for I am not vanquished yet, nor will be! Ye would do well to beware my vengeance, Lord of Beauvallet!”

“I thank ye for that warning,” Simon retorted. He held back a curtain at the end of the hall. “Go before me, madame.”

When he returned to the hall it was some time later, and he had shed his armour for a long green tunic which fell below his knees and was slit at the sides to give him greater freedom in walking. Heavy spurred boots were upon his feet, but his head was bare, the light hair still clubbed at neck and brow, brushed and smooth. He was frowning, but when he met Geoffrey’s quizzical glance, the shadows went out of his eyes, and they twinkled responsively.

===Chinese===

“那么听仔细我的话。我不予誓诺，也决不宣誓效忠。你我之间，唯有战至死的战争，因为我尚未战败，也永不会败！你还是当心我的复仇吧，博瓦莱大人！”

“多谢你这句提点，”西蒙回敬道。他掀开大厅尽头的一幅帷帘。“您前头请，夫人。”

过了好一阵，他才回到大厅，铠甲已经卸下，换上一件长长的绿袍，袍长过膝，两侧开衩，走动更添自如。脚上蹬着带踢马刺的重靴，头上却不着一物，浅色的头发依旧在颈后与额角绾成发卷，梳理得平整光洁。他眉头微蹙，可一触到杰弗里打趣的眼神，眼底的阴翳便消散了，眸子里也相应地闪起光来。

===Original===

“Oh, Simon, Simon, thou dog!” Geoffrey teased him. “What have ye done?”

The corners of Simon’s mouth turned down ruefully.

“I have brought a wildcat into our midst,” he answered. “Belrémy is not wholly mine yet, though I hold the town and the castle.”

===Chinese===

“哦，西蒙啊西蒙，你这狗东西！”杰弗里打趣他。“你都干了什么好事？”

西蒙嘴角苦着脸耷拉下来。

“我给我们中间带回了一只野猫，”他答道。“贝雷米还不全然归我，虽然城池和城堡都已在我手中。”

===Original===

Geoffrey seized him by the shoulders, pushing him backwards to a chair.

“Sit, thou squire of dames, and tell me what passed within the castle.”

“Little enough. I entered alone, and was led to my lady’s audience-chamber, where she sat amidst her court, with her cousin beside her.”

===Chinese===

杰弗里抓住他双肩，把他往后推到一把椅子跟前。

“坐下吧，淑女们的扈从，跟我讲讲城堡里出了什么事。”

“没多少可讲的。我只身入堡，被引到我夫人的觐见厅，她端坐在满朝侍臣中间，表弟就坐在近旁。”

===Original===

“Cousin?”

“Ay. Him I expected, for his father, the Sire de Galledemaine, spoke of him. A puny creature with a rose. Faugh! So soon as I had set eyes on him I knew what manner of man I had to treat with. They had thought to frighten me with threats, deeming me a fool to walk thus coolly into their trap.”

“Said I not that it was a trap!”

===Chinese===

“表弟？”

“是啊。这个人我早料到了，他父亲加勒德曼老爷（the Sire de Galledemaine）提过他。一个捧着玫瑰的孱弱货色。呸！我一眼看清了他，便知道自己是在跟什么样的人打交道。他们本想拿威胁来吓我，只当我是个傻子，才会这么从容不迫地踏进他们的圈套。”

“我不是说过那是个圈套么！”

===Original===

“Nay, but I knew the workings of it. They would have taken me prisoner, mayhap slain me. I know not.”

“What!” Geoffrey started up. “But it was truce!”

“So I thought. Yet I suspected treachery, so was I not taken unawares. There was some parley at first. My lady was proud enough, and high in her talk. Then they flung veiled threats at me, and I made an end.”

===Chinese===

“那也不要紧，我摸得清它的门道。他们本要拿我作俘虏，或许还会要我的命。天晓得。”

“什么！”杰弗里跳了起来。“可那明明是休战！”

“我也当是休战。但我疑心有诈，才不曾措手不及。起初还有几句交涉。我那位夫人傲气十足，说话口气高得很。随后他们朝我丢来些遮遮掩掩的威胁，我便收了场。”

===Original===

“Simon, thou art like an oyster! How made ye this end?”

“I drew my sword upon the Lady Margaret, and thus held her men at bay.”

“Ye﻿—ye﻿—Oh, *preux chevalier*!” Geoffrey broke into a long laugh. “They would not think that, the Frenchmen!”

===Chinese===

“西蒙，你这人真像只牡蛎！你这收场是怎么个收法？”

“我拔剑指向玛格丽特夫人，就这样逼得她手下人不敢近前。”

“你——你——哦，好一位*侠义骑士*（preux chevalier）！”杰弗里放声大笑了好一阵。“法国人可料想不到这一出！”

===Original===

“Nay. Not that dainty court. After that it was simple. They led me to Alan who lay in a fair chamber in the tower. He is wounded, but I think not badly. Then came I here, with my lady as hostage.
